[Genesis] [22]:[1] And it came to pass after these things that God tried Abraham and said to him, Abraham! Abraham! And he said, Here I am.
[Genesis] [22]:[2] And he said, Take thy son, thy dear son whom thou hast loved—Isaak; and go to the hilly country and offer him up there for a whole homage offering on one of the hills which I will point out to thee.
[Genesis] [22]:[3] So Abraham arose in the morning and saddled his ass and took with him two servants and his son Isaak: and having split wood for the whole homage offering he set out on the journey and came to the place which God told him
[Genesis] [22]:[4] on the third day. Now when Abraham raised his eyes and saw the place at a distance
[Genesis] [22]:[5] he said to his servants, Stay here with the ass, and I and the lad will go yonder, and when we have worshipped we will return to you.
[Genesis] [22]:[6] Then Abraham took the wood of the whole homage offering and laid it on his son Isaak; and he took in his hand the fire and the knife, and they both proceeded on together.
[Genesis] [22]:[7] And Isaak said to his father Abraham, Father. And he said, What is the matter, son? And Isaak said, Here is the fire and the wood; where is the sheep for a whole homage offering?
[Genesis] [22]:[8] And Abraham said, My son! God will provide for himself a sheep for a whole homage offering. So they both proceeded on together
[Genesis] [22]:[9] and came to the place which God told him. And Abraham built there the altar, and placed thereon the wood; and having bound his son Isaak he laid him on the altar above the wood.
[Genesis] [22]:[10] And when Abraham stretched forth his hand to take hold of the knife to slay his son,
[Genesis] [22]:[11] the angel of the Lord called to him out of heaven and said, Abraham! Abraham! And he said, Here I am.
[Genesis] [22]:[12] And he said, Lay not thy hand on the lad, nor do any thing to him; for now I know that thou fearest God and on my account hast not spared thy darling son.
[Genesis] [22]:[13] Then Abraham raised his eyes and looked, and lo! a ram caught by the horns in a sabek bush. And Abraham went and took the ram and offered him up for an homage offering instead of his son Isaak.
[Genesis] [22]:[14] And Abraham called the name of that place, The Lord hath seen, that at this day they might say, On this mountain, The Lord was seen.
[Genesis] [22]:[15] Then the angel of the Lord called to Abraham a second time out of heaven,
[Genesis] [22]:[16] saying, By myself I have sworn, saith the Lord, Because thou hast done this, and for my sake hast not spared thy beloved son;
[Genesis] [22]:[17] with blessings I will indeed bless thee; and I will multiply thy seed abundantly as the stars of heaven and as the sand on the sea shore: and thy seed shall inherit the cities of their adversaries;
[Genesis] [22]:[18] and by the seed of thee all the nations of the earth shall be blessed, because thou hast hearkened to my voice.
[Genesis] [22]:[19] Then Abraham returned to his servants and they arose and came together to Well of the Oath, for Abraham lived at Well of the Oath.
[Genesis] [22]:[20] And after these things it was told Abraham saying, With regard to Melcha, behold she also hath born sons to thy brother Nachor,
[Genesis] [22]:[21] Ouz the first born, and Baux his brother, and Hamuel, the father of the Syrians,
[Genesis] [22]:[22] and Chazad and Azau, and Phaldes and Jeldaph and Bathuel;
[Genesis] [22]:[23] and Bathuel begot Rebekka. These were the eight sons, whom Melcha bore to Nachor the brother of Abraham.
[Genesis] [22]:[24] And his concubine whose name was Reuma bore also Tabek and Gaam and Tochos and Mocha.
[Genesis] [23]:[1] Now the life of Sarah was a hundred and twenty seven years.
[Genesis] [23]:[2] And Sarah died in the city Arbok, which is in the vale, the same is Chebron in the land of Chanaan. And Abraham went to mourn for Sarah and to make lamentation.
[Genesis] [23]:[3] And Abraham arose from before his dead and spoke to the children of Chet, saying,
[Genesis] [23]:[4] I am a stranger and sojourner among you, give me therefore the possession of a burying place among you, that I may bury my dead from before me.
[Genesis] [23]:[5] And the children of Chet answered and said to Abraham,
[Genesis] [23]:[6] Nay, Sir, rather hearken to us. Thou art a king from God among us. In the choice of our tombs bury thy dead; for there is none of us who will deny thee his tomb to bury there thy dead.
[Genesis] [23]:[7] Then Abraham standing up made obeisance to the people of the land—the children of Chet.
[Genesis] [23]:[8] And Abraham spoke to them saying, If it be your wish that I should bury this my dead out of my sight, hearken to me and speak for me to Ephron the son of Saar.
[Genesis] [23]:[9] And let him give me the double cave which belongeth to him—that which is in a part of his field. Let him give it to me for the full value of it, among you, for a possession of a burying place.
[Genesis] [23]:[10] Now Ephron was sitting among the children of Chet; And Ephron the Chettite answered Abraham and said in the hearing of the children of Chet and of all coming into the city,
[Genesis] [23]:[11] Attend, Sir, and hearken to me. The field and the cave which is in it I give thee. In the presence of my fellow citizens I have given them to thee. Bury thy dead.
[Genesis] [23]:[12] And when Abraham had made obeisance before all the people of the land,
[Genesis] [23]:[13] he said to Ephron in the hearing of all the people of the land, As thou art here before me, hearken to me. Take of me in silver the value of the field and I will bury my dead there.
[Genesis] [23]:[14] Whereupon Ephron answered Abraham, saying,
[Genesis] [23]:[15] Nay, Sir, I have indeed heard that the land is worth four hundred didrachms of silver; but what can that be between me and thee? But bury thou thy dead.
[Genesis] [23]:[16] And Abraham hearkened to Ephron; and Abraham paid down to Ephron the money which he mentioned in the hearing of the children of Chet, four hundred didrachms of silver current among merchants.
[Genesis] [23]:[17] So the field of Ephron including the double cave which is situate before Mambre, the field and the cave in it and all the wood in the field and all within the bounds thereof round about were conveyed
[Genesis] [23]:[18] to Abraham for a possession in the presence of the children of Chet and of all who entered the city.
[Genesis] [23]:[19] After this Abraham buried Sarah his wife in the double cave of the field which is over against Mambre (the same is Chebron in the land of Chanaan.
[Genesis] [23]:[20] And the property of the field and the cave in it was confirmed to Abraham for a possession of a burying place by the children of Chet.
[Genesis] [24]:[1] Now Abraham was old and far advanced in years, and the Lord had blessed him in all things.
[Genesis] [24]:[2] And Abraham said to his servant, the oldest of his family and who was ruler over all that he had, Put thy hand under my thigh,
[Genesis] [24]:[3] and let me cause thee to swear by the Lord the God of heaven and the God of earth, that thou wilt not take a wife for my son Isaak, of the daughters of the Chananites among whom I dwell;
[Genesis] [24]:[4] but that thou wilt go to the land where I was born, and to my tribe and take thence a wife for my son Isaak.
[Genesis] [24]:[5] And the servant said to him, Perhaps the woman will not be willing to come with me to this land; shall I take back thy son to the land from which thou earnest?
[Genesis] [24]:[6] Whereupon Abraham said to him, Beware that thou take not back my son thither.
[Genesis] [24]:[7] The Lord, the God of heaven and the God of earth, who took me from my father's house and from the land of my nativity; who spoke to me, and who sware to me saying, "To thee I will give this land and to thy seed;" he will send his angel before thee, and thou shalt take thence a wife for my son.
[Genesis] [24]:[8] But if the woman will not come with thee into this land, thou shalt be absolved from this my oath, save that thou must not take my son back thither.
[Genesis] [24]:[9] Then the servant put his hand under his master Abraham's thigh, and swore to him respecting this matter.
[Genesis] [24]:[10] And the servant took ten camels of the camels of his master; and of all the goods of his master which were in his charge, and arose and went to Mesopotamia to the city of Nachor.
[Genesis] [24]:[11] And he made his camels lie down without the city, by the well of water, in the evening, when the women come out to draw water;
[Genesis] [24]:[12] and said, Lord the God of my master Abraham, prosper the way before me this day; and shew thy kindness to my master Abraham.
[Genesis] [24]:[13] Behold I stand by this well of water and the daughters of the inhabitants of the city will come out to draw water.
[Genesis] [24]:[14] Now let the virgin to whom I shall say, Let down thy pitcher that I may drink; and who shall say, Drink thou, and I will give water also to thy camels till they have all done drinking; let her be the woman whom thou hast provided for thy servant Isaak. And by this I shall know that thou hast dealt mercifully with my master Abraham.
[Genesis] [24]:[15] And before he had finished this mental speech, lo! Rebekka the daughter of Bathuel, the son of Melcha, the wife of Nachor, Abraham's brother, came out with her pitcher on her shoulder.
[Genesis] [24]:[16] Now the damsel was of a very beautiful countenance and was a virgin. No man had known her. So when she had gone down to the well and filled her pitcher and came up,
[Genesis] [24]:[17] the servant ran to meet her and said, Let me drink a little water out of thy pitcher.
[Genesis] [24]:[18] Upon which she said, Drink, my lord, and hastened to let down the pitcher on her arm and let him drink till he was satisfied.
[Genesis] [24]:[19] Then she said, I will draw water for thy camels also until they shall have all drunk.
[Genesis] [24]:[20] And she hasted and emptied the pitcher into the watering trough and ran to the well to draw more and drew water for all the camels.
[Genesis] [24]:[21] And the man observed her attentively and held his peace, that he might know whether the Lord had prospered his journey or not.
[Genesis] [24]:[22] And when all the camels had done drinking, the man took ear rings of gold of about a drachm weight, and a pair of bracelets for her hands, the weight of which was ten pieces of gold,
[Genesis] [24]:[23] and he asked her saying, Whose daughter art thou? Tell me, is there room at thy father's house for us to lodge?
[Genesis] [24]:[24] And she said, I am the daughter of Bathuel, the son of Melcha, whom she bore to Nachor.
[Genesis] [24]:[25] Moreover she said to him, We have plenty of straw and provender and room to lodge in.
[Genesis] [24]:[26] Then the man being satisfied, worshipped the Lord
[Genesis] [24]:[27] and said, Blessed be the Lord God of my master Abraham, who hath not withdrawn his kindness and his truth from my master. The Lord hath indeed given me a prosperous journey to the house of my master's brother.
[Genesis] [24]:[28] Upon this the damsel ran to her mother's house and told all these things.
[Genesis] [24]:[29] Now Rebekka had a brother whose name was Laban. And Laban ran out to the man at the well.
[Genesis] [24]:[30] When he saw the ear rings and bracelets in his sister's hands and heard all the words of Rebekka his sister, saying, Thus spake the man to me, he went to the man who was standing by his camels at the well
[Genesis] [24]:[31] and said to him, Come in, thou blessed of the Lord, why hast thou tarried without? I have the house ready and a place for the camels.
[Genesis] [24]:[32] So the man went in to the family, and Laban unsaddled the camels, and gave straw and provender to the camels, and water to wash his feet and the feet of the men who were with him,
[Genesis] [24]:[33] and set bread before them to eat. But the man said I cannot eat till I have told my business. And Laban said, speak.
[Genesis] [24]:[34] Thereupon the man said, I am Abraham's servant,
[Genesis] [24]:[35] The Lord indeed hath blessed my master exceedingly, so that he is be 35 come great. He hath given him flocks and herds and silver and gold, men servants and maid servants, camels and asses.
[Genesis] [24]:[36] And Sarah, the wife of my master, hath born one son to my master when he was old, and he hath given him all that belonged to him.
[Genesis] [24]:[37] And my master made me sware saying, Thou shalt not take a wife for my son from among the daughters of the Chananites in whose land I sojourn;
[Genesis] [24]:[38] but thou shalt go to the house of my father and to my tribe and take thence a wife for my son.
[Genesis] [24]:[39] Whereupon I said to my master, Perhaps the woman will not come with me.
[Genesis] [24]:[40] And he said to me, The Lord God, to whom I have been well pleasing in his sight, will send his angel with thee, and make thy journey prosperous, and thou shalt take a wife for my son from my tribe and from my father's house.
[Genesis] [24]:[41] On this condition only thou shalt be absolved from my curse; when thou hast gone to my tribe, if they will not give thee, then shalt thou be clear from my oath.
[Genesis] [24]:[42] Now, when I came to day to the well, I said, Lord the God of my master Abraham, if thou prosperest the journey in which I am now engaged,
[Genesis] [24]:[43] Behold I have stopped at this well of water, and the daughters of the men of the city are coming out to draw water, Let the virgin to whom I shall say, Let me drink a little water out of thy pitcher;
[Genesis] [24]:[44] and who shall say to me, Drink thou, and I will draw water for thy camels; Let her be the wife whom the Lord hath provided for his servant Isaak; and by this I shall know that thon hast dealt mercifully with my master Abraham.
[Genesis] [24]:[45] And before I had finished this my address in thought, straightway Rebekka came with the pitcher on her shoulder and went down to the well and drew water, and I said to her, Let me drink.
[Genesis] [24]:[46] Upon which she speedily let down the pitcher on Her arm, and said, Drink thou, and I will water thy camels. So I drank. And when she had watered the camels,
[Genesis] [24]:[47] I asked her saying, Whose daughter art thou? Tell me. And she said, I am the daughter of Bathuel the son of Nachor, whom Melcha bore to him. Then I put the ear rings on her and the bracelets on her hands,
[Genesis] [24]:[48] and being well pleased, I worshipped the Lord and blessed the Lord the God of my master Abraham, who had prospered me in the right way to take the daughter of my master's brother for his son.
[Genesis] [24]:[49] Now therefore, if you deal kindly and truly with my master, tell me. If not, tell me; that I may turn either to the right or left.
[Genesis] [24]:[50] In reply to which, Laban and Bathuel said, This matter hath proceeded from the Lord. We cannot gainsay thee, be it good or bad.
[Genesis] [24]:[51] Behold Rebekka is before thee. Take her and go and let her be the wife of thy master's son, as the Lord hath spoken.
[Genesis] [24]:[52] And when Abraham's servant heard these words, he worshipped the Lord on the ground.
[Genesis] [24]:[53] Then the servant brought out jewels of silver and gold and raiment and gave them to Rebekka, and made presents to her brother and to her mother.
[Genesis] [24]:[54] And when he and the men who were with him had eaten and drank, they went to rest. And he arose early next morning and said, Dismiss me that I may return to my master.
[Genesis] [24]:[55] But her brother and her mother said, Let the damsel stay with us about ten days and then she shall go.
[Genesis] [24]:[56] And he said to them, Do not detain me, seeing the Lord hath prospered my journey as far as it respecteth me. Send me away that I may go back to my master.
[Genesis] [24]:[57] Whereupon they said, Let us call our child and ask what she saith.
[Genesis] [24]:[58] So they called Rebekka and said to her, Wilt thou go with this man? And she said, I will go.
[Genesis] [24]:[59] Then they sent away their sister Rebekka and what belonged to her, and the servant of Abraham and his attendants.
[Genesis] [24]:[60] And they blessed Rebekka and said to her, Thou art our sister. Mayest thou become thousands of myriads and may thy seed inherit the cities of their enemies.
[Genesis] [24]:[61] Then arose Rebekka and her maids, and mounting the camels they went with the man. And the servant took Rebekka and departed.
[Genesis] [24]:[62] Now Isaak had travelled through the wilderness to the well of Vision, and dwelt in the south country.
[Genesis] [24]:[63] And in the evening Isaak went out into the plain to meditate; and raising his eyes he saw camels coming.
[Genesis] [24]:[64] Rebekka also having raised her eyes, saw Isaak and alighted from her camel.
[Genesis] [24]:[65] She had said to the servant, What man is that coming in the plain to meet us? And the servant said, It is my master. Whereupon she took her veil and covered herself.
[Genesis] [24]:[66] And the servant told Isaak all that he had done.
[Genesis] [24]:[67] And Isaak went to his mother's house and took Rebekka, and she became his wife, and he loved her. And Isaak was comforted for the loss of his mother Sarah.
[Genesis] [25]:[1] Now Abraham had taken another wife, whose name was Chettura
[Genesis] [25]:[2] and she bore to him Zembran and Jesan and Madal and Madiam and Jesbok and Soie.
[Genesis] [25]:[3] And Jesan begat Saba and Dedan. And the children of Dedan were Assurieim and Latusieim and Laomeim.
[Genesis] [25]:[4] And the children of Madiam were Gephar and Apheir and Enoch and Abeida and Eldaga. All these were children of Chettura.
[Genesis] [25]:[5] But Abraham gave to his son Isaak all his possessions,
[Genesis] [25]:[6] and to the children of his concubines Abraham gave gifts and sent them, while he was yet living, towards the east, into the eastern country from his son Isaak.
[Genesis] [25]:[7] Now these were the years of the days of Abraham's life which he lived, one hundred and seventy five years.
[Genesis] [25]:[8] And Abraham declining died in a good old age, an old man and full of days and he was added to his people.
[Genesis] [25]:[9] And his sons Isaak and Ismael buried him in the double cave in the field of Ephron the son of Saar the Chettite, which is over against Mambre—
[Genesis] [25]:[10] in the field and the cave which Abraham bought of the children of Chet, there they buried Abraham with Sarah his wife.
[Genesis] [25]:[11] And after the death of Abraham God blessed his son Isaak; and Isaak dwelt at the well of Vision.
[Genesis] [25]:[12] Now these are the generations of Abraham's son Ismael whom Agar the Egyptian, Sarah's maid, bore to Abraham;
[Genesis] [25]:[13] and these are the names of the children of Ismael according to the names of his families. The first born of Ismael was Nabaioth, then Kedar and Nabdael and Massam
[Genesis] [25]:[14] and Masma and Duma and Masse
[Genesis] [25]:[15] and Choddan and Thaiman and Jetur and Naphes and Kedma.
[Genesis] [25]:[16] These are the children of Ismael and these are their names in their tents and in their habitations; twelve chiefs of their respective nations.
[Genesis] [25]:[17] And these were the years of the life of Ismael, one hundred and thirty seven years, and he declining died and was added to his race.
[Genesis] [25]:[18] Now he inhabited the country from Evilat to Sour which is in front of Egypt, all the way to the Assyrians. In front of all his brethren was his habitation.
[Genesis] [25]:[19] And these are the generations of Isaak the son of Abraham. Abraham begot Isaak.
[Genesis] [25]:[20] And Isaak was forty years old when he took to wife Rebekka the daughter of Bathuel the Syrian of Syro Mesopotamia the sister of Laban the Syrian.
[Genesis] [25]:[21] And Isaak intreated the Lord for Rebekka his wife because she was barren, and the Lord hearkened to him; And Rebekka his wife conceived
[Genesis] [25]:[22] and the children struggled within her and she said; If it is to be so with me, what doth this portend? So she went to inquire of the Lord.
[Genesis] [25]:[23] And the Lord said to her, two nations are in thy womb and two peoples shall be separated from thy belly, and the one people will excel the other and the elder shall serve the younger.
[Genesis] [25]:[24] So when her days to be delivered were fulfilled there were twins in her womb.
[Genesis] [25]:[25] And the first born came forth red all over hairy like a hide and she called his name Esau;
[Genesis] [25]:[26] and after that came forth his brother and his hand had hold of Esau's heel, and she called his name Jacob. Now Isaak was sixty years old when Rebekka bore them.
[Genesis] [25]:[27] And when they grew up to be young men, Esau was a man skilled in hunting and fond of the field; but Jacob was a man of plain manners and lived at home.
[Genesis] [25]:[28] And Isaak loved Esau, because his game was his food; But Rebekka loved Jacob.
[Genesis] [25]:[29] Now when Jacob was boiling pottage Esau came from the field fainty.
[Genesis] [25]:[30] And Esau said to Jacob, Let me taste some of that red pottage, for I am fainty. For this cause his name was called Edom.
[Genesis] [25]:[31] And Jacob said to Esau, give me this day in exchange thy rights of primogeniture.
[Genesis] [25]:[32] And Esau said, Lo! I am going to die, what then are these rights of primogeniture to me?
[Genesis] [25]:[33] And Jacob said to him, Swear now to me. So he swore to him. Thus were Esau's rights of primogeniture transferred to Jacob.
[Genesis] [25]:[34] Then Jacob gave Esau bread and lentil pottage. And when he had eaten and drunk he arose and went away. Thus did Esau undervalue the rights of primogeniture.
[Genesis] [26]:[1] Now there had been a famine in the land besides the former famine which happened in the time of Abraham. And Isaak went to Abimelech the king of the Philistines, to Gerar.
[Genesis] [26]:[2] For the Lord had appeared to him and said, Go not down into Egypt, but dwell in the land which I will tell thee.
[Genesis] [26]:[3] Sojourn in this land and I will be with thee and bless thee: for to thee and to thy seed I will give all this land. And I will establish my oath which I sware to thy father Abraham
[Genesis] [26]:[4] and multiply thy seed like the stars of heaven. And to thy seed I will give all this land; and by thy seed all the nations of the earth shall be blessed;
[Genesis] [26]:[5] because Abraham thy father hearkened to my voice and kept my ordinances and my commandments and my rules of rectitude and my rites.
[Genesis] [26]:[6] And while Isaak dwelt at Gerar
[Genesis] [26]:[7] the men of the place made inquiry respecting his wife Rebekka; and he said, She is my sister, for he was afraid to say, She is my wife, lest peradventure the men of the place should kill him for Sebeka"s sake, for she was of a very beautiful countenance.
[Genesis] [26]:[8] However he continued there a long time; for Abimelech king of the Gerarites happening to look through a window saw Isaak dallying with Rebekka his wife.
[Genesis] [26]:[9] Whereupon Abimelech called Isaak and said to him certainly she is thy wife. Why then didst thou say, She is my sister? And Isaak said to him, Because I thought I might perhaps die for her.
[Genesis] [26]:[10] And Abimelech said to him, Why hast thou dealt thus with us? In a little while some person of my family might have lain with thy wife, and thou wouldst have brought guilt upon us through ignorance.
[Genesis] [26]:[11] Then Abimelech gave a strict charge to all his people saying, Whoever toucheth this man or his wife shall be liable to death.
[Genesis] [26]:[12] And Isaak sowed in that land and got that year an hundred fold of barley. And the Lord blessed him
[Genesis] [26]:[13] and the man was exalted and prospered more and more till he became exceeding great.
[Genesis] [26]:[14] And he had flocks and herds of cattle and many fields under cultivation. And the Philistines envied him;
[Genesis] [26]:[15] and all the wells which his father's servants had digged in the time of his father, the Philistines stopped up and filled them with earth.
[Genesis] [26]:[16] And Abimelech said to Isaak, Depart from us, for thou art become far mightier than we.
[Genesis] [26]:[17] Upon this Isaak departed thence and took up his abode in the valley of Gerar and dwelt there.
[Genesis] [26]:[18] And Isaak digged again the wells of water, which the servants of his father Abraham had digged and which the Philistines had stopped up, after the death of his father Abraham. And he called them by the same names, by which his father had named them.
[Genesis] [26]:[19] Isaak's servants also digged in the valley of Gerar, and found there a well of spring water.
[Genesis] [26]:[20] But the shepherds of the Gerarites contended with Isaak's shepherds, saying the water was theirs. So he called the name of that well Injustice: because they did him injustice.
[Genesis] [26]:[21] Then he removed thence and digged another well; and about this also they contended; so he called its name Enmity.
[Genesis] [26]:[22] And he removed thence and digged another well; and about this they did not contend; So he called the name of it, Room-enough; saying, For now the Lord hath made room for us, though he hath increased us in the land.
[Genesis] [26]:[23] And he went up thence to Well of the Oath;
[Genesis] [26]:[24] and the Lord appeared to him that night and said, I am the God of thy father Abraham; Fear not; for I am with thee; and I will bless thee and multiply thy seed for the sake of thy father Abraham.
[Genesis] [26]:[25] So he built there an altar and invoked the name of the Lord and fixed his tent there. And while the servants of Isaak were digging a well there in the valley of Gerar,
[Genesis] [26]:[26] Abimelech came to him from Gerar, accompanied with Ochozath his chamberlain and Phichol the general of his army.
[Genesis] [26]:[27] And Isaak said to them, Why are you come to me, seeing you have hated me and sent me away from you?
[Genesis] [26]:[28] And they said, We saw plainly that the Lord is with thee; therefore we said, Let there be an oath between us and thee; and we will make a covenant with thee
[Genesis] [26]:[29] that thou wilt not do us an injury, as we have not hated thee but used thee well and sent thee away in peace, and thou art now blessed of the Lord.
[Genesis] [26]:[30] Then he made an entertainment for them and they ate and drank.
[Genesis] [26]:[31] And early next morning they arose and swore one to the other. And Isaak dismissed them and they departed from him in peace.
[Genesis] [26]:[32] And it happened that on that very day the servants of Isaak came and told him of the well which they had been digging and said, We have found water.
[Genesis] [26]:[33] So he called it, Oath. For this cause they call the name of the city, Well of Oath, even at this day.
[Genesis] [26]:[34] Now when Esau was forty years old he took to wife Judith, the daughter of Beoch the Chettite, and Basemath the daughter of Elon the Chettite,
[Genesis] [26]:[35] and they were at strife with Isaak and Rebekka.
[Genesis] [27]:[1] And after Isaak grew old, when his eyes were so dimmed that he could not see he called Esau his eldest son and said to him. My Son. And he said here I am.
[Genesis] [27]:[2] And he said, Behold I am grown old and do not know the day of my dissolution;
[Genesis] [27]:[3] now therefore take thy arms—the quiver and the bow; and go out into the field and hunt game for me
[Genesis] [27]:[4] and make me savory meat such as I love and bring it to me, that I may eat; that my soul may bless thee before I die.
[Genesis] [27]:[5] Now Rebekka heard Isaak speaking to his son Esau. So when Esau was gone to the field to hunt game for his father,
[Genesis] [27]:[6] Rebekka said to Jacob her younger son. Lo! I heard thy father speaking to thy brother Esau saying,
[Genesis] [27]:[7] Bring me game and make me savory meat that I may eat and bless thee in the presence of the Lord before I die.
[Genesis] [27]:[8] Now therefore, my son, hearken to me as I command thee,
[Genesis] [27]:[9] and go to the flock and fetch me thence two kids tender and good, and I will make them savory meat for thy father such as he loveth
[Genesis] [27]:[10] and thou shalt carry it to thy father that he may eat; that thy father may bless thee before he die.
[Genesis] [27]:[11] And Jacob said to his mother Rebekka, My brother Esau is a hairy man and I am a smooth man.
[Genesis] [27]:[12] Perhaps my father may feel me, and I shall be before him as a deceiver, so I shall bring upon myself a curse and not a blessing.
[Genesis] [27]:[13] And his mother said to him, Upon me, my son, be thy curse. Only hearken to my voice; and go and bring me them.
[Genesis] [27]:[14] So he went and took and brought them to his mother; and his mother made savory meat such as his father loved.
[Genesis] [27]:[15] And Rebekka took the robe of Esau her eldest son, the robe of dignity which was with her in the house and put it on her younger son Jacob.
[Genesis] [27]:[16] And she put the skins of the kids on his arms and the naked parts of his neck;
[Genesis] [27]:[17] and gave the savory meat and cakes which she had baked, into the hands of her son Jacob;
[Genesis] [27]:[18] and he took them to his father and said, Father! And he said, Here I am. Who art thou, my son?
[Genesis] [27]:[19] And Jacob said to his father. I am Esau, thy first born. I have done as thou badest me. Arise, sit up, and eat of my game that thy soul may bless me.
[Genesis] [27]:[20] And Isaak said to his son, What is this, my son, which thou hast found so quickly? And he said, It is what the Lord thy God presented to my view.
[Genesis] [27]:[21] And Isaak said to Jacob, Come near my son and let me feel thee, whether thou be my son Esau or not.
[Genesis] [27]:[22] And Jacob went near to his father Isaak. And when he had felt him he said, The voice indeed is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau.
[Genesis] [27]:[23] So he did not discover him, because his hands were hairy like the hands of his brother Esau, when he blessed him.
[Genesis] [27]:[24] Then he said. Art thou my son Esau? And he said; I am.
[Genesis] [27]:[25] And he said, Bring it near to me and I will eat of thy game, my son, that my soul may bless thee. So he brought it near to him and he ate. And he brought wine and he drank.
[Genesis] [27]:[26] And Isaak his father said to him, Come near me my son and kiss me.
[Genesis] [27]:[27] And when he came near and kissed him; he smelled the smell of his raiment and blessed him and said, Behold the fragrance of my son is like the fragrance of a full field which the Lord hath blessed.
[Genesis] [27]:[28] Therefore God give thee from the dew of heaven and from the fatness of the land plenty of corn and wine.
[Genesis] [27]:[29] May nations serve thee; and princes bow down to thee. Be thou lord of thy brother. And let the children of thy father pay thee homage. Cursed be he who curseth thee; and let him who blesseth thee be blessed.
[Genesis] [27]:[30] Now after Isaak had finished blessing his son Jacob, when Jacob had but just withdrawn from the presence of his father Isaak, Esau his brother came from the chace.
[Genesis] [27]:[31] He also had made savory meat and he brought it to his father and said to him, Arise my father and eat thy son's venison, that thy soul may bless me.
[Genesis] [27]:[32] And Isaak his father said to him, Who art thou? And he said I am thy son, thy first born Esau.
[Genesis] [27]:[33] Then was Isaak absorpt in great amazement and said, Who then is he who hath hunted game and brought it to me, and I have eaten of all before thou earnest and have blessed him, and he will be blessed.
[Genesis] [27]:[34] And when Esau heard the words of his father Isaak he cried with a loud and very bitter cry and said, Bless, I beseech thee, me also, Father.
[Genesis] [27]:[35] And he said to him, Thy brother hath come deceitfully and taken thy blessing.
[Genesis] [27]:[36] And Esau said, His name is rightly called Jacob; for lo! he hath now supplanted me a second time. He took from me my rights of primogeniture; and now he hath taken my blessing. Then Esau said to his father, Hast thou not, father, reserved a blessing for me?
[Genesis] [27]:[37] And Isaak in reply said to Esau, Seeing I have made him thy lord and made all his brethren his servants and have also supported him with corn and wine, what can I do for thee, my son?
[Genesis] [27]:[38] And Esau said to his father, Hast thou but one blessing, father? Bless, I beseech thee, me also, father. And Isaak being deeply affected, Esau raised his voice and wept.
[Genesis] [27]:[39] And Isaak his father answered and said to him, Behold thy dwelling shall border on the fatness of the earth and on the dew of heaven from above.
[Genesis] [27]:[40] By thy sword thou shalt live and thou shalt serve thy brother. But the time will come when thou mayst shake off and loose his yoke from thy neck.
[Genesis] [27]:[41] So Esau harboured a rooted hatred against Jacob on account of the blessing with which his father had blessed him. And in this agitation of his mind Esau said, O! that, the days of mourning for my father were come that I may kill my brother Jacob.
[Genesis] [27]:[42] And when the words of Esau her eldest son were told to Rebekka she sent for her younger son Jacob and said to him, Behold thy brother Esau threateneth to kill thee.
[Genesis] [27]:[43] Now therefore, my son, hearken to my voice and arise and flee to Mesopotamia to my brother Laban at Charran,
[Genesis] [27]:[44] and dwell with him for some time till the fury
[Genesis] [27]:[45] and the anger of thy brother turn away from thee, and until he forget what thou hast done to him, then I will send for thee from that place. Perhaps I may be bereaved of you both in one day.
[Genesis] [27]:[46] Then Rebekka said to Isaak, I am weary of my life on account of the daughters of the children of Chet. If Jacob should take a wife of the daughters of this land what would life be to me?
[Genesis] [28]:[1] Upon this Isaak called Jacob to him and blessed him and charged him saying, Thou shalt not take a wife of the daughters of the Chananites.
[Genesis] [28]:[2] Arise and go to Mesopotamia and to the house of Bathuel thy mother's father, and take thence a wife for thyself of the daughters of Laban thy mother's brother.
[Genesis] [28]:[3] And may my God bless thee and make thee fruitful and multiply thee, that thou mayst become a multitude of nations.
[Genesis] [28]:[4] And may he give thee the blessing of my father Abraham, to thee and thy seed after thee, to inherit this land of thy sojourning, which God gave to Abraham.
[Genesis] [28]:[5] So Isaak sent away Jacob and he went to Mesopotamia to Laban the son of Bathuel the Syrian the brother of Rebekka who was the mother of Jacob and Esau.
[Genesis] [28]:[6] When Esau saw that Isaak had blessed Jacob and sent him to Syro Mesopotamia to take there a wife for himself, and that when he blessed him he charged him saying, Thou shalt not take a wife of the daughters of the Chananites;
[Genesis] [28]:[7] and that Jacob hearkened to his father and mother and was gone to Syrian Mesopotamia,
[Genesis] [28]:[8] Esau seeing that the daughters of the Chananites were displeasing in the sight of his father Isaak,
[Genesis] [28]:[9] went to Ismael and in addition to his other wives took to wife Maeleth the daughter of Ismael, Abraham's son, the sister of Nabaioth.
[Genesis] [28]:[10] Now when Jacob set out from Well of the Oath, and was proceeding on his journey to Charran
[Genesis] [28]:[11] he came to a place and went to rest there, for the sun was setting. Having taken one of the stones of the place and put it at his head he composed himself to rest in that place
[Genesis] [28]:[12] and dreamed; and lo! a ladder set on the earth, the top of which reached up to heaven and the angels of God were ascending and descending on it.
[Genesis] [28]:[13] And the Lord leaned over it and said, I am the God of Abraham thy father and the God of Isaak. Fear not. To thee and to thy seed I will give the land in which thou art sleeping.
[Genesis] [28]:[14] And thy seed shall be as the dust of the earth and shall spread abroad to the west and the south, and the north and the east. And by thee all the tribes of the earth shall be blessed, namely by the seed of thee.
[Genesis] [28]:[15] And lo! I am with thee watching; over thee in all the way thou goest and I will bring thee back to this land: for I will not leave thee until I have done all that I have spoken to thee.
[Genesis] [28]:[16] And when Jacob awoke from his sleep he said, Surely the Lord is in this place and I knew it not.
[Genesis] [28]:[17] And he was terrified and said, How awful is this place; This is no other than the house of God. And this is the gate of heaven.
[Genesis] [28]:[18] So when Jacob arose in the morning he took the stone which he had there put at his head and set it up as a pillar and poured oil on the top of it;
[Genesis] [28]:[19] and called the name of that place House of God. But the first name of this city was Oulamluz.
[Genesis] [28]:[20] And Jacob made a vow saying, If the Lord God will be with me and watch over me in the way in which I am going and give me bread to eat and raiment to clothe me
[Genesis] [28]:[21] and bring me back safely to my father's house, The Lord shall be my God.
[Genesis] [28]:[22] And this stone which I have set up as a pillar shall be for a house of God. And of all that thou mayst give me I will set apart a tenth for thee.
[Genesis] [29]:[1] Then Jacob proceeded on, and went to the land of the east to Laban the son of Bathuel, the Syrian, the brother of Rebekka who was Jacob and Esau's mother.
[Genesis] [29]:[2] And looking, he saw a well in the plain and there were three flocks of sheep lying by it; for out of that well they watered the flocks. Now there was a great stone on the mouth of the well,
[Genesis] [29]:[3] and when all the flocks were collected there, the shepherds rolled away the stone from the mouth of the well, and watered the sheep; and then put the stone again in its place on the mouth of the well.
[Genesis] [29]:[4] And Jacob said to them, Brethren, whence are you? And they said, We are of Charran.
[Genesis] [29]:[5] And he said to them, Do you know Laban the son of Nachor? And they said, We know him.
[Genesis] [29]:[6] And he said to them, Is he well? And they said, He is well. And lo yonder is Rachel his daughter! She was coming with the sheep.
[Genesis] [29]:[7] Then Jacob said to them, There is yet much day. It is not yet time for the cattle to be folded. Water the flocks and lead them off to feed.
[Genesis] [29]:[8] But they said, We cannot, till all the shepherds are come together. Then they will roll the stone from the mouth of the well; and we will water the flocks.
[Genesis] [29]:[9] While he was yet speaking with them, lo! Rachel the daughter of Laban came with her father's sheep; for she tended her father's flock.
[Genesis] [29]:[10] And when Jacob saw Rachel the daughter of Laban his mother's brother, and the sheep of Laban his mother's brother, Jacob went and rolled the stone from the mouth of the well, and watered the flock of Laban his mother's brother.
[Genesis] [29]:[11] And Jacob kissed Rachel, and raising his voice he wept
[Genesis] [29]:[12] and told Rachel that he was her father's brother; and that he was the son of Rebekka. Whereupon she ran and told her father the news.
[Genesis] [29]:[13] And when Laban heard the name of Jacob his sister's son, he ran to meet him, and having embraced him he kissed him, and brought him to his house. And Jacob related to him all these circumstances.
[Genesis] [29]:[14] And Laban said to him, Thou art of my bones and of my flesh. And when Jacob had been with him a month,
[Genesis] [29]:[15] Laban said to Jacob, Because thou art indeed my brother, thou shalt not serve me for nothing. Tell me what must be thy wages.
[Genesis] [29]:[16] Now Laban had two daughters. The name of the eldest was Leia; and the name of the youngest, Rachel.
[Genesis] [29]:[17] Leia's eyes were weak, but Rachel was of a beautiful countenance, and a very comely person.
[Genesis] [29]:[18] And Jacob loved Rachel, therefore he said, I will serve thee seven years for Rachel thy youngest daughter.
[Genesis] [29]:[19] And Laban said to him, It is better for me to give her to thee than to give her to another man. Abide with me.
[Genesis] [29]:[20] So Jacob served seven years for Rachel, and they appeared to him as a few days, because he loved her.
[Genesis] [29]:[21] Then Jacob said to Laban, Give me my wife, for the days are completed, that I may go in unto her.
[Genesis] [29]:[22] Whereupon Laban collected all the men of the place, and made a wedding feast.
[Genesis] [29]:[23] And in the evening he took his daughter Leia and brought her to Jacob; and Jacob went in unto her.
[Genesis] [29]:[24] And Laban gave his maid Zelpha to his daughter Leia, to be her handmaid.
[Genesis] [29]:[25] And in the morning, when Jacob saw it was Leia, he said to Laban, What is this thou hast done to me? Did I not serve thee for Rachel? Why then hast thou deceived me?
[Genesis] [29]:[26] And Laban answered, It is not customary with us in this place to give the younger before the elder.
[Genesis] [29]:[27] Finish therefore this one's week, and I will give thee the other for the service which thou mayst perform for me yet other seven years.
[Genesis] [29]:[28] And Jacob did so. And when he had finished this one's week, Laban gave him to wife his daughter Rachel;
[Genesis] [29]:[29] and Laban gave to his daughter Rachel, his maid Balla to be her handmaid.
[Genesis] [29]:[30] And Jacob went in unto Rachel, and loved Rachel more than Leia, and served him other seven years.
[Genesis] [29]:[31] Now when the Lord God saw that Leia was slighted, he opened her womb, but Rachel was barren.
[Genesis] [29]:[32] And Leia conceived and bore Jacob a son and called his name Reuben; saying, Because the Lord hath seen my affliction; therefore he hath given me a son. Now therefore my husband will love me.
[Genesis] [29]:[33] And she conceived again, and bore to Jacob another son, and said, Because the Lord hath heard that I am slighted, he hath given me this son also; so she called his name Symeon.
[Genesis] [29]:[34] And she conceived again and bore a son, and said, Now will my husband abide with me for I have born him three sons; therefore she called his name Levi.
[Genesis] [29]:[35] And she conceived again and bore a son and said, Now for this also I will praise the Lord, therefore she called his name Judas. Then she ceased from bearing.
[Genesis] [30]:[1] Now when Rachel saw that she bore no children to Jacob, she envied her sister and said to Jacob, Give me children, else I shall die.
[Genesis] [30]:[2] And Jacob was angry with Rachel and said to her, Am I in God's stead, who hath deprived thee of the fruit of the womb?
[Genesis] [30]:[3] Then Rachel said to Jacobs There is my maid Balla; go in unto her, and she shall bring forth on my knees, so I also shall be supplied with children by her means.
[Genesis] [30]:[4] So she gave him her maid Balla for a wife: and Jacob went in unto her,
[Genesis] [30]:[5] And Balla Rachel"s maid conceived and bore Jacob a son.
[Genesis] [30]:[6] And Rachel said, God hath judged for me and hath heard my voice and given me a son: for this cause she called his name Dan.
[Genesis] [30]:[7] And Balla, Rachel"s maid conceived again and bore to Jacob another son.
[Genesis] [30]:[8] Whereupon Rachel said, God helped me when I wrestled with my sister, and I have prevailed. So she called his name Nephthaleim.
[Genesis] [30]:[9] Now when Leia saw that she had ceased from bearing, she also took her maid Zelpha and gave her to Jacob for a wife, and he went in unto her.
[Genesis] [30]:[10] And Zelpha, Leia's maid conceived and bore Jacob a son.
[Genesis] [30]:[11] Whereupon Leia said, Good luck. So she called his name Gad.
[Genesis] [30]:[12] And Zelpha, Leia's maid conceived again and bore Jacob another son.
[Genesis] [30]:[13] Upon which Leia said, Happy am I; for the women will call me happy. So she called his name Aser.
[Genesis] [30]:[14] And at the time of the wheat harvest, Reuben went out and found in the field mandragore apples and brought them to his mother Leia. And Rachel said to her sister Leia, Give me some of thy son's mandragores.
[Genesis] [30]:[15] And Leia said, Is it not enough for thee that thou hast taken my husband? wouldst thou take also my son's mandragores? And Rachel said, Shall I not have them on this condition? Let him be with thee to night for thy son's mandragores.
[Genesis] [30]:[16] So when Jacob was coming from the field in the evening, Leia went out to meet him and said, Thou must come in to me today; for I have hired thee for my son's mandragores. And he lay with her that night,
[Genesis] [30]:[17] and God hearkened to Leia and she having conceived bore to Jacob a fifth son.
[Genesis] [30]:[18] And Leia said, God hath given me my reward for having given my maid to my husband. So she called his name Issachar, that is Reward.
[Genesis] [30]:[19] And Leia conceived again and bore a sixth son to Jacob.
[Genesis] [30]:[20] Whereupon Leia said, God hath now endowed me with a good dowry. My husband will make choice of me, for I have born him six sons. So she called his name Zabulon.
[Genesis] [30]:[21] And after this she bore a daughter and called her name Deina.
[Genesis] [30]:[22] Now God had remembered Rachel and God hearkened to her and opened her womb.
[Genesis] [30]:[23] And she having conceived bore Jacob a son. Whereupon she said, God hath taken away my reproach.
[Genesis] [30]:[24] So she called his name Joseph, saying, May God add to me another son.
[Genesis] [30]:[25] And when Rachel had born Joseph, Jacob said to Laban, Send me away, that I may go to my place and to my own country.
[Genesis] [30]:[26] Give me up my wives and my children for whom I have served thee, that I may go away: for thou knowest the service which I have done thee.
[Genesis] [30]:[27] Upon which Laban said to him, If I have found favour in thy sight, I would avert this evil: for God hath blessed me by thy coming.
[Genesis] [30]:[28] State to me thy wages and I will give it.
[Genesis] [30]:[29] And Jacob said, Thou knowest what service I have done thee, and how many cattle belonging to thee are now with me.
[Genesis] [30]:[30] Thy stock was small before I came; but it is increased to a multitude, and the Lord hath blessed thee by my attendance. Now then, when shall I provide a house for myself.
[Genesis] [30]:[31] And Laban said, What shall I give thee? To which Jacob replied, Thou shalt not give me any thing. If thou wilt do this for me, I will again feed thy flock and keep watch.
[Genesis] [30]:[32] Let all thy flocks this day pass in review and separate thou from them every grizly sheep among the lambs, and every one speckled and spotted among the goats. Such shall be my hire
[Genesis] [30]:[33] and my righteousness shall answer for me in time to come; for my wages are apparent to thee. Whatever is not speckled and spotted among the goats, and grizly among the sheep, if found with me, let it be deemed stolen.
[Genesis] [30]:[34] And Laban said to him, Let it be according to thy proposal.
[Genesis] [30]:[35] And that very day he separated the goats which were speckled and spotted, both male and female, and every one among the lambs which was grizly and every one in which there was a mixture of white, and delivered them into the hands of his sons,
[Genesis] [30]:[36] and set three days journey between them and Jacob. And Jacob fed the rest of Laban's flocks.
[Genesis] [30]:[37] And Jacob took him rods of the styrax and almond and plane-tree and peeled off the white bark, and twisting it round the green, the white which he peeled off appeared speckled.
[Genesis] [30]:[38] And he placed the rods which he had peeled, in the watering troughs, in order that, when the flocks came to drink, they might engender at the rods, while they were drinking, with the rods in view.
[Genesis] [30]:[39] So the flocks engendered at the rods and brought forth cattle streaked and spotted and ash coloured.
[Genesis] [30]:[40] And when Jacob had separated the lambs and placed before the ewes a spotted ram and all that had spots among the lambs, then he kept the flocks which were for himself apart by themselves and did not mix them with the flocks of Laban.
[Genesis] [30]:[41] And at the season when the sheep were engendering and conceiving, Jacob put the rods in the troughs before some, that they might engender before the rods;
[Genesis] [30]:[42] but before others that had lambed he did not place them. Thus the indifferent became Laban's but the choicest were Jacob's.
[Genesis] [30]:[43] And the man became very rich and had much cattle and herds and men servants and maid servants and camels and asses.
[Genesis] [31]:[1] And Jacob heard the words of Laban's sons saying, Jacob hath taken all that belonged to our father, and with our father's substance he hath acquired all this glory.
[Genesis] [31]:[2] And Jacob beheld the countenance of Laban, and lo! it was not towards him as heretofore.
[Genesis] [31]:[3] Then the Lord said to Jacob, Return to thy father's land and to thy kindred and I will be with thee.
[Genesis] [31]:[4] Thereupon Jacob sent for Leia and Rachel to the field where the flocks were
[Genesis] [31]:[5] and said to them, I see that the countenance of your father is not towards me as heretofore. Though the God of my father was with me;
[Genesis] [31]:[6] and you also know that with all my ability I have served your father;
[Genesis] [31]:[7] yet your father hath dealt deceitfully with me, and at the ten lambings hath changed my wages. But God suffered him not to hurt me.
[Genesis] [31]:[8] When he said the speckled shall be thy wages, then all the cattle brought forth speckled. And when he said, The white shall be thy wages. Then all the cattle brought forth white.
[Genesis] [31]:[9] So the Lord hath taken all the cattle of your father and given them to me.
[Genesis] [31]:[10] Indeed when the flocks engendered I beheld with my eyes in a dream, and lo! the he-goats and the rams, which leaped upon the sheep and the goats were ring-streaked, spotted and ash coloured.
[Genesis] [31]:[11] Moreover an angel of God said to me in a dream, Jacob! And I said, What is the matter.
[Genesis] [31]:[12] And he said, Lift up thy eyes and behold the he-goats and the rams which are leaping on the sheep and the goats! They are ring-streaked, speckled and ash coloured; for I have seen what Laban is doing to thee.
[Genesis] [31]:[13] I am the God who appeared to thee at the place of God, where thou didst anoint for me a pillar and where thou didst make a vow. Now therefore arise and depart from this land and go to the land of thy nativity, and I will be with thee.
[Genesis] [31]:[14] In reply to which Rachel and Leia said to him, Have we any more a portion or an inheritance in our father's house?
[Genesis] [31]:[15] Are we not accounted by him as strangers? For he hath sold us and spent the money he got for us.
[Genesis] [31]:[16] Now since all the riches and glory which God hath taken from our father shall be ours and our children's, do then what God hath commanded thee.
[Genesis] [31]:[17] So Jacob arose and put his wives and his children on camels,
[Genesis] [31]:[18] and carried off all his substance, and all the goods which he had gotten in Mesopotamia, and all that belonged to him, to go to his father Isaak in the land of Chanaan.
[Genesis] [31]:[19] Now Laban being gone to sheer his sheep, Rachel stole her father's idols.
[Genesis] [31]:[20] And Jacob concealed his design from Laban the Syrian by not telling him that he was going away.
[Genesis] [31]:[21] And he departed with all that belonged to him and crossed the river and bent his course to mount Galaad.
[Genesis] [31]:[22] But on the third day news was brought to Laban the Syrian, that Jacob was fled.
[Genesis] [31]:[23] Whereupon taking with him his brethren he pursued him seven days journey and overtook him at mount Galaad.
[Genesis] [31]:[24] But God came to Laban the Syrian in a dream by night and said to him, Take care that thou speak not evil to Jacob.
[Genesis] [31]:[25] Now when Laban overtook Jacob, Jacob had pitched his tent on the mount. So Laban halted his brethren at mount Galaad.
[Genesis] [31]:[26] And Laban said to Jacob, What hast thou done? Why didst thou flee away secretly, and steal off without my knowledge, and carry away my daughters as if captivated by the sword?
[Genesis] [31]:[27] Hadst thou told me I might have sent thee away with joy and musick, with timbrels and the kithara.
[Genesis] [31]:[28] But I have not been thought worthy to kiss my children and my daughters. Now therefore thou hast acted foolishly;
[Genesis] [31]:[29] and it is now in my power to punish thee. But the God of thy father spoke to me last night saying, Take care that thou speak not evil to Jacob.
[Genesis] [31]:[30] Now grant that thou hast gone because thou hadst a longing desire to return to thy father's house, yet why hast thou stolen my Gods?
[Genesis] [31]:[31] In reply to this Jacob said to Laban, I was afraid; for I thought that thou perhaps wouldst take from me thy daughters and all that are mine:
[Genesis] [31]:[32] but said Jacob, with whomsoever thou findest thy Gods, let him not live. In the presence of our brethren, examine what there is of thine with me and take it. He knew of nothing with him. Now Jacob did not know that his wife Rachel had stolen them.
[Genesis] [31]:[33] So when Laban had gone in and searched Leia's tent and found them not; and from Leia's tent had gone and searched the tent of Jacob and in the tent of the two handmaids but did not find them. Then he went in also to Rachel's tent.
[Genesis] [31]:[34] Now Rachel had taken the idols and put them in the camel's furniture and sat upon them,
[Genesis] [31]:[35] and she said to her father, Be not offended my lord, I cannot rise before thee, for the custom of women is upon me. So when Laban had searched the whole tent but did not find the idols,
[Genesis] [31]:[36] Jacob was inflamed with resentment and remonstrated against Laban. And Jacob addressing Laban said, What is my crime, and what my offence that thou hast pursued me?
[Genesis] [31]:[37] And that thou hast searched all the goods of my house? What hast thou found of all the goods of thy house? Set it here before thy brethren and my brethren and let them judge between us both
[Genesis] [31]:[38] These twenty years that I have been with thee, thy sheep and thy goats have not cast their young; nor have I devoured the rams of thy flock.
[Genesis] [31]:[39] What was torn by wild beasts I brought not to thee. I made up at my own expence the loss of what was stolen by day or by night.
[Genesis] [31]:[40] I have borne the sultry heat of the day and the frost of the night, and sleep departed from my eyes.
[Genesis] [31]:[41] I have been in thy family these twenty years. I served thee fourteen years for thy two daughters; and six years for thy flocks; though thou didst change my wages at the ten lambings.
[Genesis] [31]:[42] Had not the God of my father Abraham and the fear of Isaak been with me, thou wouldst now have sent me away empty. God hath seen my affliction and the labour of my hands, therefore he rebuked thee last night.
[Genesis] [31]:[43] In reply to this Laban said to Jacob, These daughters are my daughters, and the children are my children, and the cattle are my cattle. Even all that thou seest are mine and my daughters. What now can I do to them and their children whom they have born?
[Genesis] [31]:[44] Now therefore come and let us make a covenant, both I and thou. And let it be for a memorial between me and thee. And he said to him, Behold there is none with us; God a witness between me and thee hath seen.
[Genesis] [31]:[45] Then Jacob took a stone and set it up for a pillar.
[Genesis] [31]:[46] And Jacob said to his brethren, Collect stones. And when they had collected stones and made a heap, they eat thereon. Then Laban said to him, "This heap is this day a witness between me and thee;"
[Genesis] [31]:[47] so Laban called it, The heap of witness; but Jacob called it Heap witness.
[Genesis] [31]:[48] And Laban said to Jacob, Behold this heap and this pillar which I have set between me and thee! This heap witnesseth and this pillar witnesseth
[Genesis] [31]:[49] (for this cause its name was called Heap witnesseth and the sight, which said he, God would see between me and thee, because we shall be separated one from the other)
[Genesis] [31]:[50] if thou afflict my daughters, if thou take other wives besides my daughters, look! though there be none with us to see, God is a witness between me and thee.
[Genesis] [31]:[51] Moreover Laban said to Jacob, Behold; this heap is a witness and this pillar also,
[Genesis] [31]:[52] that if I pass not to thee neither shalt thou pass to me beyond this heap and this pillar for harm.
[Genesis] [31]:[53] The God of Abraham and the God of Nachor judge between us. And Jacob sword by the Fear of his father Isaak.
[Genesis] [31]:[54] Then he offered a sacrifice upon the mount and invited his brethren and they ate and drank and slept on the mount.
[Genesis] [31]:[55] And in the morning Laban arose and kissed his daughters and blessed them. And Laban departed and went to his place
[Genesis] [32]:[1] and Jacob proceeded on his journey. And raising his eyes he saw a camp of God pitched and messengers of God were coming to meet him.
[Genesis] [32]:[2] And when Jacob saw them he said, That is a camp of God, So he called the name of that place Camps.
[Genesis] [32]:[3] Now Jacob had sent messengers before him to his brother Esau, to the land of Seir, to the country of Edom,
[Genesis] [32]:[4] and had given them a charge, saying, Thus shall you say to my lord Esau, Thus saith thy servant Jacob, I have sojourned with Laban and staid till now.
[Genesis] [32]:[5] And I have got oxen and asses and sheep and men servants and maid servants. And I have sent to give notice to my lord Esau, that thy servant may find favour in thy sight.
[Genesis] [32]:[6] So the messengers returned to Jacob and said, We have been to thy brother Esau; and lo! he is coming to meet thee; and four hundred men with him.
[Genesis] [32]:[7] Thereupon Jacob was greatly terrified and distressed. And he divided the people who were with him and the cattle and the camels and the flocks into two camps.
[Genesis] [32]:[8] And Jacob said, If Esau come to the first camp and smite it, there will be another camp to escape.
[Genesis] [32]:[9] Then Jacob said, God of my father Abraham, and the God of my father Isaak, Thou, Lord, who saidst to me, Return to the land of thy nativity, and I will deal well with thee;
[Genesis] [32]:[10] let me be satisfied with all the kindness and with all the truth which thou hast shewn to thy servant. For with this staff of mine I crossed the Jordan, but now I am become two camps.
[Genesis] [32]:[11] Deliver me from the hand of my brother—from the hand of Esau; for I am afraid of him. Peradventure he may come and smite me and the mother with the children.
[Genesis] [32]:[12] But thou hast said, I will deal well with thee and make thy seed like the sand of the sea, which, for multitude cannot be numbered.
[Genesis] [32]:[13] So he halted there that night and took presents of what he had brought and sent to his brother Esau
[Genesis] [32]:[14] two hundred she-goats and twenty he-goats; two hundred ewes and twenty rams;
[Genesis] [32]:[15] thirty milch camels with their thirty colts; forty cows and ten bulls; twenty asses and ten foles.
[Genesis] [32]:[16] These he delivered to his servants, each drove by itself, and he said to his servants, Go on before me and make a small distance between drove and drove.
[Genesis] [32]:[17] And he gave orders to the first saying, If my brother Esau meet thee and ask thee saying, To whom dost thou belong, and whither art thou going? And whose are these before thee?
[Genesis] [32]:[18] thou shalt say, They belong to thy servant Jacob—presents which he hath sent to my lord Esau. And lo! he is behind us.
[Genesis] [32]:[19] This charge he gave to the first, and to the second, and to the third, and to all who went before him after their droves, saying, In this manner speak to Esau when you meet him.
[Genesis] [32]:[20] Moreover ye shall say, Behold thy servant Jacob is coming behind us. For he said, I will appease him with these previous gifts, and afterwards I will look him in the face, perhaps he will receive me favourably.
[Genesis] [32]:[21] So the presents went on before him; but he lay that night in the camp.
[Genesis] [32]:[22] And he arose in the night and took his two wives with the two handmaids and his eleven sons and crossed the ford of Jabok.
[Genesis] [32]:[23] And when he had taken and conducted them over the brook he caused all belonging to him to cross over;
[Genesis] [32]:[24] and Jacob was left alone. And a man wrestled with him till the morning.
[Genesis] [32]:[25] And when he saw that he could not prevail against him he touched the hollow of his thigh; and the hollow of Jacob's thigh became numb, in wrestling with him.
[Genesis] [32]:[26] And he said to him, Let me go: for the dawn approached. But Jacob said I will not let thee go unless thou bless me.
[Genesis] [32]:[27] And he said to him what is thy name? to which he replied, Jacob.
[Genesis] [32]:[28] Thereupon he said to him, Thy name shall no more be Jacob only, but Israel also shall be thy name. Since thou hast been enabled to prevail with God, therefore with men thou shalt be mighty.
[Genesis] [32]:[29] Then Jacob asked him saying, Tell me thy name. And he said why dost thou ask my name? Then he blessed him there.
[Genesis] [32]:[30] And Jacob called the name of that place, Face of God: for I have seen God face to face and my life is preserved.
[Genesis] [32]:[31] Now the sun was risen when he departed from Face of God: and he limped with his thigh.
[Genesis] [32]:[32] For this cause even to this day the children of Israel do not eat the sinew which is in the hollow of the thigh which was numbed; for he had touched the hollow of Jacob's thigh on the sinew and it was numbed.
[Genesis] [33]:[1] Now when Jacob looked up and saw with his eyes, that lo! Esau his brother was coming and with him four hundred men; thereupon Jacob divided his children to Leia, and Rachel, and the two maids.
[Genesis] [33]:[2] And he put the two maids and their children foremost, and Leia and her children next, and Rachel and Joseph hindmost.
[Genesis] [33]:[3] And he himself went before them; and he made a low obeisance to the ground seven times until he came near to his brother.
[Genesis] [33]:[4] And Esau ran to meet him and embraced him and fell upon his neck and kissed him and they both wept.
[Genesis] [33]:[5] And when Esau looked up and saw the women and the children he said, What! Are these thine! To which Jacob replied, These are the children with which God hath favoured thy servant.
[Genesis] [33]:[6] Then the maids and their children came near and made obeisance;
[Genesis] [33]:[7] next Leia and her children came near and made obeisance; and after that Rachel came near with Joseph; and when they had made obeisance
[Genesis] [33]:[8] he said, What is the meaning of all those droves which I met? And Jacob said, That thy servant may find favour in the sight of thee my lord.
[Genesis] [33]:[9] Whereupon Esau said, I have enough, my brother; keep thou thine own.
[Genesis] [33]:[10] But Jacob replied, If I have found favour in thy sight, receive these presents at my hands, because I have seen thy face as one would see the face of God; and thou wilt oblige me.
[Genesis] [33]:[11] Receive these my blessings which I have brought thee; because God hath dealt bountifully with me, and is all things to me. So he pressed him and he took them.
[Genesis] [33]:[12] Then he said, Let us depart and march on straightway.
[Genesis] [33]:[13] But Jacob said to him, My lord perceiveth that the children are tender, and the sheep and cows with me have just had young. If therefore I over drive them one day, all the cattle will die.
[Genesis] [33]:[14] Let my lord go on before his servant, and I will recruit them on the way by a leisurely march as fast as the young can walk, until I come to my lord at Seir.
[Genesis] [33]:[15] Then Esau said, Let me leave with thee some of the people who are with me. To which Jacob replied, What need is there? It is sufficient that I have found favour in the sight of thee, my lord.
[Genesis] [33]:[16] So Esau set out that day on his return to Seir
[Genesis] [33]:[17] and Jacob marched on to Booths, and there built houses for himself and booths for his cattle, for which cause he called the name of that place BOOTHS.
[Genesis] [33]:[18] And when Jacob came from Syrian Mesopotamia he went to Salem a city of the Sekimites which is in the land of Chanaan, and encamped before the city,
[Genesis] [33]:[19] and purchased of Emmor, the father of Sychem, the part of the field where he had pitched his tent for a hundred lambs
[Genesis] [33]:[20] and he erected there an Altar and invoked the God of Israel.
[Genesis] [34]:[1] And Deina the daughter of Leia, whom she bore to Jacob, went out to make an acquaintance with the daughters of the neighbours.
[Genesis] [34]:[2] And Sychem the son of Emmor the Evite, who was the prince of the country, saw her and took her and lay with her and humbled her.
[Genesis] [34]:[3] And having applied himself to gain the affections of Deina the daughter of Jacob, for he loved the virgin, and having spoken to her according to the wishes of the virgin,
[Genesis] [34]:[4] Sychem said to his father Emmor, Get me this young woman for a wife.
[Genesis] [34]:[5] Now Jacob had heard that the son of Emmor had defiled his daughter Deina; but as his sons were with the cattle in the field Jacob held his peace till they came.
[Genesis] [34]:[6] So Emmor the father of Sychem went out to Jacob to speak to him;
[Genesis] [34]:[7] and Jacob's sons came from the plain. And when the men heard, they were stung to the quick; and it was exceedingly painful to them, that he had brought dishonour on Israel by lying with Jacob's daughter, and it should not pass so.
[Genesis] [34]:[8] And Emmor spoke to them saying, My son Sychem hath a passionate fondness for your daughter, therefore give her to him for a wife,
[Genesis] [34]:[9] and unite yourselves to us by the ties of affinity. Give us your daughters and take our daughters for your sons
[Genesis] [34]:[10] and dwell among us. Behold the land before you is spacious. Dwell and trade therein and purchase possessions in it.
[Genesis] [34]:[11] Sychem also said to her father and to her brothers, Let me find favour in your sight; and what ever you say we will give.
[Genesis] [34]:[12] Make the dower ever so great I will give whatever you tell me, so you give me this damsel for a wife.
[Genesis] [34]:[13] But the sons of Jacob answered Sychem and Emmor his father deceitfully. And because their sister had been defiled,
[Genesis] [34]:[14] Symeon and Levi the brothers of Deina spoke to them and said, We cannot do this thing to give our sister to a man who is uncircumcised; for that with us is a reproach.
[Genesis] [34]:[15] On this condition only will we coalesce with you and dwell with you—if you become like us by every male of you being circumcised,
[Genesis] [34]:[16] then we will give you our daughters and take your daughters for wives, and dwell among you and be as one race.
[Genesis] [34]:[17] But if you will not hearken to us to be circumcised, we will take our daughter and depart.
[Genesis] [34]:[18] And the proposal pleased Emmor and Sychem the son of Emmor.
[Genesis] [34]:[19] And the young man made no delay to carry this into execution, for he was passionately fond of Jacob's daughter. Now he was the most honourable of all his father's family:
[Genesis] [34]:[20] so Emmor and his son Sychem went to the gate of their city and spoke to the men of their city saying.
[Genesis] [34]:[21] These are peaceable men; let them dwell with us in the land and use it for the purposes of trade. Behold the land before them is spacious. Let us take their daughters for our wives and give them our daughters.
[Genesis] [34]:[22] Only on this condition will these men dwell with us so as to be one people—that every male among us be circumcised as they are circumcised.
[Genesis] [34]:[23] Will not their cattle and four footed beasts and all their substance become ours? Let us only be like them in this, and they will dwell with us.
[Genesis] [34]:[24] Accordingly all who went in at the gate of their city hearkened to Emmor and his son Sychem and were every male of them, circumcised.
[Genesis] [34]:[25] And on the third day when they were in pain, the two sons of Jacob, Symeon and Levi the brothers of Deina took, each his sword and entered the city without danger and slew every male.
[Genesis] [34]:[26] They slew both Emmor and Sychem his son with the edge of the sword, and took Deina out of Sychem's house and went away.
[Genesis] [34]:[27] And the sons of Jacob came upon the slain and rifled the city in which their sister Deina had been defiled;
[Genesis] [34]:[28] and took their flocks and their herds and their asses, both all that was in the city and all that was in the field,
[Genesis] [34]:[29] and took captive all their servants and all their children and their wives and plundered what was in the city and what was in the houses.
[Genesis] [34]:[30] Thereupon Jacob said to Symeon and Levi, You have made me an object of hatred so as to be deemed a mortal enemy to all the inhabitants of the land, both to the Chananites and the Pherezites. Now as I am few in number they will assemble against me and fall upon me, and I and my house shall be utterly destroyed.
[Genesis] [34]:[31] And they said, But shall they treat our sister like a harlot?
[Genesis] [35]:[1] Then God said to Jacob, arise and go to the place Bethel and dwell there. And erect there an altar to the God who appeared to thee, when thou wast fleeing from the face of thy brother Esau.
[Genesis] [35]:[2] Thereupon Jacob said to his family and to all who were with him, Put away the strange gods which are with you and purify yourselves and change your garments;
[Genesis] [35]:[3] and let us arise and go to Bethel and build there an altar to the God who hearkened to me in a day of distress—who was with me and preserved me in the way in which I went.
[Genesis] [35]:[4] So they gave Jacob the strange gods, which were in their hands and the ear rings which were in their ears; and Jacob hid them in the pine grove, which was in the country of the Sekimites and destroyed them even to this day.
[Genesis] [35]:[5] Then Israel removed from the country of the Sekimites; and a terror of God was on all the cities around them, so that they did not pursue the children of Israel.
[Genesis] [35]:[6] And when Jacob came to Louza which is in the land of Chanaan, the same is Baithel, he and all the people who were with him,
[Genesis] [35]:[7] he built there an altar, and called the name of the place Baithel; because God appeared to him there, when he was fleeing from the face of his brother Esau.
[Genesis] [35]:[8] And Debbora the nurse of Rebekka died and was buried under the Oak below Baithel. So Jacob called its name Oak of mourning.
[Genesis] [35]:[9] And God appeared to Jacob again at Louza after he came from Syrian Mesopotamia, and God blessed him
[Genesis] [35]:[10] and God said to him, Thy name shall no more be called Jacob; but Israel shall be thy name. So he called his name Israel.
[Genesis] [35]:[11] And God said to him, I am thy God. Increase and multiply. Of thee shall be nations and collections of nations; and kings shall spring from thy loins.
[Genesis] [35]:[12] And this land, which I gave to Abraham and Isaak, I have given to thee. To thee it shall belong: and to thy seed after thee I will give this land.
[Genesis] [35]:[13] And when God ascended from him—from the place where he had spoken with him,
[Genesis] [35]:[14] Jacob set up a pillar—a stone pillar in the place where God spoke to him; and he made a libation thereon and poured oil upon it.
[Genesis] [35]:[15] And Jacob called the name of the place where God spoke with him, Baithel.
[Genesis] [35]:[16] And Jacob removed from Baithel and pitched his tent beyond the tower of Gader. And when he was near Chabratha on the way to Ephratha, Rachel was taken in labour and had a hard delivery.
[Genesis] [35]:[17] And when she was in the sharp pangs of child birth, the midwife said to her, Courage; for this also is a son for thee.
[Genesis] [35]:[18] And just as she was departing (for she died) she called his name Sun of my sorrow; but his father called his name Benjamin.
[Genesis] [35]:[19] And Rachel died and was buried in the race ground of Ephratha, which is Bethlehem.
[Genesis] [35]:[20] And Jacob erected a pillar over her grave. The same is the pillar of Rachel's grave even to this day.
[Genesis] [35]:[22] And while Israel dwelt in that land, Reuben went and lay with Balla the concubine of his father Jacob, and Israel heard of it, and it appeared evil in his sight. Now the sons of Jacob were twelve.
[Genesis] [35]:[23] The sons of Leia, Reuben, Jacob's first born, Symeon, Levi, Judas, Issachar, Zabulon:
[Genesis] [35]:[24] and the sons of Rachel, Joseph and Benjamin;
[Genesis] [35]:[25] and the sons of Balla, Rachel's handmaid, Dan and Nephthaleim:
[Genesis] [35]:[26] and the sons of Zelpha, Leia's handmaid, Gad and Aser. These were the sons of Jacob, who were born to him in Syrian Mesopotamia.
[Genesis] [35]:[27] Then Jacob went to his father Isaak, to Mambre, a city of the plain, the same is Chebron in the land of Chanaan, where Abraham and Isaak had sojourned.
[Genesis] [35]:[28] Now the days which Isaak lived were one hundred and eighty years.
[Genesis] [35]:[29] And Isaak fainting away died and was added to his race being an old man and full of days: and his sons Esau and Jacob buried him.
[Genesis] [36]:[1] Now these are the families of Esau, the same is Edom.
[Genesis] [36]:[2] Esau took to himself wives from the daughters of the Chananites—Ada the daughter of Ailom the Chettite; and Olibema the daughter of Ana, the son of Sebegon the Evite;
[Genesis] [36]:[3] and Basemath, Ismael's daughter, the sister of Nabaioth.
[Genesis] [36]:[4] And Ada bore him Eliphas; and Basemath bore Raguel;
[Genesis] [36]:[5] and Olibema bore Jeous and Jeglom and Kore. These were the sons of Esau who were born to him in the land of Chanaan.
[Genesis] [36]:[6] And Esau took his wives and his sons and his daughters and all the persons belonging to his family, and all his substance, and all his cattle, and all that he possessed, and all that he had gotten in the land of Chanaan, and went out from the land of Chanaan, from the face of his brother Jacob:
[Genesis] [36]:[7] for their substance was too great for them to dwell together, and the land where they sojourned could not support them on account of the abundance of their stock.
[Genesis] [36]:[8] And Esau made his abode on mount Seir. Esau is the same as Edom.
[Genesis] [36]:[9] Now these are the families of Esau, the father of the Edomites, on mount Seir.
[Genesis] [36]:[10] And these are the names of Esau's sons—Eliphas son of Ada, Esau's wife; and Raguel, son of Basemath, Esau's wife.
[Genesis] [36]:[11] And the sons of Eliphas were Thaiman, Omar, Sophar, Gotham and Kenez.
[Genesis] [36]:[12] And Thamna was a concubine of Eliphas, the son of Esau, and she bore to Eliphas, Amalek. These were the children of Ada the wife of Esau.
[Genesis] [36]:[13] And these are the sons of Raguel, Nachoth, Zare, Some and Mose. These were the children of Basemath, the wife of Esau.
[Genesis] [36]:[14] And these are the children of Olibema the daughter of Ana, the son of Sebegon, the wife of Esau. She bore to Esau, Jeous and Jeglom and Kore.
[Genesis] [36]:[15] These were the emirs of son Esau. The sons of Eliphas, Esau's first born. Emir Thaiman, emir Omar, emir Sophar, emir Kenez,
[Genesis] [36]:[16] emir Kore, emir Gothom, emir Amelek. These were the emirs of Eliphas in the land of Idumea. These were the children of Ada.
[Genesis] [36]:[17] And these were sons of Raguel, son of Esau, emir Nachoth, emir Zare, emir Some, emir Mose. These were the emirs of Raguel in the land of Edom. These were the children of Basemath, the wife of Esau.
[Genesis] [36]:[18] And these were the sons of Olibema the wife of Esau, emir Jeous, emir Jeglom, emir Kore. These were the emirs of Olibema, the daughter of Ana, Esau's wife.
[Genesis] [36]:[19] These were the children of Esau and these their Emirs. They are the Edomites.
[Genesis] [36]:[20] Now these were sons of Seir the Chorrite, the inhabitant of the land, Lotan, Sobal, Sebegon, Ana,
[Genesis] [36]:[21] and Deson, and Asar, and Rison. These were the emirs of the Chorrite—of son Seir in the land of Edom.
[Genesis] [36]:[22] And these were the sons of Lotan, Chorri and Aiman, and the sister of Lotan, Thamna.
[Genesis] [36]:[23] And these were the sons of Sobal, Golam and Manachath and Gaibel and Sophar and Omar.
[Genesis] [36]:[24] And these were the sons of Sebegon, Aie and Ana. This is that Ana who found the Jameins in the wilderness, when he was feeding his father Sebegon's asses.
[Genesis] [36]:[25] And these were sons of Ana; Deson and Olibema a daughter of Ana:
[Genesis] [36]:[26] And these were the sons of Deson, Amada and Asban and Ithran and Charran.
[Genesis] [36]:[27] And these were the sons of Asar, Balaam and Zoukam and Joukam.
[Genesis] [36]:[28] And these were the sons Rison, Os and Aran.
[Genesis] [36]:[29] And these were the emirs of the Chorrites, emir Lotan, emir Sobal, emir Sebegon, emir Ana,
[Genesis] [36]:[30] emir Deson, emir Asar, emir Rison. These were the emirs of the Chorrites in their clans in the land of Edom.
[Genesis] [36]:[31] And these were the kings who reigned in Edom before a king reigned in Israel;
[Genesis] [36]:[32] Balak son of Beor reigned in Edom and the name of his city was Donnaba;
[Genesis] [36]:[33] and when Balak died, Jobab son of Zara of Bosorra reigned in his stead;
[Genesis] [36]:[34] and when Jobab died, Asom of the land of the Thaimanites reigned in his stead;
[Genesis] [36]:[35] and when Asom died, Adad son of Barad who smote Madiam in the plain of Moab reigned in his stead and the name of his city was Gethaim.
[Genesis] [36]:[36] And when Adad died, Samada of Masekka reigned in his stead;
[Genesis] [36]:[37] and when Samada died, Saul of Rooboth which is by the river reigned in his stead;
[Genesis] [36]:[38] and when Saul died, Ballenon son of Achobar reigned in his stead;
[Genesis] [36]:[39] and when Ballenon son of Achobar died, Arad son of Barad reigned in his stead; and the name of his city was Phogor, and his wife's name, Metebeel. She was a daughter of Matraith, son of Maizoob.
[Genesis] [36]:[40] These are the names of the emirs of Esau in their tribes according to their place in their countries and their nations; Emir of Thamna, emir of Gola, emir of Jether,
[Genesis] [36]:[41] emir of Olibemas, emir of Elas; emir of Phinon,
[Genesis] [36]:[42] emir of Kenez, emir of Thaiman, emir of Mazar,
[Genesis] [36]:[43] emir of Magediel, emir of Zaphoin. These are the emirs of Edom in their respective abodes in the land of their possession. Esau and father Edom is the same.
[Genesis] [37]:[1] Now Jacob dwelt in the land where his father had sojourned in the land of Chanaan;
[Genesis] [37]:[2] and this is the history of Jacob's posterity.
