When Joseph was seventeen years of age and feeding his father's sheep with his brothers, as he was young, namely with the sons of Balla and the sons of Zelpha his father's wives, they carried an ill report against Joseph to Israel their father.
[Genesis] [37]:[3] Now Jacob loved Joseph above all his children because he was the son of his old age; and he had made him a robe of different colours.
[Genesis] [37]:[4] So when his brothers saw that their father loved him more than all his sons, they hated him and could not speak peaceably to him.
[Genesis] [37]:[5] Besides Joseph having dreamed a dream told it to his brothers
[Genesis] [37]:[6] and said to them, Hear this dream which I have dreamed.
[Genesis] [37]:[7] I thought we were binding sheaves in the middle of a field; and my sheaf arose and stood upright, and your sheaves being ranged around made obeisance to my sheaf.
[Genesis] [37]:[8] Upon which his brothers said to him, Shalt thou indeed reign over us or have dominion over us? So they hated him still more for his dreams and for his words.
[Genesis] [37]:[9] And he had another dream and told this to his father and his brothers and said, Behold, I have dreamed another dream, as if the sun and the moon and eleven stars made obeisance to me.
[Genesis] [37]:[10] Upon which his father rebuked him saying, What is this dream which thou hast dreamed? What! Shall I and thy mother and thy brothers come and bow down to thee on the ground?
[Genesis] [37]:[11] So his brothers envied him; but his father pondered the matter.
[Genesis] [37]:[12] After this his brothers having gone to Sychem to feed their father's flocks,
[Genesis] [37]:[13] Israel said to Joseph, Are not thy brothers tending the flocks at Sychem? Come, let me send thee to them. And he said, I am ready.
[Genesis] [37]:[14] Whereupon Israel said to him, Go and see if thy brothers and the flocks be well and bring me word. So he despatched him from the valley of Hebron and he went to Sychem:
[Genesis] [37]:[15] and a man found him wandering in the plain. And the man asked him saying, What art thou seeking?
[Genesis] [37]:[16] To which he replied, I am seeking my brothers, tell me where they are feeding.
[Genesis] [37]:[17] And the man said to him, They have removed from this place; for I heard them say, Let us go to Dothaim. So Joseph went after his brothers and found them at Dothaim.
[Genesis] [37]:[18] Now when they saw him at a great distance before he came near them, they wickedly thought of killing him
[Genesis] [37]:[19] and said one to another, Behold the dreamer is coming.
[Genesis] [37]:[20] Now therefore come let us kill him and throw him into one of these pits, and we will say a savage beast hath devoured him. And let us see what will become of his dreams.
[Genesis] [37]:[21] But when Reuben heard he rescued him out of their hands and said, Let us not touch his life.
[Genesis] [37]:[22] Reuben indeed said to them, Shed not blood. Throw him into one of these pits in the wilderness, but lay not a hand on him, in order that he might rescue him out of their hands and deliver him to his father.
[Genesis] [37]:[23] So when Joseph came to his brothers they stripped him of the variegated robe which he had on
[Genesis] [37]:[24] and took him and threw him into the pit. But the pit was empty. It had no water.
[Genesis] [37]:[25] Then they sat down to eat bread. And raising their eyes they looked; and lo! Ismaelite travellers were coming out of Galaad and their camels were loaded with spiceries, with balm and stacte, which they were carrying to Egypt.
[Genesis] [37]:[26] Thereupon Judas said to his brothers, What advantage will it be though we slay our brother and conceal his blood?
[Genesis] [37]:[27] Come let us sell him to these Ismaelites and let not our hands be upon him, for he is our brother and our flesh. So his brothers hearkened to him.
[Genesis] [37]:[28] And when the Madienite merchants came, they drew him up. They lifted Joseph up out of the pit and sold him to the Ismaelites for twenty pieces of gold; and they carried Joseph down to Egypt.
[Genesis] [37]:[29] Now when Reuben came back to the pit and did not see Joseph in it, he rent his cloaths
[Genesis] [37]:[30] and returned to his brothers and said, The boy is gone. And as for me where now am I to go?
[Genesis] [37]:[31] Then they took Joseph's coat, and having killed a kid of the goats they besmeared the coat with the blood
[Genesis] [37]:[32] and sent away the variegated robe and had it carried to his father and said, This we found. Examine whether it be thy son's robe or not.
[Genesis] [37]:[33] And he knew it and said, It is my son's robe. An evil beast hath devoured him. A wild beast hath torn Joseph.
[Genesis] [37]:[34] Then Jacob rent his cloaths and put on sackcloth on his loins and mourned for his son many days.
[Genesis] [37]:[35] And all his sons and daughters assembled and came to comfort him, but he would not be comforted saying, I will go to my son mourning, to the mansion of the dead. Thus his father mourned for him.
[Genesis] [37]:[36] But the Madienites sold Joseph in Egypt to Petephres the chamberlain of Pharao, the captain of the guards.
[Genesis] [38]:[1] Now at that time Judas went down from his brothers and came to a certain Odollamite whose name was Eiras.
[Genesis] [38]:[2] And Judas saw there a daughter of a Chananite, her name was Sava. And he took her and went in unto her
[Genesis] [38]:[3] and she conceived and bore a son and called his name Er.
[Genesis] [38]:[4] And she conceived again and bore a son and called his name Aunan.
[Genesis] [38]:[5] And she bore another son and called his name Selom. Now she was at Chasbi when she bore them.
[Genesis] [38]:[6] And for Er his first born Judas took a wife whose name was Thamar.
[Genesis] [38]:[7] But Er the first born of Judas was wicked in the sight of the Lord, and God slew him.
[Genesis] [38]:[8] Whereupon Judas said to Aunan, Go in unto thy brother's wife and be a husband to her and raise up a seed for thy brother.
[Genesis] [38]:[9] But when Aunan knew that the seed would not be his, it came to pass that when he went in unto his brother's wife he shed it on the ground; that he might not give seed to his brother.
[Genesis] [38]:[10] And it appeared evil in the sight of God that he had done this, therefore he caused him also to die.
[Genesis] [38]:[11] Then Judas said to Thamar his daughter in law, Continue a widow in thy father's house till my son Selom grow up. For he said, Peradventure he also may die as his brothers did. So Thamar went and dwelt at her father's house.
[Genesis] [38]:[12] But the time was prolonged and Sava the wife of Judas died. And when Judas was comforted he went up to his sheep shearers, he and his shepherd Eiras the Odollamite, to Thamna.
[Genesis] [38]:[13] And it was told Thamar his daughter in law saying, Lo! thy father in law is going up to Thamna to shear his sheep.
[Genesis] [38]:[14] Whereupon she put off the robes of her widowhood and put on a veil and adorned herself and sat near the gates of Ainan in the way to Thamna. For she saw that Selom was grown up and that he had not given her to him for a wife.
[Genesis] [38]:[15] And when Judas saw her, he thought she was a harlot, for she had covered her face
[Genesis] [38]:[16] and he did not know her. So he turned aside to her and said to her, Let me come in unto thee. For he did not know that she was his daughter in law. And she said, What wilt thou give me if thou come in unto me?
[Genesis] [38]:[17] To which he replied, I will send thee a kid of the goats from the flock. And she said, Wilt thou give me a pledge, till thou send it?
[Genesis] [38]:[18] And he said, What pledge shall I give thee? And she said, Thy ring and bracelet and the staff in thy hand. So he gave them to her, and went in unto her, and she conceived by him.
[Genesis] [38]:[19] Then she arose and went away, and took off her veil and put on the garments of her widowhood.
[Genesis] [38]:[20] And Judas sent the kid of the goats by the hands of his shepherd the Odollamite to receive from the woman the pledge.
[Genesis] [38]:[21] But he not finding her asked the men of the place, Where is the harlot that was at Ainan by the way side? And they said, There was not a harlot there.
[Genesis] [38]:[22] Thereupon he returned to Judas and said, I have not found her. And the men of the place said there was not a harlot there.
[Genesis] [38]:[23] Then Judas said, Let her keep them. But perhaps we may be laughed at. I indeed sent the kid. Thou however hast not found her.
[Genesis] [38]:[24] Now after three months it was told Judas saying, Thamar thy daughter in law hath played the harlot, and lo! she is with child by her whoredom. Whereupon Judas said, Bring her out and let her be burned.
[Genesis] [38]:[25] And when she was brought out she sent to her father in law saying, By the man to whom these belong I am with child. Moreover she said, Examine, whose is this ring and this bracelet and this staff?
[Genesis] [38]:[26] And Judas acknowledged them and said, Thamar hath more justice on her side than I, because I did not give her to my son Selom. And he proceeded no more to have knowledge of her.
[Genesis] [38]:[27] And when the time of her delivery came she had twins.
[Genesis] [38]:[28] And when she was in child birth, one put forth its hand, and the midwife took and tied a scarlet thread about its hand, saying, This will be the first born.
[Genesis] [38]:[29] But when it had drawn back its hand, his brother immediately came forth. Whereupon she said, What! Hath there been a breach made for thee? So she called his name Phares.
[Genesis] [38]:[30] And after this his brother came forth, on whose hand was the scarlet thread. So she called his name Zara.
[Genesis] [39]:[1] Now when Joseph was brought to Egypt, Petephres the chamberlain of Pharao, the captain of the guards, an Egyptian, bought him at the hands of the Ismaelites, who had brought him thither.
[Genesis] [39]:[2] And the Lord was with Joseph, so that he was a fortunate man and was in the same house with his master the Egyptian.
[Genesis] [39]:[3] And his master perceived that the Lord was with him, and that the Lord prospered in his hands what ever he did.
[Genesis] [39]:[4] So Joseph found favour in the sight of his master; and he was pleased with him and set him over his household and committed all that he had to Joseph's charge.
[Genesis] [39]:[5] And after he had made him overseer of his household, and over all that he had, the Lord blessed the house of the Egyptian for Joseph's sake; and there was a blessing of the Lord on all his substance, in his house and in his field.
[Genesis] [39]:[6] So he left to Joseph's management all that he had, and knew not any thing he had save the bread which he ate. Now Joseph was a comely person and of a very beautiful countenance,
[Genesis] [39]:[7] and after these things his master's wife cast her eyes on Joseph and said, Lie with me;
[Genesis] [39]:[8] but he refused and said to his master's wife, Seeing my lord, on account of his confidence in me, knoweth not any thing in his house, and hath committed to my charge all that he hath
[Genesis] [39]:[9] and there is none in the family superior to me, nor any thing withheld from me, except thyself because thou art his wife; how then can I do this wicked act and commit sin in the presence of God.
[Genesis] [39]:[10] And when she had spoken to Joseph day after day and he hearkened not to her to lie down with her to have commerce with her,
[Genesis] [39]:[11] it came to pass that one day when Joseph went into the house to transact his business and none of the domestics were within,
[Genesis] [39]:[12] she took hold of his mantle saying, Lie with me. But he leaving his mantle in her hands fled and went out.
[Genesis] [39]:[13] And when she saw that he had left his mantle in her hands and had fled and was gone,
[Genesis] [39]:[14] she called those who were in the house and spoke to them saying, See! He hath brought in amongst us a Hebrew slave to insult us. He came to me saying, Lie with me. But I cried with a loud voice.
[Genesis] [39]:[15] And when he heard that I raised my voice and cried aloud he left his mantle with me, fled and went out.
[Genesis] [39]:[16] And she kept the garment by her till his master came home
[Genesis] [39]:[17] and spoke to him in like terms saying, The Hebrew slave whom thou hast brought in amongst us came in to me to insult me and said to me, Let me lie with thee.
[Genesis] [39]:[18] But when he heard that I raised my voice and cried aloud; leaving his mantle with me he fled and went out.
[Genesis] [39]:[19] When his master heard the words of his wife, which she spoke saying, In this manner did thy slave treat me, he was greatly incensed.
[Genesis] [39]:[20] So Joseph's master took and cast him into prison—into the place where the king's prisoners were confined.
[Genesis] [39]:[21] But in the prison the Lord was with Joseph, and continued his kindness and gave him favour in the sight of the principal keeper of the prison.
[Genesis] [39]:[22] And the principal keeper of the prison committed to Joseph's care the prison and all the prisoners who were in the prison. And whatever was done there, he was the person who caused it to be done.
[Genesis] [39]:[23] The principal keeper took no cognizance of any thing himself; for all things were committed to Joseph's management, because the Lord was with him, and the Lord prospered in his hands whatever he did.
[Genesis] [40]:[1] Now after these things it happened that the chief butler of the king of Egypt and the chief baker offended their lord the king of Egypt.
[Genesis] [40]:[2] And Pharao was incensed against his two officers, against the chief butler and against the chief baker
[Genesis] [40]:[3] and sent them under a guard to prison—to the place to which Joseph had been sent.
[Genesis] [40]:[4] And the keeper of the prison committed them to Joseph and he attended them. And when they had been there some days in prison,
[Genesis] [40]:[5] they both had a dream, in the same night. And the appearance of the dream both of the chief butler and the chief baker, who belonged to the king of Egypt and were in prison, was similar.
[Genesis] [40]:[6] And in the morning when Joseph went in to them he saw that they were troubled.
[Genesis] [40]:[7] Whereupon he asked the officers of Pharao who were confined with him at his master's, saying, Why are your countenances sad to day?
[Genesis] [40]:[8] And they said to him, We have had a dream and there is none to interpret it. And Joseph said, Is not the interpretation thereof from God? Therefore tell me.
[Genesis] [40]:[9] So the chief butler told Joseph his dream and said, In my sleep there was a vine before me,
[Genesis] [40]:[10] and on the vine three branches and one was flourishing and had produced clusters, and the grapes of one cluster were ripe.
[Genesis] [40]:[11] And I had in my hand the cup of Pharao. So I took the bunch and squeezed it into the cup and gave the cup into Pharao's hand.
[Genesis] [40]:[12] Thereupon Joseph said to him, This is the interpretation of it. The three branches are three days.
[Genesis] [40]:[13] Three days hence Pharao will take cognizance of thy administration and restore thee to thy office of cup bearer, and thou shalt give Pharao's cup into his hand according to thy former dignity, when thou wast butler.
[Genesis] [40]:[14] But think of me by thy own case, when it shall be well with thee; and do me the favour to mention me to Pharao and release me from this prison.
[Genesis] [40]:[15] For I have been stolen from the land of the Hebrews; and here I have done nothing, yet they have thrown me into this dungeon.
[Genesis] [40]:[16] And when the chief baker saw that he interpreted well he said to Joseph, I also had a dream, I thought I was carrying on my head three baskets of cakes,
[Genesis] [40]:[17] and in the uppermost basket, pastry of all the sorts which Pharao eateth. And the birds of the air devoured those in the uppermost basket on my head.
[Genesis] [40]:[18] And Joseph answered and said to him, This is the interpretation thereof. The three baskets are three days.
[Genesis] [40]:[19] Three days hence Pharao will take off thy head and hang thee on a gibbet and the birds of the air will devour thy flesh.
[Genesis] [40]:[20] Accordingly it fell out, that the third day was Pharao's birth day, and he made an entertainment for all his servants and took cognizance of the administration of the butler, and the administration of the baker in the midst of his servants.
[Genesis] [40]:[21] And he restored the butler to his office, and he gave the cup into Pharao's hand.
[Genesis] [40]:[22] But the chief baker he hanged as Joseph had interpreted to them.
[Genesis] [40]:[23] Yet the chief butler made no mention of Joseph, but passed him over in oblivion.
[Genesis] [41]:[1] But it happened that at the end of two years Pharao had a dream. He thought he was standing by the river;
[Genesis] [41]:[2] and lo! there came up out of the river seven cows of a beautiful figure and very fat; and they fed on the sedge.
[Genesis] [41]:[3] And seven other cows came up after them out of the river, ill favoured and lean; and they fed near the other cows on the bank of the river;
[Genesis] [41]:[4] and the seven ill favoured and lean cows devoured the seven cows which were of a beautiful figure and full of flesh. And Pharao awoke.
[Genesis] [41]:[5] Again he dreamed a second time; and lo! seven ears of corn sprang upon one stalk, choice and good:
[Genesis] [41]:[6] and lo! seven ears thin and blasted sprang up after them.
[Genesis] [41]:[7] And the seven thin and blasted ears devoured the seven ears which were choice and full. And Pharao awoke; and it was a dream.
[Genesis] [41]:[8] And in the morning his spirit was troubled, and he sent for all the interpreters of Egypt, and all the wise men thereof, and told them his dream, but there was none who could interpret it to Pharao.
[Genesis] [41]:[9] Upon this the chief butler spoke to Pharao, saying, I this day acknowledge my fault.
[Genesis] [41]:[10] Pharao was angry with his servants, and ordered us to be confined in the house of the captain of the guards—both me and the chief baker.
[Genesis] [41]:[11] And both I and he had a dream the same night, each had a dream regarding himself.
[Genesis] [41]:[12] And there was there with us a youth, a Hebrew servant of the captain of the guard, and we told him, and he interpreted to us.
[Genesis] [41]:[13] And it fell out as he interpreted to us—I was restored to my office; but he was hanged.
[Genesis] [41]:[14] Upon this Pharao sent for Joseph, and they brought him out of the prison. And when they had shaved him and changed his raiment, he came to Pharao.
[Genesis] [41]:[15] And Pharao said to Joseph, I have had a dream and there is none who can interpret it. But I have heard people say of thee, that upon hearing dreams thou canst interpret them.
[Genesis] [41]:[16] In reply to which, Joseph said to Pharao, Without God no answer of peace can be given to Pharao.
[Genesis] [41]:[17] Then Pharao spoke to Joseph saying, In my sleep I thought I was standing near the edge of the river;
[Genesis] [41]:[18] and there came up as it were out of the river, seven cows of a beautiful figure, and very fat; and they fed on the sedge.
[Genesis] [41]:[19] And lo! seven other cows came up after them, out of the river, ugly and ill favoured and lean of flesh; such that I never saw worse in all the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [41]:[20] And the seven ill favoured and lean cows devoured the first seven cows which were choice and good.
[Genesis] [41]:[21] But though they devoured them, they gave no sign of fulnes. Their appearance was as ugly as before. Then I awoke and again went to sleep,
[Genesis] [41]:[22] and in my sleep I again saw as if seven ears of corn sprang up, on one stalk, full and good;
[Genesis] [41]:[23] and adjoining them seven other ears sprang up, thin and blasted.
[Genesis] [41]:[24] And the seven thin and blasted ears devoured the seven ears which were full and good. Now I have told the interpreters; but there is not one who can interpret this to me.
[Genesis] [41]:[25] Then Joseph said to Pharao, The dream of Pharao is one. God hath shewn Pharao what he is about to do.
[Genesis] [41]:[26] The seven beautiful cows are seven years; and the seven good ears are seven years. The dream of Pharao is one.
[Genesis] [41]:[27] The seven lean cows also which came up after them are seven years; and the seven thin and blasted ears are seven years. There shall be seven years of famine.
[Genesis] [41]:[28] This is the thing which I have said to Pharao, God hath shewn Pharao what he is about to do.
[Genesis] [41]:[29] Behold seven years are coming of great plenty in all the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [41]:[30] But after these there will come seven years of famine. And they will cause the coming plenty to be forgotten in all Egypt. And the famine will consume the land,
[Genesis] [41]:[31] so that the plenty will not be known in the land, by reason of the famine which will come after it: for it will be very great.
[Genesis] [41]:[32] And in as much as the dream of Pharao was doubled, the thing which God had determined will surely come to pass; and God is hasting to do it.
[Genesis] [41]:[33] Now therefore look out for a man of prudence and understanding, and set him over the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [41]:[34] And let Pharao appoint and set governors of districts over the whole land, and let them send in a fifth of all the products of the land of Egypt, during the seven years of plenty,
[Genesis] [41]:[35] and collect all the eatables of those seven good years which are coming. And let food be collected under the care of Pharao, and eatables stored in the cities.
[Genesis] [41]:[36] So shall food be kept in the country, against the seven years of famine which will be in the land of Egypt, and the country will not be wasted with the famine.
[Genesis] [41]:[37] And the advice was agreeable in the view of Pharao and in the view of all his servants.
[Genesis] [41]:[38] And Pharao said to all his servants, Can we find such a man as this who hath in him the spirit of God?
[Genesis] [41]:[39] Then Pharao said to Joseph, Seeing God hath shewn thee all these things, there is none more prudent and wise than thou thyself.
[Genesis] [41]:[40] Thou shalt be over my household and to thy command all my people shall pay obedience. Only on the throne will I be greater than thou.
[Genesis] [41]:[41] Moreover Pharao said to Joseph, Behold I have set thee this day over all the land of Egypt,
[Genesis] [41]:[42] And Pharao took off the ring from his own hand and put it on Joseph's hand, and clothed him with a cotton robe, and put a chain of gold about his neck;
[Genesis] [41]:[43] and caused him to mount a chariot next to that of his own. And a herald made proclamation before him. And when he had set him over all the land of Egypt,
[Genesis] [41]:[44] Pharao said to Joseph, I am Pharao. Without thee no man shall lift up his hand in all the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [41]:[45] And Pharao called Joseph's name Psonthomphanech and gave him Aseneth a daughter of Petephres the priest of Heliopolis to be his wife.
[Genesis] [41]:[46] Now Joseph was thirty years old, when he stood before Pharao king of Egypt. And Joseph went out from the presence of Pharao and went through all the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [41]:[47] And in the seven years of plenty the land produced in vast abundance
[Genesis] [41]:[48] and he collected all the varieties of food of those seven years, in which there was plenty in the land of Egypt and stored it in the cities. In every city he stored the eatable productions of the fields around.
[Genesis] [41]:[49] So Joseph collected food like the sand of the sea in great abundance until it could not be numbered, for it was innumerable.
[Genesis] [41]:[50] Now before the seven years of famine Joseph had two sons, whom Aseneth the daughter of Petephres the priest of Heliopolis bore to him.
[Genesis] [41]:[51] And Joseph called the name of his first born Manasses, because God hath made me forget all my troubles and all my father's family.
[Genesis] [41]:[52] And the name of his second son he called Ephraim, because God hath made me fruitful in the land of my affliction.
[Genesis] [41]:[53] And when the seven years of plenty which had been in the land of Egypt were past,
[Genesis] [41]:[54] the seven years of famine began to come as Joseph said. And there was a famine in the whole earth, but in all the land of Egypt there was bread.
[Genesis] [41]:[55] And when all the land of Egypt began to be in want and the people cried to Pharao for bread, Pharao said to all the Egyptians, Go to Joseph and do whatever he saith to you.
[Genesis] [41]:[56] As the famine was over the face of the whole earth, when Joseph opened all his granaries and sold to all the Egyptians,
[Genesis] [41]:[57] all the countries came to Egypt to buy of Joseph, for the famine was great in the whole earth.
[Genesis] [42]:[1] And when Jacob found that there was a sale in Egypt he said to his sons, Why sit ye still?
[Genesis] [42]:[2] Behold I have heard that there is corn in Egypt; go down thither and buy us a little food that we may live and not die.
[Genesis] [42]:[3] So Joseph's ten brothers went down to purchase corn from Egypt:
[Genesis] [42]:[4] but Benjamin the brother of Joseph he did not send with his brothers; for he said, Perhaps some misfortune may happen to him.
[Genesis] [42]:[5] Now when the sons of Israel went to buy in company with others who were going, for the famine was in the land of Chanaan,
[Genesis] [42]:[6] Joseph was the governor of the country; he sold to all the people of that land. So when Joseph's brothers came they made obeisance to him with their face to the ground.
[Genesis] [42]:[7] And when Joseph saw his brothers he knew them and was reserved and spoke harshly to them and said to them, Whence come you? And they said, From the land of Chanaan to buy food.
[Genesis] [42]:[8] Now Joseph knew his brothers, but they did not know him.
[Genesis] [42]:[9] And Joseph remembered the dreams, which he had dreamed and said to them, You are spies. You are come to examine the ways into this country.
[Genesis] [42]:[10] And they said, No, my lord. We thy servants are come to buy food.
[Genesis] [42]:[11] We are all the sons of one man. We are men of peace. Thy servants are not spies.
[Genesis] [42]:[12] But he said, Nay, you are come for no other purpose, but to examine the ways into this country.
[Genesis] [42]:[13] Then they said, Thy servants were twelve brothers in the land of Chanaan. And lo! the youngest is now with our father; but the other is no more.
[Genesis] [42]:[14] Upon this Joseph said, It is as I said, you are spies.
[Genesis] [42]:[15] By this you shall be proved. By the health of Pharao you shall not depart hence unless your youngest brother come hither.
[Genesis] [42]:[16] Send one of you and bring your brother; but you must be confined, until your words be proved whether you speak true or not. If not, by the health of Pharao you are certainly spies.
[Genesis] [42]:[17] So he committed them to prison three days.
[Genesis] [42]:[18] And on the third day he said to them, Do this and live; for I fear God.
[Genesis] [42]:[19] If you are men of peace let one brother of you be kept in confinement. As for the rest, go and carry back your purchase of provisions
[Genesis] [42]:[20] and bring your younger brother to me, so shall your words be believed. Unless you do this you shall die. On this occasion they acted thus—
[Genesis] [42]:[21] they said one to another, We indeed are to blame in respect to our brother. Because we disregarded the anguish of his soul, when he intreated us and we would not hear him; for this cause therefore this affliction is come upon us.
[Genesis] [42]:[22] And Reuben in reply said to them, Did I not speak to you saying, Do the lad no injury, but you hearkened not to me. Now behold inquisition is made for his blood.
[Genesis] [42]:[23] They indeed did not know that Joseph heard; for there was one to interpret between them.
[Genesis] [42]:[24] On this Joseph withdrew from them and wept. Then he came to them again and spoke to them and he took Symeon from among them and bound him before their eyes.
[Genesis] [42]:[25] Then Joseph gave orders to fill their sacks with corn and to put every one's money in his bag and to furnish them with provisions for the journey. So this being done for them
[Genesis] [42]:[26] they loaded their asses with the corn, and departed thence.
[Genesis] [42]:[27] And one of them having loosed his bag to give his asses provender at a place where they lodged saw his roll of money, for it was in the mouth of his bag,
[Genesis] [42]:[28] and said to his brothers, The money is returned to me. See, here it is in my bag. Whereupon their heart fainted and they were alarmed and said one to another, What is this which God hath done to us?
[Genesis] [42]:[29] And when they came to their father Jacob to the land of Chanaan, they told him all that had happened to them and said,
[Genesis] [42]:[30] The man the lord of the country spoke harshly to us and put us in prison as coming to spy the country;
[Genesis] [42]:[31] and we told him, We are men of peace, We are not spies.
[Genesis] [42]:[32] We are twelve brothers, sons of one father. One is no more. And the youngest is now with our father in the land of Chanaan.
[Genesis] [42]:[33] Upon which the man the lord of the country said to us, By this I shall know that you are men of peace. Leave here with me one brother; and taking the corn purchased for your family go
[Genesis] [42]:[34] and bring me your younger brother, then I shall know that you are not spies; but men of peace. And I will restore to you your brother and you may traffick in the land.
[Genesis] [42]:[35] And when they emptied their sacks every one's roll of money was in their several sacks. And when they and their father saw their rolls of money they were terrified.
[Genesis] [42]:[36] And Jacob their father said to them, Me you have bereaved of children. Joseph is gone. Symeon is gone. And will you take away Benjamin? All these things are against me.
[Genesis] [42]:[37] Then Reuben spoke to their father saying, Slay my two sons, if I bring him not back to thee. Commit him to my charge and I will bring him back to thee.
[Genesis] [42]:[38] But he said, My son shall not go down with you. For his brother is dead and he alone is left. If any mischief should happen to him in the way you are going, you will bring down my old age with sorrow to the mansion of the dead.
[Genesis] [43]:[1] Still the famine prevailed in the land.
[Genesis] [43]:[2] So when they had eaten up the corn which they brought from Egypt their father said to them, Go again and buy for us a little food.
[Genesis] [43]:[3] Whereupon Judas spoke to him and said, The man who is the lord of the country solemnly protested saying, You shall not see my face, unless your youngest brother be with you.
[Genesis] [43]:[4] If therefore thou wilt send our brother with us we will go down and buy thee food.
[Genesis] [43]:[5] But if thou wilt not send our brother with us we will not go. For the man spoke to us saying, You shall not see my face unless your youngest brother be with you.
[Genesis] [43]:[6] And Israel said, Why did you do me this ill in telling the man that you had a brother?
[Genesis] [43]:[7] And they said, The man examined us strictly touching our family and said, Is your father alive? And have you a brother? And we answered him according to this examination. Could we know that he would say, Bring your brother?
[Genesis] [43]:[8] Moreover Judas said to his father Israel, Send thy son with me and we will arise and go that we may live and not die, both we and thou and all that we have.
[Genesis] [43]:[9] I undertake the charge of him. At my hand require him. If I bring him not back to thee and set him before thee let me be a sinner against thee for ever.
[Genesis] [43]:[10] For if we had not been delayed we might have now been back a second time.
[Genesis] [43]:[11] Then Israel their father said to them, If it must be so, do this, Take of all the fruits of this land in your vessels and carry down for the man presents of balm and honey and incense both stacte and terebinthus and almonds.
[Genesis] [43]:[12] Take also double money in your hands and carry back the money which was returned in your sacks; perhaps it was a mistake.
[Genesis] [43]:[13] And take your brother and arise and go down to the man.
[Genesis] [43]:[14] And may my God grant you favour in the sight of the man that he may send back your brother with Benjamin. As for me I am bereaved of children as I have been bereaved.
[Genesis] [43]:[15] So the men took the presents and the double money in their hands and Benjamin and arose and went down to Egypt and stood before Joseph.
[Genesis] [43]:[16] And when Joseph saw them and Benjamin his brother by the same mother he said to his steward, conduct these men into the house and kill victims and make ready, for these men are to dine with me at noon.
[Genesis] [43]:[17] So the man did as Joseph ordered and conducted them to Joseph's house.
[Genesis] [43]:[18] And when the men saw that they were conducted to Joseph's house, they said, On account of the money which was returned in our bags in the former journey we are brought in for the purpose of accusing us and charging us with it, that he may make us slaves and take our asses.
[Genesis] [43]:[19] Therefore they went near to the man the steward of Joseph's house and spoke to him at the gate of the house saying,
[Genesis] [43]:[20] Sir, we came down for the first time to purchase provisions,
[Genesis] [43]:[21] But when we came to the lodging place and opened our bags every man's money was in his bag. This money we have now brought back in our hands in full weight.
[Genesis] [43]:[22] And we have brought with us other money to buy provisions—Who put the money into our bags we do not know.
[Genesis] [43]:[23] Upon which he said to them, Peace be to you: fear not, your God and the God of your fathers hath given you treasures in your bags. But as for your money, with perfect good will I decline receiving it. Then he brought out Symeon to them
[Genesis] [43]:[24] and brought them water to wash their feet and gave provender to their asses.
[Genesis] [43]:[25] And they were employed in making ready the presents until Joseph came at noon, for they heard that he was to dine there.
[Genesis] [43]:[26] So when Joseph came home they brought him the presents which they had in their hands into the house and made obeisance to him with their face on the ground.
[Genesis] [43]:[27] And he asked them of their welfare and said to them, Is your father well, the old man of whom you spoke? Is he still living?
[Genesis] [43]:[28] And they said, Thy servant, our father is well. He is still alive. And he said, Blessed of God is that man. Whereupon they bowed down and made obeisance to him.
[Genesis] [43]:[29] And when Joseph raised his eyes and saw Benjamin his brother by the same mother, he said, This is your youngest brother whom you promised to bring to me? Then he said, God be gracious to thee, my son.
[Genesis] [43]:[30] And Joseph was discomposed, for his bowels yearned towards his brother; so he sought where to weep and went into his chamber and wept there.
[Genesis] [43]:[31] And when he had washed his face and come out he restrained himself and said, Serve up dinner.
[Genesis] [43]:[32] So they served up for him by himself and for them by themselves, and for the Egyptians who dined with him, by themselves. For the Egyptians could not eat bread with the Hebrews. For that to the Egyptians is an abomination.
[Genesis] [43]:[33] And they sat before him, the first born according to his birth right and the youngest according to his youth. And the men were astonished, one at another.
[Genesis] [43]:[34] And when they received their several messes from him, the mess of Benjamin was five times larger than any of theirs. And when they had drank and been plentifully regaled with him
[Genesis] [44]:[1] Joseph gave orders to the steward of his house saying, Fill the bags of these men with corn, as much as they can carry, and put every one's money in the mouth of his bag,
[Genesis] [44]:[2] and put my silver cup in the bag of the youngest along with the price of his corn. And Joseph's orders being punctually executed;
[Genesis] [44]:[3] the next morning as soon as it was light the men were sent away they and their asses.
[Genesis] [44]:[4] And when they were gone out of the city, but had not got far off, Joseph said to his steward, Arise and pursue those men; and when thou shalt overtake them, say to them, Why have you returned evil for good?
[Genesis] [44]:[5] Why have you stolen my silver cup? Is not this it out of which my lord drinketh. By it indeed he divineth. You have done evil in so doing.
[Genesis] [44]:[6] So when he overtook them he spoke to them in these terms.
[Genesis] [44]:[7] Upon which they said to him, Why doth my lord speak in such terms as these: Far be it from thy servants to do such an act.
[Genesis] [44]:[8] If we indeed brought back to thee from the land of Chanaan the money which we found in our bags, how should we steal out of thy lord's house silver or gold?
[Genesis] [44]:[9] With whomsoever of thy servants thou shalt find the cup, let him die, and we will be our lord's slaves.
[Genesis] [44]:[10] And he said, Well; Let it be as you say thus far—With whomsoever the cup be found, he shall be my slave; but you shall be blameless.
[Genesis] [44]:[11] Then they hasted, and set down, every one his bag on the ground, and opened, every one his bag.
[Genesis] [44]:[12] And he searched beginning at the eldest till he came to the youngest; and he found the cup in Benjamin's bag.
[Genesis] [44]:[13] Whereupon they rent their cloaths and put every one his bag on his ass and returned to the city.
[Genesis] [44]:[14] And Judas with his brothers went in to Joseph who was still there; and they fell on the ground before him.
[Genesis] [44]:[15] And Joseph said to them, What is this you have done? Did you not know that such a man as I can divine?
[Genesis] [44]:[16] And Judas said, What answer can we make to my lord? Or what can we say? Or how can we justify ourselves? God hath indeed found out the iniquity of thy servants. Behold we are slaves to our lord—both we and he with whom the cup was found.
[Genesis] [44]:[17] And Joseph said, Far be it from me to do such a thing. The man with whom the cup was found shall be my servant; but as for you go up in peace to your father.
[Genesis] [44]:[18] Upon this Judas came near to him and said, my lord, let thy servant speak a word in thy presence and be not angry with thy servant, for thou art next to Pharao.
[Genesis] [44]:[19] Thou my lord didst ask thy servants saying, Have you a father or a brother?
[Genesis] [44]:[20] And we said to my lord, We have a father an old man; and he hath a younger son of his old age. His brother is dead, and as he is the only one of his mother left; his father loveth him.
[Genesis] [44]:[21] Then thou saidst to thy servants, Bring him down to me and I will take care of him.
[Genesis] [44]:[22] And we said to my lord, This son cannot leave his father; for if he were to leave him, his father would die.
[Genesis] [44]:[23] Then thou saidst to thy servants, Unless your younger brother come down with you, you shall no more be admitted to see my face.
[Genesis] [44]:[24] So when we went up to thy servant our father, we told him the words of our lord.
[Genesis] [44]:[25] And when our father said, Go again and buy for us a little food;
[Genesis] [44]:[26] we said, We cannot go. If indeed our youngest brother will go down with us, we will go down. For we cannot see the man's face unless our youngest brother be with us.
[Genesis] [44]:[27] Thereupon thy servant our father said to us, You know that my wife bore me two sons;
[Genesis] [44]:[28] and that one went out from me and you said he was devoured by a wild beast and I have not seen him since.
[Genesis] [44]:[29] If therefore you take this one also from me and any misfortune happen to him on the journey, you will bring down my old age with sorrow to the grave.
[Genesis] [44]:[30] Now therefore if I should go to thy servant our father and this son be not with us, as his life dependeth on the life of this son,
[Genesis] [44]:[31] it will come to pass that when he shall see that this son is not with us he will die. Thus will thy servants bring down the old age of thy servant our father with sorrow to the grave.
[Genesis] [44]:[32] Besides as thy servant received this son from his father and said, If I bring him not back to thee and set him before thee, let me be as a sinner against my father for ever;
[Genesis] [44]:[33] now therefore let me stay with thee instead of him as a slave to my lord, and let this son go up with his brothers
[Genesis] [44]:[34] (for how can I go up to my father and this son not with us?) that I may not see the evils which will befal my father.
[Genesis] [45]:[1] Upon this Joseph could not restrain himself before all his attendants, but said, Dismiss all from me. So there was no attendant with Joseph when he made himself known to his brothers,
[Genesis] [45]:[2] but in weeping he raised his voice so that all the Egyptians heard and the news was carried to Pharao's house,
[Genesis] [45]:[3] And Joseph said to his brothers, I am Joseph. Is my father still alive? But his brothers could not answer him; for they were confounded.
[Genesis] [45]:[4] Then Joseph said to his brothers, Come near to me? And when they came near; he said, I am Joseph your brother whom you sold into Egypt.
[Genesis] [45]:[5] Now therefore be not grieved, nor let it afflict you that you sold me hither; for God sent me before you for life.
[Genesis] [45]:[6] For this is but the second year of famine on the earth and there will be yet five years, in which there will be neither tillage nor harvest,
[Genesis] [45]:[7] so that God hath sent me before you to preserve for you a remnant on the earth and to nourish your great remnant.
[Genesis] [45]:[8] Now therefore it was not you who sent me hither, but it was God. And he hath made me like a father to Pharao, and lord of all his house and governor of all the land of Egypt.
[Genesis] [45]:[9] Haste therefore and go up to my father and say to him, Thus saith thy son Joseph, God hath made me ruler of all the land of Egypt. Come down therefore to me and tarry not.
[Genesis] [45]:[10] And thou shalt dwelt in the land of Gesem; Arabia and be near me; both thou and thy sons and thy grandsons, thy flocks and thy herds and all that thou hast.
[Genesis] [45]:[11] And I will nourish thee there, (for the famine will continue five years longer) that thou and thy sons and all that belong to thee may not be consumed.
[Genesis] [45]:[12] Behold your eyes and the eyes of my brother Benjamin see that it is my mouth which speaketh to you.
[Genesis] [45]:[13] Therefore tell my father all my glory in Egypt and all that you see; and make haste and bring my father down hither.
[Genesis] [45]:[14] Then he fell upon the neck of his brother Benjamin and wept upon him.
[Genesis] [45]:[15] And Benjamin wept on his neck. And he kissed all his brothers and wept upon them. And after that his brothers conversed with him.
[Genesis] [45]:[16] Now when the news spread to Pharao's house saying, Joseph's brothers are come, Pharao and his servants were rejoiced.
[Genesis] [45]:[17] And Pharao said to Joseph, Say to thy brothers, Do this, Load your beasts and go to the land of Chanaan
[Genesis] [45]:[18] and take up your father and all that you have and come to me and I will give you of all the good things of Egypt, and ye shall eat the fat of the land.
[Genesis] [45]:[19] And do thou issue these orders, Take with you from the land of Egypt waggons for your children and your wives and take up your father and come;
[Genesis] [45]:[20] and pay no regard to your furniture, for all the good things of Egypt shall be yours.
[Genesis] [45]:[21] And the sons of Israel did so. And Joseph gave them waggons as Pharao the king commanded. He gave them also provisions for the journey.
[Genesis] [45]:[22] And to all he gave two suits of raiment; but to Benjamin he gave three hundred pieces of gold and five distinguished suits of raiment.
[Genesis] [45]:[23] And he sent the like presents to his father together with ten asses which carried some of all the good things of Egypt, and ten mules loaded with bread for his father on the journey.
[Genesis] [45]:[24] And he dismissed them: And as they were departing, he said to them, See that you do not fall out by the way.
[Genesis] [45]:[25] So they went up out of Egypt and came to the land of Chanaan to their father Jacob
[Genesis] [45]:[26] and told him saying. Thy son Joseph is alive and he is the governor of all the land of Egypt. Whereupon Jacob was absorpt in thought, for he could not believe them.
[Genesis] [45]:[27] But when they told him all that Joseph had commanded them, and he saw the waggons which Joseph had sent to carry him, the spirit of Jacob their father revived.
[Genesis] [45]:[28] And Israel said, It is a great thing for me that my son Joseph is still alive. I will go and see him, before I die.
[Genesis] [46]:[1] Then Israel removed with all that he had and went to Well of the Oath, and offered a sacrifice to the God of his father Isaak.
[Genesis] [46]:[2] And God spoke to Israel in a vision of the night and said, Jacob! Jacob! And he said, Here I am.
[Genesis] [46]:[3] And God said to him, I am the God of thy fathers. Fear not to go down into Egypt; for I will make thee there a great nation.
[Genesis] [46]:[4] I indeed will go down with thee into Egypt and I will at last cause thee to come up, when Joseph shall have put his hands on thy eyes.
[Genesis] [46]:[5] Then Jacob arose from Well of the Oath and the sons of Israel put their father, and the baggage and their wives in the waggons which Joseph had sent to carry him;
[Genesis] [46]:[6] and taking their stock and all their effects which they had gotten in the land of Chanaan, Jacob and all his seed with him,
[Genesis] [46]:[7] his sons and his grandsons, his daughters and his granddaughters with him went to Egypt. So he brought all his offspring to Egypt.
[Genesis] [46]:[8] Now these are the names of Israel's children who went down to Egypt with their father Jacob. The first born of Jacob was Reuben,
[Genesis] [46]:[9] and the sons of Reuben, Enoch and Phallus, Asron and Charmi.
[Genesis] [46]:[10] And the sons of Symeon, Jemuel and Jamin and Aod and Achin and Saar and Saul the son of a Chananitish woman.
[Genesis] [46]:[11] And the sons of Levi, Gerson, Kaath and Merari.
[Genesis] [46]:[12] And the sons of Judas were Er and Aunan and Selom and Phares and Zara but Er and Aunan died in the land of Chanaan. And the sons of Phares were Esron and Jemuel.
[Genesis] [46]:[13] And the sons of Issachar were Thola and Phua and Asum and Sambran.
[Genesis] [46]:[14] And the sons of Zabulon, Sered and Allon and Achoel.
[Genesis] [46]:[15] These were the sons of Leia whom she bore to Jacob in Syrian Mesopotamia with Deina his daughter. All the souls, sons and daughters were thirty three.
[Genesis] [46]:[16] And the sons of Gad; Saphon and Aggis and Sannis and Thasoban and Aodeis and Aroedeis and Areeleis.
[Genesis] [46]:[17] And the sons of Aser; Jemna and Jessua and Jeus and Baria with Sara their sister. And the sons of Baria; Chobar and Melchiil.
[Genesis] [46]:[18] These were the children of Zelpha whom Laban gave to his daughter Leia and she bore them to Jacob. Sixteen souls.
[Genesis] [46]:[19] And the children of Rachel the wife of Jacob were Joseph and Benjamin.
[Genesis] [46]:[20] And to Joseph children were born in the land of Egypt, whom Aseneth the daughter of Petephres, the priest of Heliopolis, bore to him, namely, Manasses and Ephraim. And to Manasses, there were sons born, whom his concubine Syra bore to him, namely, Machir. And Machir begot Galaad. And the sons of Ephraim the brother of Manasses were Soutalaam and Taam. And the sons of Soutalaam, Edom.
[Genesis] [46]:[21] And the sons of Benjamin were Bala and Bochor and Asbel. And to Bala there were sons born, namely, Gera and Noeman and Achis and Ros and Mamphim, and Gera begot Arad.
[Genesis] [46]:[22] These, were the children of Rachel, whom she bore to Jacob. All the souls were eighteen.
[Genesis] [46]:[23] And the sons of Dan; Asom.
[Genesis] [46]:[24] And the sons of Nephthaleim; Asiel and Goni and Issaar and Sellem.
[Genesis] [46]:[25] These were the children of Balla whom Laban gave to his daughter Rachel, and she bore them to Jacob. All the souls, seven.
[Genesis] [46]:[26] So all the souls who went with Jacob into Egypt, even all who sprung from him, exclusive of the wives of Jacob's children—All the souls were sixty six.
[Genesis] [46]:[27] And including the children of Joseph who were born to him in the land of Egypt, being nine, all the souls of Jacob's house who came with Jacob into Egypt were seventy five.
[Genesis] [46]:[28] Now he had sent Judas before him to Joseph, that he might meet him at Heroopolis, in the land of Harnesses.
[Genesis] [46]:[29] And Joseph having got ready his chariots, went up to meet his father Israel at Heroopolis. And when he saw him he fell on his neck and wept abundantly.
[Genesis] [46]:[30] And Israel said to Joseph, Now let me die, since I have seen thy face, for thou art still alive.
[Genesis] [46]:[31] Then Joseph said to his brothers, I will go up and inform Pharao, and tell him that my brothers, my father's family who were in the land of Chanaan, are come to me;
[Genesis] [46]:[32] but the men are shepherds; for their occupation hath been to feed cattle, and they have brought their flocks and their herds and all that they have.
[Genesis] [46]:[33] If therefore Pharao send for you and say to you, What is your occupation?
[Genesis] [46]:[34] you will say, We thy servants are feeders of cattle from our youth even to this time, as well as our fathers; that you may dwell in Gesem-Arabia. For every shepherd is an abomination to the Egyptians.
[Genesis] [47]:[1] So Joseph went and told Pharao saying, My father and my brothers with their flocks and their herds and all that belong to them are come from the land of Chanaan; and lo! they are in the land of Gesem.
[Genesis] [47]:[2] And he took of his brothers five men and presented them before Pharao.
[Genesis] [47]:[3] And Pharao said to Joseph's brothers, What is your occupation? and they said to Pharao, Thy servants are shepherds, both we and our fathers.
[Genesis] [47]:[4] Moreover they said to Pharao, We are come to sojourn in this land, because there is no pasture for thy servants' cattle, for the famine hath been great in the land of Chanaan. Now therefore let us sojourn in the land of Gesem. And Pharao said to Joseph, Let them dwell in the land of Gesem; and if thou knowest any men of ability among them, make them overseers of my cattle.
[Genesis] [47]:[5] Now when Jacob and his sons came into Egypt to Joseph and Pharao king of Egypt heard of it he said to Joseph, Thy father and thy brothers are come to thee,
[Genesis] [47]:[6] behold the land of Egypt is before thee, settle thy father and thy brothers in the best of the land.
[Genesis] [47]:[7] And when Joseph introduced his father Jacob and presented him before Pharao and Jacob had blessed Pharao,
[Genesis] [47]:[8] Pharao said to Jacob, How many are the years of the days of thy life?
[Genesis] [47]:[9] And Jacob said to Pharao, The days of the years of my life which I am now sojourning are a hundred and thirty years. Few and evil have been the days of the years of my life. They have not reached to the days of the years of the life of my fathers which they sojourned.
[Genesis] [47]:[10] And when Jacob had blessed Pharao he went out from him.
[Genesis] [47]:[11] And Joseph settled his father and his brothers and gave them a possession in the land of Egypt, in the best of the land of Ramesses, as Pharao had ordered.
[Genesis] [47]:[12] And Joseph supplied his father and his brothers and all his father's household with a certain portion of corn for each person.
[Genesis] [47]:[13] Now there was no food in the land, for the famine prevailed exceedingly; and both the land of Egypt and the land of Chanaan fainted by reason of the famine.
[Genesis] [47]:[14] And Joseph collected all the money which was found in the land of Egypt and in the land of Chanaan for the corn which they bought and which he dealt out to them. And Joseph brought all the money to Pharao's house.
[Genesis] [47]:[15] And when all the money was gone, out of the land of Egypt and out of the land of Chanaan, all the Egyptians came to Joseph and said, Give us bread. Must we die in thy presence because we have no money?
[Genesis] [47]:[16] Thereupon Joseph said to them, Bring your cattle, and I will give you bread for your cattle if your money be gone.
[Genesis] [47]:[17] So they brought their cattle to Joseph, and he gave them bread for their horses and for their flocks and for their herds and for their asses and supplied them with bread that year for all their cattle.
[Genesis] [47]:[18] When that year was ended they came to him the next year and said, Perhaps we may be rejected by our lord: for as our money is gone and our stock and cattle are transferred to thee our lord, there is indeed nothing left us in the presence of our lord, but our persons and our lands.
[Genesis] [47]:[19] That we therefore may not die in thy presence and the land become a waste, purchase us and our land for bread, and we with our lands will be servants to Pharao. Give us seed that we may sow and live and not die. So the land will not become desolate.
[Genesis] [47]:[20] Upon this Joseph purchased all the land of the Egyptians for Pharao. For the Egyptians sold their land to Pharao, because the famine prevailed over them. Thus the land became Pharao's,
[Genesis] [47]:[21] and he reduced the people to a state of vassalage from one border of Egypt to the other,
[Genesis] [47]:[22] save the land of the priests only. This Joseph did not buy; because Pharao had assigned them a gratuitous portion, and they ate the portion which Pharao assigned them. Therefore they did not sell their land.
[Genesis] [47]:[23] Then Joseph said to all the Egyptians, Behold I have this day purchased you and your land for Pharao. Take seed and sow the land.
[Genesis] [47]:[24] And of the products thereof you shall give Pharao the fifth. And the other four parts shall be your own to sow the land, and to be food for you and all your families.
[Genesis] [47]:[25] And they said, Thou hast saved our lives. We have found favour in the sight of our lord, and we will be servants to Pharao.
[Genesis] [47]:[26] So Joseph enjoined upon them the giving a fifth to Pharao as a statute which continueth even to this day in all the land of Egypt, excepting only the land of the priests. That did not belong to Pharao.
[Genesis] [47]:[27] Thus Israel became sojourners in the land of Egypt in the district of Gesem, and they had possessions therein and increased and multiplied exceedingly.
[Genesis] [47]:[28] And Jacob lived in the land of Egypt seventeen years. And all the days of the years of his life were a hundred and forty seven years.
[Genesis] [47]:[29] Now when the time of Israel's death drew nigh he sent for his son Joseph and said to him, If I have found favour in thy sight, put thy hand under my thigh and thou shalt deal kindly and truly with me, that thou wilt not bury me in Egypt;
[Genesis] [47]:[30] but let me sleep with my fathers. Therefore thou shalt carry me out of Egypt and bury me in their sepulchre. And he said I will do as thou hast said.
[Genesis] [47]:[31] And he said, Swear to me. So he sword to him. And Israel bowed down on the head of his staff.
[Genesis] [48]:[1] After this when it was told Joseph, Thy father is sick, he took his two sons Manasses and Ephraim and went to Jacob.
[Genesis] [48]:[2] And when they told Jacob saying, Behold thy son Joseph is coming to thee, Israel strengthened himself and sat upon the bed.
[Genesis] [48]:[3] And Jacob said to Joseph, My God appeared to me at Louza in the land of Chanaan and blessed me
[Genesis] [48]:[4] and said, Behold I will make thee fruitful and multiply thee and make thee a multitude of nations, and I will give thee this land even to thy seed after thee for a perpetual possession.
[Genesis] [48]:[5] Now therefore thy two sons, who have been born to thee in the land of Egypt, before I came to thee into Egypt, are mine. Ephraim and Manasses, like Reuben and Symeon, shall be mine.
[Genesis] [48]:[6] And the issue which thou mayst have after this shall go by the name of their brothers. They shall be enrolled in their inheritances.
[Genesis] [48]:[7] Now when I came out of Syrian Mesopotamia Rachel thy mother died in the land of Chanaan, just as I drew near the race ground of Chabratha on the way to Ephratha. So I buried her in the way of the race ground the same is Bethlehem.
[Genesis] [48]:[8] Then Israel seeing the sons of Joseph said, Who are these with thee?
[Genesis] [48]:[9] And Joseph said to his father, They are my sons, whom God hath given me here. Whereupon Jacob said, Bring them near to me that I may bless them.
[Genesis] [48]:[10] Now Israel's eyes were dim by reason of old age and he could not see clearly. And when he brought them near to him, he kissed them and embraced them.
[Genesis] [48]:[11] And Israel said to Joseph, Lo! was I not deprived of thy presence? yet behold God hath shewn me even thy seed.
[Genesis] [48]:[12] Then Joseph took them from his knees; and when they had made obeisance to him with their face to the ground,
[Genesis] [48]:[13] Joseph taking his two sons, Ephraim in his right hand towards Israel's left and Manasses in his left hand towards Israel's right, brought them near to him.
[Genesis] [48]:[14] But Israel stretching out his right hand laid it on Ephraim's head (now he was the youngest) and his left hand on Manasses' head crossing his hands.
[Genesis] [48]:[15] And he blessed them and said, The God whom my fathers Abraham and Isaak worshipped—the God who hath fed me from my youth to this day—
[Genesis] [48]:[16] the Angel who delivered me from all evils, bless these children! And let them be called by my name and by the name of my fathers Abraham and Isaak; and may they be increased to a great multitude on the earth!
[Genesis] [48]:[17] Now when Joseph saw that his father put his right hand on Ephraim's head, it appeared amiss to him; and he took hold of his father's hand to remove it from Ephraim's head to the head of Manasses.
[Genesis] [48]:[18] And Joseph said to his father, Not so, my father: for this is the first born, put thy right hand on his head.
[Genesis] [48]:[19] But he refused and said, I know; my son; I know. This indeed will be a people and will be exalted. But his younger brother will be greater than he; and his seed will be a multitude of nations.
[Genesis] [48]:[20] And he blessed them that day and said, By you Israel will bless saying, God make thee like Ephraim and Manasses. So he put Ephraim before Manasses.
[Genesis] [48]:[21] Then Israel said to Joseph, Lo I die. But God will be with you and bring you back to the land of your fathers.
[Genesis] [48]:[22] Now to thee exclusively above thy brothers, I give Sikima, which I took out of the hand of the Amorites with my sword and with my bow.
[Genesis] [49]:[1] Then Jacob called his sons and said to them, Assemble that I may tell you what will happen to you in the latter days.
[Genesis] [49]:[2] Assemble and hear me, ye sons of Jacob: Hearken to Israel; hearken to your father.
[Genesis] [49]:[3] Reuben my first born! Thou my strength and the head of my children! Too imperious and too self sufficient!
[Genesis] [49]:[4] In the excess of thy insolence, Thou shouldst not have burst forth like water. For thou wentest up to thy father's bed, Then thou pollutedst the couch to which thou wentest up.
[Genesis] [49]:[5] Symeon and Levi are brothers. They accomplished their iniquitous purpose.
[Genesis] [49]:[6] O my soul, come not into their council; And in their conspiracy let not my passions be engaged. Because in their wrath they slew men, And in their rage ham-strung a bull;
[Genesis] [49]:[7] Cursed be their wrath, for it was headstrong; And their enmity, for it was bitter. I will parcel them out in Jacob And scatter them among Israel.
[Genesis] [49]:[8] Judas! Let thy brethren praise thee: Thy hands will be on the back of thy enemies. The sons of thy father shall bow down to thee.
[Genesis] [49]:[9] Judas is a lion's whelp. From a vigorous stem, my son, thou art sprung. Couching down thou art gone to sleep Like a young lion: Who dare rouse him!
[Genesis] [49]:[10] A chief shall not fail from Judas. Nor a leader from his loins; Till the things reserved for him shall come— Even He, the expectation of nations.
[Genesis] [49]:[11] Binding his colt to a vine And his asses colt to the young vine, He will wash his robe in wine, And his mantle in the blood of the grape.
[Genesis] [49]:[12] His eyes are more exhilarating than wine, And his teeth are whiter than milk.
[Genesis] [49]:[13] Zabulon shall dwell by the sea; And he will be near the haven of ships, And will extend as far as Sidon.
[Genesis] [49]:[14] Issachar desired earnestly what was good, Halting between two choices.
[Genesis] [49]:[15] But seeing the resting place, that it was good, And the land, that it was fertile, He bowed his shoulder to labour And became a husbandman.
[Genesis] [49]:[16] Dan will judge his people And be like a tribe in Israel.
[Genesis] [49]:[17] Let Dan be indeed a serpent in the way, Lying in watch in a path, Biting the horse's heel, That the horseman may fall backwards;
[Genesis] [49]:[18] Waiting continually the salvation of the Lord.
[Genesis] [49]:[19] Gad: Troops of plunderers will plunder him; And he to his utmost, will plunder them.
[Genesis] [49]:[20] Aser. His meat will be fat And he will furnish dainties for princes.
[Genesis] [49]:[21] Nephthaleim is a spreading stock, Super-adding beauty by its product.
[Genesis] [49]:[22] Joseph is a fruitful son. My fruitful son was envied. Abide with me, my younger son, Against whom calumniators brought false accusations.
[Genesis] [49]:[23] Though the masters of bows took aim at him; Yet their bows and their strength were broken, And the sinewy arms of their hands enfeebled By the hand of the mighty One of Jacob.
[Genesis] [49]:[24] Thence he who hath strengthened Israel Is from the God of thy father;
[Genesis] [49]:[25] And My God hath helped thee; and blessed thee With the blessing of heaven from above, And with the blessing of a land having all things, By reason of the blessing of breasts and womb,
[Genesis] [49]:[26] He hath made the blessings of thy father and mother, More durable than the blessings of perpetual mountains, And more permanent than the blessings of everlasting hills. They shall be on the head of Joseph And on the crown of the head of brothers Of whom he was the leader.
[Genesis] [49]:[27] Benjamin is a ravening wolf. In the morning he is still eating, And for the evening he provideth food.
[Genesis] [49]:[28] All these were the twelve sons of Jacob. And these were the words which their father spoke to them when he blessed them, each according to his peculiar blessing.
[Genesis] [49]:[29] And when he had blessed them he said, I am going to be added to my people, and you shall bury me with my fathers,
[Genesis] [49]:[30] in the cave which is in the field of Ephron the Chettite, in the double cave which is over against Mambre in the land of Chanaan—the cave which Abraham bought of Ephron the Chettite for the possession of a burying place.
[Genesis] [49]:[31] There they buried Abraham and Sarah his wife. There they buried Isaak and Rebekka his wife. There Leia was buried—
[Genesis] [49]:[32] in the field namely in the cave in it, which was purchased of the children of Chet.
[Genesis] [49]:[33] And when Jacob had finished this charge to his sons he drew up his feet into the bed and gave up the ghost and was added to his people.
[Genesis] [50]:[1] Then Joseph fell upon his father's face and bewailed him and kissed him.
[Genesis] [50]:[2] And Joseph gave orders to his servants the embalmers to embalm his father. And when the embalmers had embalmed Israel,
[Genesis] [50]:[3] and compleated his forty days; (for this is the time allowed for embalming) the Egyptians mourned for him seventy days.
[Genesis] [50]:[4] And when the days of mourning were over, Joseph spoke to the princes of Pharao saying, If I have found favour in your sight, speak for me to Pharao and say,
[Genesis] [50]:[5] My father adjured me saying, In the tomb which I cut out for myself in the land of Chanaan there thou shalt bury me. Now therefore let me go up and bury my father and I will return again.
[Genesis] [50]:[6] And Pharao said to Joseph, Go up and bury thy father as he adjured thee.
[Genesis] [50]:[7] So Joseph went up to bury his father. And there went up with him all the servants of Pharao and the elders of his house, and all the elders of the land of Egypt,
[Genesis] [50]:[8] and all Joseph's family and his brothers and all his lather's household and his kindred. But their flocks and herds they left in the land of Gesem.
[Genesis] [50]:[9] There went up also with him chariots and horsemen, so that the camp was very large.
[Genesis] [50]:[10] And when they came to the threshing floor of Atad, which is on the bank of the Jordan, they mourned for him with a very great and sore lamentation. And he continued the mourning for his father seven days.
[Genesis] [50]:[11] And when the inhabitants of the land of Chanaan saw the mourning at the threshing floor of Atad, they said, This is a great grief to the Egyptians. For this cause they called its name Grief of Egypt. It is on the bank of the Jordan.
[Genesis] [50]:[12] When his sons had done this for him—
[Genesis] [50]:[13] when they had carried him to the land of Chanaan and buried him in the double cave; the cave which Abraham bought for the possession of a burying place from Ephron the Chettite over against Mambre,
[Genesis] [50]:[14] then Joseph returned to Egypt, he and his brethren and those who went up with him to bury his father.
[Genesis] [50]:[15] Now when Joseph's brothers saw that their father was dead, they said, Perhaps Joseph may bear us a grudge and requite us for all the ills which we have done to him.
[Genesis] [50]:[16] So they came to Joseph and said, Our father before he died adjured us saying,
[Genesis] [50]:[17] Thus shall you say to Joseph. Forgive them their iniquity and their sin for having done thee evil. Now therefore forgive the iniquity of the servants of the God of thy father. And while they were speaking to him, Joseph wept.
[Genesis] [50]:[18] And they came near to him and said, We are thy servants,
[Genesis] [50]:[19] upon which Joseph said to them, Fear not, for I am God's servant.
[Genesis] [50]:[20] You indeed consulted against me for evil: but God determined concerning me for good, to bring to pass as at this day, that much people might be nourished.
[Genesis] [50]:[21] Moreover he said to them, Be not afraid. I will continue to nourish you and your families. So he comforted them, and spoke affectionately to them,
[Genesis] [50]:[22] And Joseph dwelt in Egypt, he and his brothers and all his father's family. And when Joseph had lived a hundred and ten years
[Genesis] [50]:[23] and saw Ephraim's children to the third generation; and the children of Machir the son of Manasses were also dandled on his knees;
[Genesis] [50]:[24] Joseph spoke to his brothers, saying, I die, but God will assuredly visit you and conduct you out of this land into the land which God with an oath gave to our fathers, Abraham, Isaak and Jacob.
[Genesis] [50]:[25] And Joseph adjured the children of Israel saying, In the visitation wherewith God will visit you, you shall carry my bones hence with you.
[Genesis] [50]:[26] So Joseph died being a hundred and ten years old, and when they had embalmed him they put him into a coffin in Egypt.
[Leviticus] [1]:[1] Then the Lord called up Moses and spoke to him out of the tabernacle of the testimony saying,
[Leviticus] [1]:[2] Speak to the children of Israel and thou shalt say to them, When any man among you bringeth gifts to the Lord from your cattle, you shall bring them either from the flocks or the herds.
[Leviticus] [1]:[3] If his gift be a whole burnt offering from the herd, he shall bring a male without blemish to the door of the tabernacle of the testimony. He shall bring it before the Lord for acceptance;
[Leviticus] [1]:[4] and he shall lay his hand on the head of the homage offering to be accepted for him—to make atonement for him.
[Leviticus] [1]:[5] And they shall kill the young bull before the Lord; and the sons of Aaron the priests shall carry the blood and pour it out round about on the altar which is at the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony;
[Leviticus] [1]:[6] and having flayed the whole burnt offering they shall cut it in quarters;
[Leviticus] [1]:[7] and the sons of Aaron the priests shall put the fire on the altar and pile wood on the fire;
[Leviticus] [1]:[8] and the sons of Aaron the priests shall lay the quarters with the head and the suet on the wood which is on the fire on the altar;
[Leviticus] [1]:[9] and they shall wash the entrails and the feet with water, and the priests shall lay them all on the altar. It is an homage gift, a sacrifice, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [1]:[10] And if his gift to the Lord be from the flocks, either from the lambs or the kids for a whole burnt offering, he shall bring a male without blemish
[Leviticus] [1]:[11] and he shall lay his hands on its head; and they shall kill it on the north side of the altar before the Lord: and the sons of Aaron, the priests, shall pour the blood on the altar round about;
[Leviticus] [1]:[12] then they shall cut it in quarters and the priests shall lay these with the head and the suet on the wood which is on the fire on the altar;
[Leviticus] [1]:[13] and they shall wash the entrails and the feet with water; and the priests shall carry them all up and lay them on the altar. It is an homage offering, a sacrifice, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [1]:[14] And if he bring an homage offering from among the fowls as his gift to the Lord; he shall bring his gift either from among the turtle doves or the pigeons;
[Leviticus] [1]:[15] and the priest shall carry it to the altar and wring off the head, and the priest shall lay it on the altar; then he shall squeeze out the blood against the base of the altar;
[Leviticus] [1]:[16] and he shall take away the craw with the feathers and throw it on the east side of the altar into the ash hole,
[Leviticus] [1]:[17] then he shall break it off from the wings but shall not divide it; and the priest shall lay it on the altar, on the wood which is upon the fire. It is an homage offering, a sacrifice, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[1] And when any person shall bring a sacrificial gift to the Lord, his gift shall be fine flour, and he shall pour oil on it and lay frankincense on it.
[Leviticus] [2]:[2] It is a sacrifice; and he shall carry it to the sons of Aaron, the priests; and the priest having taken out a handful of the flour with the oil, and all the frankincense thereof shall lay this memorial of it on the altar. It is a sacrifice, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[3] And the rest of the sacrifice shall be for Aaron and his sons, a most holy portion from the sacrifices of the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[4] And if he bring a gift, a baked sacrifice from the oven, his gift to the Lord shall be of fine flour—he shall bring unleavened loaves tempered with oil or unleavened cakes anointed with oil.
[Leviticus] [2]:[5] And if thy gift be a sacrifice from the pan, it must be of fine flour tempered with oil, without leaven.
[Leviticus] [2]:[6] And having broken it to pieces thou shalt crumble the fragments and pour oil thereon. It is a sacrifice to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[7] And if thy gift be a sacrifice from the hearth, the fine flour must be made up with oil.
[Leviticus] [2]:[8] And he shall bring the sacrifice which he hath prepared of these materials for the Lord and carry it to the priest; and the priest shall carry it near to the altar
[Leviticus] [2]:[9] and shall take from the sacrifice the memorial of it and lay it on the altar. It is an homage offering, a smell of fragrance for the Lord;
[Leviticus] [2]:[10] and the rest of the sacrifice shall be for Aaron and his sons. These are most holy portions from the homage offerings of the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[11] Every sacrifice which you bring to the Lord, you shall prepare without leaven; for you must not bring any kind of leaven, not even honey, as a gift to pay homage to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[12] You may bring them as gifts of first fruits to the Lord; but they shall not be laid on the altar for a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [2]:[13] Every gift of your sacrifice must be seasoned with salt. You shall not withhold the salt of the covenant of the Lord from your sacrifices. With every gift you make, you shall offer salt to the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [2]:[14] And when thou bringest a sacrifice of first fruits to the Lord, it must be young parched grains broken in the mill. This sacrifice of the first fruits thou shalt offer to the Lord,
[Leviticus] [2]:[15] and thou shalt pour oil on it and put frankincense on it. It is a sacrifice.
[Leviticus] [2]:[16] And the priest shall carry up as the memorial of it some of the broken grains with all the frankincense. It is an homage offering to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [3]:[1] And if his gift to the Lord be a sacrifice of thanks giving, and he bring it from the herd, whether it be a male or a female, he shall bring it without blemish before the Lord
[Leviticus] [3]:[2] and shall lay his hands on the head of the gift and kill it before the Lord at the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony. And the sons of Aaron, the priests shall pour the blood on the altar of whole burnt offerings round about:
[Leviticus] [3]:[3] And from the sacrifice of thanksgiving they shall offer up as an homage offering to the Lord the caul which covereth the belly and all the suet on the belly
[Leviticus] [3]:[4] and the two kidneys with the suet on them, and that on the flanks, and with the kidneys he shall take off the lobe of the liver.
[Leviticus] [3]:[5] And the sons of Aaron, the priests shall offer up these on the altar—on the whole burnt offerings which are on the wood on the fire. It is an offering of homage, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [3]:[6] And if his gift be a sacrifice of thanksgiving to the Lord from the flock, whether it be a male or a female, he shall offer it without blemish.
[Leviticus] [3]:[7] If he bring a lamb as his gift he shall bring it before the Lord
[Leviticus] [3]:[8] and lay his hands on the head of his gift and kill it at the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony, and the sons of Aaron the priest shall pour the blood on the altar round about.
[Leviticus] [3]:[9] And from the sacrifice of thanksgiving he shall offer as an offering of homage to the Lord the fat tail and the whole loin which he shall take off with the rump; and having taken off the caul which covereth the inwards and all the fat on the belly
[Leviticus] [3]:[10] and the two kidneys with the suet on them and on the loin and the lobe of the liver with the kidneys
[Leviticus] [3]:[11] the priest shall offer up these on the altar. It is a sweet savour, an offering of homage to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [3]:[12] And if his gift be from the goats, he shall bring it before the Lord
[Leviticus] [3]:[13] and lay his hands on its head, and they shall kill it before the Lord at the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony; and the sons of Aaron, the priests, shall pour out the blood against the altar round about;
[Leviticus] [3]:[14] And from it he shall offer up as an offering of homage to the Lord the caul which covereth the belly and all the fat on the belly
[Leviticus] [3]:[15] and both the kidneys and all the suet on them. That on the loins and the lobe of the liver he shall take off with the kidneys
[Leviticus] [3]:[16] and the priest shall offer them on the altar. It is an offering of homage, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [3]:[17] All the suet for the Lord shall be an ordinance for ever throughout your generations. You shall not eat suet nor blood of any kind.
[Leviticus] [4]:[1] Again the Lord spoke to Moses saying,
[Leviticus] [4]:[2] Speak to the children of Israel and say, When any person sinneth unwittingly before the Lord, and doth any of those things, which by any of the commandments of the Lord he ought not to do—
[Leviticus] [4]:[3] if for instance the chief priest who hath been anointed, sin; on the account of his causing the people to sin, he shall, for the sin which he hath committed, bring a young bull without blemish from the herd before the Lord for his sin offering;
[Leviticus] [4]:[4] and having brought the young bull to the door of the tabernacle of the testimony before the Lord he shall lay his hand on the head of the young bull before the Lord and slay the bull in the presence of the Lord;
[Leviticus] [4]:[5] And the priest who is anointed, he whose hands have been consecrated, having taken some of the blood of the bull shall carry it into the tabernacle of the testimony.
[Leviticus] [4]:[6] And this priest shall dip his finger in the blood and sprinkle some of the blood seven times before the Lord, before the holy veil;
[Leviticus] [4]:[7] and he shall put some of the blood of the bull on the horns of the altar of compound incense, which is before the Lord in the tabernacle of the testimony. Now all the blood of the bull he is to pour out at the base of the altar of whole burnt offerings which is at the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony.
[Leviticus] [4]:[8] And with regard to all the suet of the bull for the sin offering, he shall strip off the caul, which covereth the entrails and all the fat on the entrails
[Leviticus] [4]:[9] and the two kidneys with the suet on them—that which is on the loins and the lobe which is on the liver he shall strip off with the two kidneys in the same manner
[Leviticus] [4]:[10] as it is to be taken from the young bull of the sacrifice of thanksgiving; and the priest shall offer up these upon the altar of homage offerings:
[Leviticus] [4]:[11] but the skin of the bull and all his flesh with the head and the limbs and the belly and the dung
[Leviticus] [4]:[12] even the whole bull they shall carry forth without the camp to a clean place where the ashes are emptied out, and burn him on wood with fire. On the out pourings of the ashes he shall be burned.
[Leviticus] [4]:[13] And if the whole congregation of Israel be unwittingly ignorant and a commandment hath escaped the notice of the whole congregation, and they shall do an act, which by any of the commandments of the Lord ought not to be done and shall transgress:
[Leviticus] [4]:[14] when the sin which they have thereby committed is made known to them, the congregation shall bring a young bull from the herd without blemish for their sin offering; and when they have brought him to the doors of the tabernacle of the testimony,
