[Leviticus] [18]:[7] the nakedness of thy father, that is the nakedness of thy mother, thou shalt not uncover; for she is thy mother, thou shalt not uncover her nakedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[8] The nakedness of thy father's wife thou shalt not uncover; for it is thy father's nakedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[9] The nakedness of thy sister by the same father or by the same mother, whether born at home or born abroad, their nakedness thou shalt not uncover.
[Leviticus] [18]:[10] The nakedness of thy son's daughter, or of thy daughter's daughter, their nakedness thou shalt not uncover, for it is thy own nakedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[11] The nakedness of the daughter of thy father's wife thou shalt not uncover; being of the same father she is thy sister: thou shalt not uncover her nakedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[12] The nakedness of thy father's sister thou shalt not uncover, for she is thy father's near kinswoman.
[Leviticus] [18]:[13] The nakedness of thy mother's sister thou shalt not uncover; for she is thy mother's near kinswoman.
[Leviticus] [18]:[14] The nakedness of thy father's brother thou shalt not uncover; thou shalt not go in unto his wife for she is thy near relation.
[Leviticus] [18]:[15] The nakedness of thy daughter in law thou shalt not uncover; for she is thy son's wife, thou shalt not uncover her nakedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[16] The nakedness of thy brother's wife thou shalt not uncover, it is the nakedness of thy brother.
[Leviticus] [18]:[17] The nakedness of a wife and of her daughter thou shalt not uncover. Thou shalt not take thy son's daughter, nor thy daughter's daughter to uncover their nakedness; for they are thy near kinswomen. It is wickedness.
[Leviticus] [18]:[18] In addition to a sister thou shalt not take to wife a rival sister to uncover her nakedness while her sister is still living.
[Leviticus] [18]:[19] And thou shalt not approach to a wife during the time of her separation for uncleanness, to uncover her nakedness:
[Leviticus] [18]:[20] and with thy neighbour's wife thou shall not lie carnally to defile thyself with her.
[Leviticus] [18]:[21] Thou shalt not give any of thy seed to serve an Archon; nor shalt thou profane the holy name I the Lord.
[Leviticus] [18]:[22] Thou shalt not lie with a man as with a woman; for it is an abomination.
[Leviticus] [18]:[23] Nor shalt thou lie with any beast to pollute thyself with it; nor shall a woman be exposed to any beast to be pressed by it; for it is horrible.
[Leviticus] [18]:[24] Be not polluted with any such things; for with all such things have those nations been polluted, which I drive out before you,
[Leviticus] [18]:[25] and the land hath been polluted, and for its sake I have retributed to them their iniquity, and the land is become a mortal enemy to them who dwell therein.
[Leviticus] [18]:[26] You therefore shall keep all my institutions and all my ordinances; and none of you, neither the native, nor the proselyte who dwelleth among you,
[Leviticus] [18]:[27] shall commit any of these abominations, which the men of the country who were before you have committed, and by which the land hath been polluted;
[Leviticus] [18]:[28] that the land may not become a mortal enemy to you when you pollute it; as it hath been to those nations which were before you.
[Leviticus] [18]:[29] For whoever shall commit any of these abominations, the persons who shall commit them shall be cut off from among their people
[Leviticus] [18]:[30] Therefore you shall keep my ordinances that you may not practise any of these abominable customs, which were practised before you. You shall not pollute yourselves with them; for I the Lord am your God.
[Leviticus] [19]:[1] Again the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [19]:[2] Speak to the congregation of the children of Israel; and thon shalt say unto them, You shall be holy, for I the Lord your God am holy.
[Leviticus] [19]:[3] Let every one reverence his father and his mother. And you shall keep my sabbaths. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[4] you shall not follow idols, nor make for yourselves molten gods. I the Lord am your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[5] therefore when you offer a sacrifice of thanksgiving to the Lord, you shall offer it in an acceptable manner.
[Leviticus] [19]:[6] On the day you offer such a sacrifice it shall be eaten; or on the next day; and if any of it be left till the third day, it shall be burned with fire.
[Leviticus] [19]:[7] And if it be eaten on the third day, it is as if it had not been offered.
[Leviticus] [19]:[8] It will not be accepted. He who eateth it will contract guilt, because he hath profaned the holy things of the Lord. The persons therefore who eat it shall be cut off from among their people.
[Leviticus] [19]:[9] And when you gather in the harvest of your land, you shall not make a com pleat ingathering of the harvest of your field, nor gather up the gleanings of thy harvest;
[Leviticus] [19]:[10] nor shalt thou go over thy vineyard a second time, nor gather the straggling clusters of thy vineyard. Thou shalt leave them for the poor, and for the stranger. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[11] you shall not steal; you shall not lie; nor shall any one accuse his brother for trifles,
[Leviticus] [19]:[12] you shall not swear by my name to a falsehood; nor profane the holy name of your God. I am the Lord your God,
[Leviticus] [19]:[13] thou shalt nut injure thy neighbour, nor commit rapine; nor shall the wages of a hireling remain with thee till the morning.
[Leviticus] [19]:[14] Thou shalt not curse the deaf, nor lay a stumbling block before the blind, but shalt fear the Lord thy God. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[15] you shall not do an unjust thing in the execution of justice: thou shalt not respect the face of the poor, nor revere the face of the mighty. With righteousness thou shalt judge thy neighbour.
[Leviticus] [19]:[16] Thou shalt not go about with deceit among thy nation, nor set thyself against the blood of thy neighbour. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[17] thou shalt not hate thy brother in thy heart. With rebuke thou shalt correct thy neighbour, and not contract guilt on his account;
[Leviticus] [19]:[18] but let not thy hand take vengeance. Thou shalt not bear a grudge against the children of thy people; but thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself. I am the Lord;
[Leviticus] [19]:[19] you shall keep my law. Thou shalt not let the cattle gender with one of another kind, nor plant thy vineyard with different sorts of seed; nor put on unscoured, a woven garment made of flax and wool.
[Leviticus] [19]:[20] If any man lie carnally with a woman, and she be a servant betrothed to a man, and hath not been redeemed, nor had her freedom granted her, they shall be chastised, but shall not be put to death, because she was not set free.
[Leviticus] [19]:[21] And for his trespass against the Lord, he shall bring to the door of the tabernacle of the testimony, the ram of a trespass offering;
[Leviticus] [19]:[22] and the priest shall make atonement for him with the ram of the trespass offering before the Lord for the sin which he hath committed; and the sin which he hath committed shall be forgiven him.
[Leviticus] [19]:[23] Now when you come into the land which the Lord your God giveth you, you may plant all sorts of trees for food; but you must purge away their impurity. The fruit thereof shall be three years unclean to you, and shall not be eaten:
[Leviticus] [19]:[24] and in the fourth year all the fruit thereof shall be hallowed, to praise the Lord;
[Leviticus] [19]:[25] and in the fifth year, you may eat the fruit. The products thereof will be an additional supply to you. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[26] you must not eat on the mountains, nor practise divination, nor augury by birds.
[Leviticus] [19]:[27] You shall not make a rounding of the hair of your head, nor disfigure the appearance of your beard.
[Leviticus] [19]:[28] You shall not make gashes in your body for the dead, nor any fixed marks on you. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[29] thou shalt not prostitute thy daughter to cause her to commit whoredom, that the country may not fall into whoredom; and that the land may not be filled with iniquity.
[Leviticus] [19]:[30] You shall keep my sabbaths, and reverence my holy things. I am the Lord your God:
[Leviticus] [19]:[31] you shall not go after belly speakers, nor attach yourselves to sorcerers to be polluted by them. I am the Lord your God:
[Leviticus] [19]:[32] you shall rise up before the hoary head, and honour the face of an elder, and fear thy God. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[33] if any stranger come among you in your land, you shall not afflict him.
[Leviticus] [19]:[34] The stranger who cometh to you, shall be as one born among you; and thou shalt love him as thyself; for you were strangers in the land of Egypt. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [19]:[35] you shall not do injustice in the exercise of judgment, nor in measures, nor in weights, nor in balances.
[Leviticus] [19]:[36] You shall have among you just balances, just weights and a just measure. I am the Lord your God who brought you out of the land of Egypt,
[Leviticus] [19]:[37] therefore you shall keep all my law and all my statutes and do them. I am the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [20]:[1] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [20]:[2] Thou shalt also say to the children of Israel, If any of the children of Israel, or of those who have become proselytes among Israel, shall give any of his seed to an Archon let him be put to death. The people of the land shall stone him with stones:
[Leviticus] [20]:[3] and as for me I will myself set my face against that man, and destroy him from among his people, because he hath given of his seed to an Archon, to pollute my holy things, and to profane the name of those who have been hallowed to me.
[Leviticus] [20]:[4] And though those born in the land may, upon seeing him, connive at that man when he giveth of his seed to an Archon, and not kill him,
[Leviticus] [20]:[5] I will indeed set my face against that man, and against his family, and destroy him, and all who encourage him, to go a whoring after the Archons, from among their people.
[Leviticus] [20]:[6] And with respect to the person who shall follow belly speakers and sorcerers, so as to go a whoring after them, I will set my face against that person, and destroy him from among his people.
[Leviticus] [20]:[7] You shall therefore be holy, because I the Lord your God am holy.
[Leviticus] [20]:[8] And you shall keep my statutes and do them. It is I the Lord who hallow you.
[Leviticus] [20]:[9] Be the man who he may who curseth his father or his mother, let him be put to death. He hath cursed his father or his mother, he shall be held guilty.
[Leviticus] [20]:[10] Whosoever committeth adultery with the wife of a man who is head of a family, or who shall commit adultery with the wife of his neighbour, let both the adulterer and the adulteress be put to death.
[Leviticus] [20]:[11] And if any one lie with his father's wife he hath uncovered his father's nakedness, let them be put to death; they are both guilty.
[Leviticus] [20]:[12] And if any man lie with his daughter in law, let them both be put to death; for they have committed wickedness, and are guilty.
[Leviticus] [20]:[13] And if any man lie with man as with woman, they have both committed an abomination; let them be put to death. They are guilty.
[Leviticus] [20]:[14] And if any man take to wife a mother and a daughter it is a violation of law, they shall burn him and them with fire that there may be no transgressing of law among you.
[Leviticus] [20]:[15] And whoever shall lie with a beast, he shall be put to death, and you shall kill the beast.
[Leviticus] [20]:[16] And if any woman approach to any beast to be pressed under it, you shall kill the woman and the beast. Let them be put to death. They are guilty.
[Leviticus] [20]:[17] Whoever shall take his sister by the same father, or the same mother, and see her nakedness, and she see his; it is a disgraceful act. They shall be excommunicated in the presence of the children of their family. He hath uncovered the nakedness of his sister. They shall bear their sin.
[Leviticus] [20]:[18] And if any man lie with his wife during her infirmity, and uncover her nakedness, he hath uncovered her fountain, and she hath discovered her menstrual efflux; they shall both be cut off from their family.
[Leviticus] [20]:[19] Thou shalt not uncover the nakedness of thy father's sister or thy mother's sister; for when one uncovereth his near of kin they shall bear their sin.
[Leviticus] [20]:[20] Whoever hath lain with his near of kin, he hath uncovered the nakedness of his near relation, they shall die childless.
[Leviticus] [20]:[21] If a man take his brother's wife, it is an act of uncleanness. He hath uncovered the nakedness of his brother; they shall die childless.
[Leviticus] [20]:[22] You shall keep all my statutes and my judgments and do them, that the land to which I am leading you to dwell therein, may not become your mortal enemy.
[Leviticus] [20]:[23] You must not walk in the customs of the nations which I drive out from before you. Because they did all these things, therefore I held them in abhorrence,
[Leviticus] [20]:[24] and said to you, You shall inherit their land, and I will give it to you for a possession—a land flowing with milk and honey. I am the Lord your God, who have separated you from all the nations,
[Leviticus] [20]:[25] you shall therefore keep yourselves separate by making a distinction between the clean beasts, and the beasts which are unclean; and between the clean birds, and the birds which are unclean; and you shall not pollute yourselves with the beasts, or the birds, or any of the reptiles of the earth which I have distinguished for you, by uncleanness,
[Leviticus] [20]:[26] and you shall be consecrated to me. Because I the Lord your God am the Holy One, who hath separated you from all the nations, that you should be mine,
[Leviticus] [20]:[27] therefore the man, or the woman, among you, who hath become a belly speaker, or a sorcerer, shall be put to death. Stone them with stones. They are guilty.
[Leviticus] [21]:[1] Again the Lord spoke to Moses, saying, Speak to the priests, the sons of Aaron, and thou shalt say unto them, They must not defile themselves for the dead in their family,
[Leviticus] [21]:[2] except only for their nearest of kin. For a father, or a mother, or for sons, or daughters, or for a brother,
[Leviticus] [21]:[3] and for a virgin sister who dwelleth with him, and who hath not been betrothed to a husband; for these he may defile himself.
[Leviticus] [21]:[4] He must not defile him self unexpectedly among his people, to occasion their defilement.
[Leviticus] [21]:[5] You shall not shave the head to make it bald, for the dead. Nor shall they shave their beard, nor make gashes in their flesh.
[Leviticus] [21]:[6] They shall be consecrated to their God, and shall not profane the name of their God, for they are to offer up the sacrifices of the Lord—the gifts to their God; therefore they shall be holy.
[Leviticus] [21]:[7] They shall not take to wife a harlot, nor a woman who hath been defiled, nor a woman divorced from her husband. Because he is consecrated to the Lord his God,
[Leviticus] [21]:[8] therefore thou shalt hallow him. He offereth up the gifts of the Lord your God, he shall be hallowed. Because I the Lord am the holy one who halloweth them,
[Leviticus] [21]:[9] therefore if the daughter of a priest profane herself by whoredom, she profaneth the name of her father, she shall be burned with fire.
[Leviticus] [21]:[10] And with regard to the priest, who is great above his brethren, the oil of the anointed one having been poured on his head, and he having been consecrated to put on the garments, he must not take the tiara from his head, nor rend his garments,
[Leviticus] [21]:[11] nor go near any dead person, nor defile himself for his father, nor for his mother,
[Leviticus] [21]:[12] nor go out of the holy places, nor defile the sanctuary of his God; because the holy oil, the anointing of his God, is upon him. I am the Lord.
[Leviticus] [21]:[13] He shall take to wife a virgin of his own family.
[Leviticus] [21]:[14] A widow, or a woman divorced or defiled, or a harlot, these he shall not take. He shall take none to wife, but a virgin of his own tribe;
[Leviticus] [21]:[15] and he shall not profane his seed among his people. It is I the Lord who hallow him.
[Leviticus] [21]:[16] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [21]:[17] Say to Aaron, be the man who he may of thy family, throughout your generations, who hath any blemish about him, he shall not come near to present the gifts of his God.
[Leviticus] [21]:[18] No man who hath any blemish, who is blind, or lame, or hath a hare lip, or a slit ear;
[Leviticus] [21]:[19] nor any man who hath a contortion in the hand, or a splay foot,
[Leviticus] [21]:[20] or who is hump backed, or dim sighted, or blear eyed, nor any man who hath the scurvy, or the itch, or a rupture—
[Leviticus] [21]:[21] No person of the seed of Aaron the priest, who hath any blemish about him, shall come near to offer the sacrifices of thy God. Because he hath a blemish he shall not come near to offer the gifts of God.
[Leviticus] [21]:[22] With regard to the gifts of God, even the most holy, of these he may eat;
[Leviticus] [21]:[23] but he shall not approach to the veil, nor come near to the altar, because he hath a blemish. And he shall not profane the sanctuary of his God, because I am the Lord who halloweth them.
[Leviticus] [21]:[24] And when Moses had spoken to Aaron and his sons, and to all the children of Israel,
[Leviticus] [22]:[1] then the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [22]:[2] Speak to Aaron and his sons and let them take heed not to profane my holy name, in respect to any of the holy things of the children of Israel, which they dedicate to me. I am the Lord;
[Leviticus] [22]:[3] say unto them, What man soever of your seed, throughout your generations, shall come near to the holy things which the children of Israel may dedicate to the Lord, and have any uncleanness upon him, that person shall be cut off from me. I am the Lord your God;
[Leviticus] [22]:[4] though a man be of the seed of Aaron the priest, yet if he be a leper, or hath a gonorrhea, he shall not eat of the holy things until he be cleansed. And with respect to him who toucheth any uncleanness of the dead, or the man from whom seminal matter may proceed;
[Leviticus] [22]:[5] or who may touch any unclean reptile which may defile him; or any man, by whom he may be defiled, whatever his uncleanness may be;
[Leviticus] [22]:[6] the person who shall touch such things shall be unclean until evening. He shall not eat of the holy things unless he wash his body with water,
[Leviticus] [22]:[7] and the sun be set. When he shall be clean, then he may eat of the holy things; for it is his food.
[Leviticus] [22]:[8] What died of itself, or is torn by wild beasts, he shall not eat to pollute himself therewith. I am the Lord,
[Leviticus] [22]:[9] therefore they shall keep my statutes, that they may not, on the account of those things, contract guilt, and die because of them, when they profane them. I am the Lord, the God who halloweth them,
[Leviticus] [22]:[10] therefore none of another family shall eat the holy things, nor shall he, who sojourneth with a priest, or who is hired by him, eat of them.
[Leviticus] [22]:[11] But if the priest shall have a person bought with money, this person may eat of his food. And they who are born in his family may eat of his food.
[Leviticus] [22]:[12] But if the daughter of a priest be married to a man of another family, she shall not eat of the dedications of the sanctuary.
[Leviticus] [22]:[13] But if the daughter of a priest become a widow, or be divorced, and have no issue, she may return to her father's house as in her youth, and eat of her father's food: But none of another family shall eat thereof.
[Leviticus] [22]:[14] And whoever shall eat of the holy things through inadvertence, he shall add a fifth to it, and give the holy thing to the priest.
[Leviticus] [22]:[15] Thus the priests will not profane the holy things of the children of Israel, which they set apart for the Lord,
[Leviticus] [22]:[16] nor bring upon themselves the guilt of a trespass, when they eat their holy things, for I am the Lord who hallow them.
[Leviticus] [22]:[17] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [22]:[18] Speak to Aaron and his sons, and to the whole congregation of Israel, and thou shalt say unto them, Be the man who he may of the children of Israel, or of the proselytes who dwell among them in Israel, who shall bring his gifts according to any vow they may have made, or according to any determination they may have come to, whatever they bring for a whole burnt offering to God,
[Leviticus] [22]:[19] must, in order to be accepted for you, be males without blemish, from the herd, or from the sheep, or from the goats.
[Leviticus] [22]:[20] What hath any blemish in it they shall not bring to the Lord; for it will not be accepted for you.
[Leviticus] [22]:[21] And whoever shall bring a sacrifice of thanksgiving to the Lord, either in discharge of a vow, or as a free will offering, or at your festivals, from the herd or from the flock, it must, in order to its being accepted, be without blemish. There must be no blemish in it.
[Leviticus] [22]:[22] That which is blind, or bruised, or which hath its tongue cut, or hath been eaten by ants, or is infected with scurvy or mange—these they shall not bring to the Lord, nor shall you give any such for an offering of homage on the altar to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [22]:[23] A young bull, or a sheep which hath its ear cropped, or its tail cut, these thou mayst slaughter for thyself; but in discharge of thy vow they will not be accepted.
[Leviticus] [22]:[24] What are castrated by crushing, bruising, cutting or extracting—these thou shalt not bring to the Lord, nor shall you make an offering of them in your land,
[Leviticus] [22]:[25] nor from the hand of a stranger bring any such forward as gifts for your God. Because there is a defect in them—a blemish in them; they will not be accepted for you.
[Leviticus] [22]:[26] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [22]:[27] With respect to a young bull, or a sheep, or a goat, when it is brought forth, it must be seven days under its dam, and on the eighth day, and thenceforward, it will be accepted as a gift for an homage offering to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [22]:[28] But whether it be a young bull, or a lamb, thou must not kill the dam and the young on the same day.
[Leviticus] [22]:[29] And if thou wilt offer a sacrifice—a vow of joy, to the Lord, in order that you may offer it acceptably,
[Leviticus] [22]:[30] it must be eaten on the same day. You shall not leave any of the flesh of it till the next morning. I am the Lord,
[Leviticus] [22]:[31] therefore you shall keep these my commands, and do them;
[Leviticus] [22]:[32] And you shall not profane the name of the Holy One; so shall I be hallowed in the midst of the children of Israel. I am the Lord,
[Leviticus] [22]:[33] who am hallowing you, who am bringing you out of the land of Egypt, to be your God. I am the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[1] Again the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [23]:[2] Speak to the children of Israel, and thou shalt say unto them, With respect to the festivals of the Lord, which you shall proclaim holy, set days,— these are my festivals.
[Leviticus] [23]:[3] Six days thou shalt perform works, but on the seventh day are sabbaths, a solemn rest dedicated to the Lord, thou shalt not do any work. Sabbaths are for the Lord in all your dwellings.
[Leviticus] [23]:[4] These are the festivals for the Lord—holy, set days, which you shall proclaim in their set times.
[Leviticus] [23]:[5] In the first month, on the fourteenth day of the month, between the two evenings, is the passover for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[6] And on the fifteenth day of this month beginneth the festival of unleavened bread for the Lord. Seven days you shall eat unleavened bread.
[Leviticus] [23]:[7] Now the first day shall be a holy, set day for you. You shall do no sacrificial service,
[Leviticus] [23]:[8] but offer whole burnt offerings to the Lord seven days, and the seventh day shall be a holy set day for you. You shall do no sacrificial service.
[Leviticus] [23]:[9] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [23]:[10] Speak to the children of Israel, and thou shalt say unto them, When you are come into the land which I give you, and are about to reap the harvest thereof, you shall bring a sheaf, as the first fruits of your harvest, to the priest,
[Leviticus] [23]:[11] and he shall offer up the sheaf before the Lord, to be accepted for you— On the morrow after the first day, the priest shall offer this up.
[Leviticus] [23]:[12] And on the day when you bring the sheaf, you shall prepare a lamb without blemish of the first year, for a whole burnt offering for the Lord,
[Leviticus] [23]:[13] and for the sacrifice thereof, two tenths of fine flour, made up with oil for the Lord, a smell of fragrance for the Lord—and for the libation thereof, the fourth of an hin of wine.
[Leviticus] [23]:[14] You shall not eat bread, nor parched grains, of the new corn, till this day—until you have offered these gifts to your God. It is an everlasting statute for your generations, in all your dwellings,
[Leviticus] [23]:[15] That from the morrow of these sabbaths—from the day on which you offer up the dedicated sheaf, you shall reckon for yourselves seven whole weeks,
[Leviticus] [23]:[16] even till the morrow after the last week, you shall reckon fifty days, and then offer a sacrifice of new corn to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[17] You shall bring from your dwelling two loaves, as loaves of dedication. They shall consist of two tenths of fine flour. Being of the first fruits for the Lord,
[Leviticus] [23]:[18] they shall be baked with leaven. And with the loaves you shall present seven lambs of the first year, without blemish, and one young bull from the herd, and two rams without blemish; and they shall be a whole burnt offering for the Lord; and their sacrifices, and their libations, shall be a sacrifice, a smell of fragrance for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[19] They shall also prepare one kid of the goats for a sin offering, and two lambs of the first year, for a sacrifice of thanksgiving, with the loaves of the first fruits.
[Leviticus] [23]:[20] And the priest shall dedicate these, with the loaves of the first fruits, as a dedication before the Lord, with the two lambs. They shall be dedicated to the Lord; they shall be for the priest who offereth them.
[Leviticus] [23]:[21] And you shall call this day, a set day. It shall be hallowed for you. On it you shall do no sacrificial work. It is an everlasting statute for your generations in all your dwellings.
[Leviticus] [23]:[22] And when you reap the harvest of your land, you shall not make a clean riddance of the harvest of thy field when thou reapest, nor shalt thou gather up the gleanings of thy harvest. Thou shalt leave them for the poor, and for the stranger. I am the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [23]:[23] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [23]:[24] Speak to the children of Israel, and say, In the seventh month, on the first day of the month, there shall be a rest for you—a memorial of trumpets.
[Leviticus] [23]:[25] It shall be a holy set day for you. You shall not do any sacrificial service, but offer a whole burnt offering to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[26] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [23]:[27] On the tenth day of the seventh month is the day of Atonement. It shall be a holy set day for you; and you shall humble your souls, and offer a whole burnt offering to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[28] On this day you shall not do any kind of work: for it is the day of Atonement for you, to make atonement for you before the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [23]:[29] Every soul who shall not humble himself on that day, shall be cut off from among his people.
[Leviticus] [23]:[30] And every person who shall do any work on that day, shall be destroyed from among the people.
[Leviticus] [23]:[31] You shall not do any kind of work. It is an everlasting statute for your generations in all your dwellings.
[Leviticus] [23]:[32] They shall be sabbaths of sabbaths for you, that you may humble your souls. From the ninth of the month, from evening till evening, you shall keep these sabbaths of yours.
[Leviticus] [23]:[33] Moreover the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [23]:[34] Speak to the children of Israel, and say, On the fifteenth of this seventh month shall commence the festival of Tabernacles, seven days for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[35] The first day shall be proclaimed holy. You shall do no sacrificial work;
[Leviticus] [23]:[36] seven days you shall offer whole burnt offerings to the Lord, and the eighth day shall be proclaimed holy for you, and you shall offer whole burnt offerings to the Lord. It is the exit. You shall not do any sacrificial service.
[Leviticus] [23]:[37] These are the festivals for the Lord, which you shall proclaim holy, in order that you may offer homage offerings to the Lord—whole burnt offerings, with their sacrifices and their libations—that allotted for each day on its day,
[Leviticus] [23]:[38] over and above the sabbaths of the Lord, and over and above your gifts, even besides all your vows, and besides your free will offerings, which you may give to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [23]:[39] Now on the fifteenth day of this seventh month, when you have finished the ingathering of the products of the land, you shall begin this festival of seven days for the Lord. On the first day there shall be a rest; and on the eighth day, a rest.
[Leviticus] [23]:[40] And on the first day you shall take the ripe fruit of trees, and branches of palm trees, and bushy boughs of trees and willows, and branches of the willow of the brook, to rejoice before the Lord your God seven days of the year.
[Leviticus] [23]:[41] It shall be an everlasting statute for your generations. In the seventh month you shall celebrate this festival.
[Leviticus] [23]:[42] You shall dwell in booths seven days. All that are Israelites born, shall dwell in booths,
[Leviticus] [23]:[43] that your posterity may see that I caused the children of Israel to dwell in tents when I brought them out of the land of Egypt. I am the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [23]:[44] And when Moses had mentioned these festivals of the Lord to the Israelites,
[Leviticus] [24]:[1] the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [24]:[2] Give orders to the children of Israel, and let them bring the oil, pure expressed oil for light, that a lamp may burn continually,
[Leviticus] [24]:[3] without the veil, in the tabernacle of the testimony. And Aaron and his sons shall keep it burning, from evening till morning, continually, before the Lord. It is an everlasting statute for your generations.
[Leviticus] [24]:[4] You shall keep the lamps burning on the pure candlestick before the Lord, until the morning.
[Leviticus] [24]:[5] And you shall take fine flour, and make it into twelve loaves, every loaf shall consist of two tenths;
[Leviticus] [24]:[6] and you shall lay them in two piles, six loaves in each pile, on the pure table before the Lord.
[Leviticus] [24]:[7] And on each pile you shall lay pure frankincense, and salt. They shall be set before the Lord for loaves of remembrance.
[Leviticus] [24]:[8] On the day of the sabbaths they shall be set continually before the Lord, in the sight of the children of Israel, as an everlasting covenant;
[Leviticus] [24]:[9] and they shall be for Aaron and his sons. And they shall eat them in a holy place; for they are most holy. This shall be their peculiar due, for ever, from those things sacrificed to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [24]:[10] Now there had come out among the Israelites a son of an Israelitish woman, but his father was an Egyptian. And this son of the Israelitish woman, and a man who was an Israelite, quarrelled in the camp.
[Leviticus] [24]:[11] And the son of the Israelitish woman having named the name, cursed it. Upon which they brought him before Moses. Now his mother's name was Salomith, she was a daughter of Dabri, of the tribe of Dan.
[Leviticus] [24]:[12] And when they had committed him to custody, to pass sentence on him by the decree of the Lord,
[Leviticus] [24]:[13] the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [24]:[14] Lead out him who cursed, without the camp, and all who heard shall lay their hands on his head, and all the congregation shall stone him.
[Leviticus] [24]:[15] Then speak to the children of Israel, and thon shalt say to them, Be the man who he may who shall curse a god, he shall contract guilt;
[Leviticus] [24]:[16] but let him who nameth the name of Lord be put to death. Let the whole congregation of Israel stone him with stones; whether he be a proselyte, or born among them, when he nameth the name of the Lord, let him die.
[Leviticus] [24]:[17] And whosoever smiteth another so that he die, let him be put to death.
[Leviticus] [24]:[18] And whosoever shall smite a beast, so that it die, let him make compensation, beast for beast.
[Leviticus] [24]:[19] And whosoever shall cause a blemish in his neighbour; as he hath done to him, so shall it be done to himself;
[Leviticus] [24]:[20] bruise for bruise; eye for eye; tooth for tooth; according to the blemish he hath given the man, so shall it be returned to him.
[Leviticus] [24]:[21] Whosoever shall smite a man so that he die, let him be put to death,
[Leviticus] [24]:[22] shall be one and the same decision for the proselyte, and for the neighbour; for I am the Lord your God.
[Leviticus] [24]:[23] Thereupon Moses spoke to the children of Israel, and they brought forth him who had cursed, without the camp, and stoned him with stones. And when the children of Israel had done as the Lord commanded Moses,
[Leviticus] [25]:[1] The Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [25]:[2] Speak to the children of Israel, and thou shalt say unto them, When you are come into the land which I give you, the land which I give you must rest sabbaths for the Lord.
[Leviticus] [25]:[3] Six years thou shalt sow thy field, and six years thou shalt prune thy vineyard, and gather the fruit thereof.
[Leviticus] [25]:[4] But in the seventh year there shall be sabbaths—there shall be a rest for the land—sabbaths for the Lord. Thou shalt neither sow thy field nor prune thy vineyard,
[Leviticus] [25]:[5] nor reap the spontaneous productions of thy field, nor gather the grapes of thy dedication. It shall be a year of rest for the land.
[Leviticus] [25]:[6] And the sabbaths of the land shall be food for thee, and for thy man servant, and for thy maid servant, and for thy hired servant, and for the stranger who sojourneth with thee;
[Leviticus] [25]:[7] and for thy cattle, and the wild beasts in thy land, shall the whole product thereof be for food.
[Leviticus] [25]:[8] Moreover thou shalt reckon for thyself seven rests of years, seven times seven years, and these forty nine years, shall be the seven weeks of years;
[Leviticus] [25]:[9] and you shall make proclamation with the sound of a trumpet, throughout all your land, in the seventh month, and tenth day of the month—On the day of the atonement, yon shall make proclamation with a trumpet, throughout all your land,
[Leviticus] [25]:[10] and dedicate the fiftieth year, and proclaim liberty in the land, to all the inhabitants thereof. And this shall be a year of rest, a Jubilee for you; and every one shall return to his possession, and every one shall go to his family.
[Leviticus] [25]:[11] This jubilee, this fiftieth year, shall be for you a general release, you shall not sow, nor shall you reap the spontaneous productions thereof, nor gather the vintage which is dedicated.
[Leviticus] [25]:[12] Because it is a jubilee, it shall be holy to you. From the fields you may eat the productions thereof.
[Leviticus] [25]:[13] In this year of release, proclaimed by the sound of trumpets, every one shall return to his possession.
[Leviticus] [25]:[14] Whether therefore thou sellest to thy neighbour, or purchasest of thy neighbour, let no man take advantage of his neighbour.
[Leviticus] [25]:[15] According to the number of years after the jubilee, thou shalt purchase of thy neighbour; and according to the number of years of crops, he shall sell to thee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[16] In proportion to the greater number of years, thou shalt rate his possession higher, and according to the fewness of the years, thou shalt rate it lower. For according to the number of crops, so shall he sell to thee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[17] Let no man take advantage of his neighbour. Thou shalt fear the Lord thy God. I am the Lord your God,
[Leviticus] [25]:[18] therefore you shall practise all my rules of rectitude, and all my judgments, and keep and do them; so shall you dwell securely in the land,
[Leviticus] [25]:[19] and the land shall yield its productions; and you shall eat plentifully, and dwell securely therein.
[Leviticus] [25]:[20] Now if you should say, What shall we eat in this seventh year, if we neither sow nor gather our produce?
[Leviticus] [25]:[21] I indeed will send my blessing for you, in the sixth year, and it will make the products thereof sufficient for the three years.
[Leviticus] [25]:[22] Though you are to sow in the eighth year, yet on account of the produce, you shall eat the old, even to the ninth year, even till the products of that come in, you shall eat of the old store.
[Leviticus] [25]:[23] Now the land must not be sold forever; for the land is mine. Because you are strangers, and sojourners before me,
[Leviticus] [25]:[24] therefore in all the land of your possession, you shall grant a right of redeeming the land.
[Leviticus] [25]:[25] And if thy brother who is with thee become poor, and sell any part of his possession; when he who is next of kin to him cometh, he shall redeem what his brother hath sold.
[Leviticus] [25]:[26] And if any person hath none to redeem, and his own hand hath attained, and found a sufficiency, he shall have the right of redemption.
[Leviticus] [25]:[27] And he shall compute the years of his sale, and pay back what is still due to the man to whom he sold it. So shall he return to his possession.
[Leviticus] [25]:[28] But if his hand cannot attain a sufficiency to repay him, the sale shall be good to him who purchased, until the last sixth year of the jubilee, and in the jubilee, he shall quit, and the seller shall return to his possession.
[Leviticus] [25]:[29] And if any person sell a dwelling house in a walled city, he shall have the right of redeeming it till the term expire. The term for redemption shall be a year.
[Leviticus] [25]:[30] And if it be not redeemed till the whole year expire, the house which is in a walled city, shall be confirmed for ever to the purchaser. And he shall not go out in the jubilee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[31] But the houses which are in villages, unsurrounded with walls, shall be accounted as the fields in the country, and shall be always subject to redemption, and go out in the jubilee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[32] And the cities of the Levites, the houses of the cities of their possession, shall always be redeemable by the Levites;
[Leviticus] [25]:[33] and whoever among the Levites shall redeem, the intermediate purchase of the houses of the city of their possession shall terminate in the jubilee. For the houses of the cities of the Levites are their possession among the children of Israel.
[Leviticus] [25]:[34] And the fields set apart for their cities shall not be sold; for this is their perpetual possession.
[Leviticus] [25]:[35] And if thy brother who is with thee become poor and distressed in his circumstances when near thee, thou shalt help him, as a proselyte or a sojourner, and thy brother shall live with thee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[36] Thou shalt not take from him usury or increase, but thou shalt fear thy God, I the Lord; and thy brother shall live with thee.
[Leviticus] [25]:[37] Thou shalt not lend him thy money on usury, nor give him thy victuals for an increase.
[Leviticus] [25]:[38] I am the Lord your God who brought you out of the land of Egypt, to give you the land of Chanaan, and to be your God.
[Leviticus] [25]:[39] And if thy brother who dwelleth by thee be humbled, and sold to thee, he shall not serve thee as a slave.
[Leviticus] [25]:[40] He shall be with thee as a hireling, or a sojourner. Until the year of the release he shall work for thee,
[Leviticus] [25]:[41] and in the year of the release he shall go out, and his children with him, and shall go to his own family—to his paternal possession he shall haste away.
[Leviticus] [25]:[42] Because they are my servants, whom I brought out of the land of Egypt; he must not be sold as a slave.
[Leviticus] [25]:[43] Thou shall not over burthen him with toil, but fear the Lord thy God.
[Leviticus] [25]:[44] The male and female slaves, whom thou mayst have, must be of the nations around thee. Of them you may purchase a male or a female slave;
[Leviticus] [25]:[45] or of the children of strangers who sojourn among you—of them you may purchase and of their relations who may be born in your land.
[Leviticus] [25]:[46] Let them be your property, and divide them among your children after you, and they shall be your slaves for life: but of your brethren, the children of Israel, one shall not rule over another with rigour.
[Leviticus] [25]:[47] And if a proselyte, or a sojourner who is with thee, prosper, and thy brother, being poor, be sold to the proselyte, or to the stranger who is with thee, or to the descendant of the proselyte;
[Leviticus] [25]:[48] after he is sold he shall have a right to be redeemed. One of his brethren may redeem him,
[Leviticus] [25]:[49] his uncle, or his uncle's son, may redeem him, or any of his blood relations, of the same tribe, may redeem him; and if he be able he may redeem himself.
[Leviticus] [25]:[50] In which case he shall settle with him who purchased him, from the year he sold himself, to the year of the release, and the price of his purchase shall be as the yearly wages of a hired servant for the years he is to be with him.
[Leviticus] [25]:[51] And if any one hath many years to serve, for them he shall pay the rates of redemption, on account of the money paid for him;
[Leviticus] [25]:[52] and if but few years remain to the year of release, he shall settle with him according to those years, and pay the rates of his redemption.
[Leviticus] [25]:[53] As a servant hired from year to year, he shall be with him; and thou shalt not suffer him to be oppressed with labour in thy sight.
[Leviticus] [25]:[54] If he be not redeemed in any of these ways, in the year of the release he shall go out, and his children with him;
[Leviticus] [25]:[55] for the children of Israel are my domestics—they are my servants whom I brought out of Egypt.
[Leviticus] [26]:[1] I am the Lord your God; you shall not make for yourselves carved or graven images, nor set up a pillar for yourselves, nor place a watch stone in your land to worship it. I am the Lord your God,
[Leviticus] [26]:[2] you shall keep my sabbaths, and reverence my holy things. I am the Lord;
[Leviticus] [26]:[3] if you walk in my statutes, and keep my commandments, and do them,
[Leviticus] [26]:[4] I will give you rain in its season: and the land shall yield its productions;
[Leviticus] [26]:[5] and the trees of the field shall yield their fruit; and the thresher shall overtake the vintager; and the vintager shall overtake the sower; and you shall eat your bread in plenty; and dwell in your land securely, and war shall not pass through your land.
[Leviticus] [26]:[6] I will indeed give you peace in your land; and you shall lie down to rest; and there shall be none to make you afraid. I will destroy the wild beasts out of your land;
[Leviticus] [26]:[7] and you shall pursue your enemies, and they shall fall before you with destruction.
[Leviticus] [26]:[8] Even five of you shall pursue an hundred; and a hundred of you shall pursue myriads, and your enemies shall fall before you by the sword.
[Leviticus] [26]:[9] And I will watch over you, and increase you, and multiply you, and establish my covenant with you.
[Leviticus] [26]:[10] And you shall eat the last year's crops, and the crops of the year before, and remove old stores out of the way of new.
[Leviticus] [26]:[11] And I will fix my tabernacle among you, and my soul will not abhor you.
[Leviticus] [26]:[12] And I will walk about among you, and be your God, and you shall be my people.
[Leviticus] [26]:[13] I am the Lord your God, who brought you out of the land of Egypt. When you were slaves, I broke the bond of your yoke, and led you out in confidence:
[Leviticus] [26]:[14] Now if you will not hearken to me, nor perform these my commandments,
[Leviticus] [26]:[15] but disobey them, and your souls abhor my judgments, so as not to perform all my commandments, so as to break my covenant,
[Leviticus] [26]:[16] I will deal with you in this manner—I will send against you distress, and the scab, and the gangrene to eat out your eyes; and wasting consumption; and you shall sow your seed in vain; for your enemies shall eat it:
[Leviticus] [26]:[17] and I will set my face against you; and you shall fall before your enemies; and they who hate you shall pursue you; and you shall flee when there is none pursuing you.
[Leviticus] [26]:[18] And if for all this you will not hearken to me, I will proceed to chastise you seven fold for your offences,
[Leviticus] [26]:[19] and break the haughtiness of your pride.—I will make the heaven for you as iron, and your land as brass;
[Leviticus] [26]:[20] and your strength shall be spent in vain. The earth shall not yield you its seed, nor shall the trees of the field yield you their fruit.
[Leviticus] [26]:[21] And after all these things, if you walk adversely and will not hearken to me, I will bring seven fold plagues upon you according to your sins—
[Leviticus] [26]:[22] I will send against you the wild beasts of the earth, and they shall devour you, and destroy your cattle; and I will make you few in number, and your high ways shall be desolate.
[Leviticus] [26]:[23] And if by these things you be not reformed, but walk adversely before me,
[Leviticus] [26]:[24] I also will walk with you in adverse wrath, and smite you seven fold for your sins—
[Leviticus] [26]:[25] I will bring a sword against you to avenge the cause of the covenant; and when you flee to your cities, I will send pestilence among you, and you shall be delivered into the hands of your enemies.
[Leviticus] [26]:[26] In your affliction for want of bread, ten women shall bake your bread in one oven and dole out your bread by weight, and you shall eat, but shall not be satisfied.
[Leviticus] [26]:[27] And if for these things you will not hearken to me, but walk adversely before me,
[Leviticus] [26]:[28] I also will walk with you in adverse ire, and chastise you seven fold, according to your sins.
[Leviticus] [26]:[29] And you shall eat the flesh of your sons; and the flesh of your daughters also you shall eat:
[Leviticus] [26]:[30] and I will lay waste your pillars, and utterly destroy your carved images of wood, and cast your mangled limbs among the mangled limbs of your idols, and my soul will abhor you.
[Leviticus] [26]:[31] And I will lay waste your cities, and make your sanctuaries a desolation, and no longer be regaled with the smell of your sacrifices.
[Leviticus] [26]:[32] I will also make your land a desolation, and your enemies who dwell therein shall be astonished at it.
[Leviticus] [26]:[33] And I will scatter you among the nations, and a sword shall overtake and consume you. And your land shall be a waste, and your cities a desolation.
[Leviticus] [26]:[34] And the land shall then enjoy its sabbaths all the time of its desolation. When you shall be in the land of your enemies, the land shall then rest; and the land shall keep those sabbaths,
[Leviticus] [26]:[35] which it did not keep by your sabbaths when you dwelt therein.
[Leviticus] [26]:[36] And upon those of you who are left, I will bring a slavery, which shall reach their very hearts, in the land of their enemies. The sound of a leaf driven by the wind will scare them; and they shall flee like those who flee from a battle, and shall fall when none pursueth.
[Leviticus] [26]:[37] And brother shall overlook brother as in a battle, though none attacketh; and you shall not be able to withstand your enemies.
[Leviticus] [26]:[38] And you shall perish among the nations, and the land of your enemies shall devour you.
[Leviticus] [26]:[39] And those of you who are left shall pine away for their sins, and for the sins of their fathers—In the land of their enemies they shall pine away,
[Leviticus] [26]:[40] and confess their sins and the sins of their fathers, that they have transgressed, and neglected me, and that they have walked perversely before me,
[Leviticus] [26]:[41] and I have walked with them in vengeful ire. When I am destroying them in the land of their enemies, then will their uncircumcised heart be ashamed, and then will they bear their sins with humble submission;
[Leviticus] [26]:[42] whereupon I will call to remembrance the covenant with Jacob, and the covenant with Isaak, and I will call to remembrance the covenant with Abraham, and remember the land.
[Leviticus] [26]:[43] When the land shall be forsaken by them, and when the land shall have enjoyed its sabbaths by being made a waste on their account, and they have borne their iniquities because they neglected my judgments, and in their hearts abhorred my statutes,
[Leviticus] [26]:[44] (as I did not, when they were in their enemies' country, wholly overlook them, nor abhor them so as to destroy them utterly, for breaking my covenant which I made with them, for I am the Lord their God)
[Leviticus] [26]:[45] then will I remember the covenant with them—that old covenant when I brought them out of the land of Egypt—out of the house of bondage, to be their God. I am the Lord;
[Leviticus] [26]:[46] These are my judgments, and these are my statutes, and this is the law which the Lord hath given between himself and the children of Israel, at mount Sina, by the ministration of Moses.
[Leviticus] [27]:[1] Again the Lord spoke to Moses, saying,
[Leviticus] [27]:[2] Speak to the children of Israel, and thou shalt say to them, Whoever shalt vow a vow as the price of his life to the Lord, the price shall be—
[Leviticus] [27]:[3] of a male from twenty to sixty years of age, the price shall be fifty didrachms of silver, sanctuary weight;
[Leviticus] [27]:[4] and of a female, the valuation shall be thirty didrachms.
[Leviticus] [27]:[5] And if it be of one from five to twenty years of age, the price of a male shall be twenty didrachms, and of a female ten didrachms.
[Leviticus] [27]:[6] And if it be of one from a month to five years old, the price of a male shall be five didrachms, and of a female three didrachms of silver.
[Leviticus] [27]:[7] And if it be of one from sixty years old and upwards; if a male the price shall be fifteen didrachms of silver, and if a female, ten didrachms.
[Leviticus] [27]:[8] And if any be too poor for this valuation, he shall present himself before the priest and the priest shall set a value on him—according to the ability of him who made the vow, the priest shall value him.
[Leviticus] [27]:[9] But if the gift to the Lord be from the cattle which may be offered, whoever shall give any of these to the Lord, it shall be sacred.
[Leviticus] [27]:[10] He shall not change it, a good for a bad; nor a bad for a good: and if he actually change this beast for another, both it and that given in exchange shall be sacred.
[Leviticus] [27]:[11] But if it be any kind of unclean beast of which a gift cannot be made to the Lord, he shall present the beast before the priest;
[Leviticus] [27]:[12] and the priest shall value it at a medium between a good and a bad one; and according as the priest shall value it, so it shall stand;
[Leviticus] [27]:[13] and if the person chuse to redeem it, he shall add a fifth to the valuation.
[Leviticus] [27]:[14] And if a man make a dedication of his house to the Lord, the priest shall value it at a medium between a good and a bad house. And according as the priest shall value it so it shall stand;
[Leviticus] [27]:[15] and if he who dedicated it chuse to redeem it, he shall add thereto a fifth of the money at which it was rated, and it shall be his.
[Leviticus] [27]:[16] And if it be a part of the land of his possession, which a man shall dedicate to the Lord, the valuation shall be according to the seed thereof, fifty didrachms of silver for a core of barley—
[Leviticus] [27]:[17] If he dedicate it at the jubilee; at this valuation it shall stand.
[Leviticus] [27]:[18] But if he dedicate his field after the jubilee; the priest shall compute for him the money on the years which remain to the jubilee, and make a deduction from the full valuation of it.
[Leviticus] [27]:[19] And if the man who made the dedication chuse to redeem the field, he shall add one fifth of the money to the valuation of it, and it shall be his.
[Leviticus] [27]:[20] But if he decline to redeem the field, and selleth the field to another man, he shall never afterwards redeem it;
[Leviticus] [27]:[21] but at the going out of the jubilee the field shall be consecrated to the Lord, and he shall have possession of it as land set apart for the priest.
[Leviticus] [27]:[22] And if a man make a dedication to the Lord of a field which he hath purchased, and which is not a part of his inheritance,
[Leviticus] [27]:[23] though the priest settle with him the amount of the price from the year of the jubilee, and he pay the price at which it was rated on the day he made the dedication to the Lord;
[Leviticus] [27]:[24] yet in the year of the jubilee the field shall be restored to the man of whom he purchased it, and whose field of possession it was.
[Leviticus] [27]:[25] Now every valuation shall be by holy weight, twenty oboli making a didrachm.
[Leviticus] [27]:[26] And with regard to every firstling if it be among the cattle, it shall be for the Lord, and none shall dedicate it. Whether it be of the herds or flocks it is the Lord's.
[Leviticus] [27]:[27] And if it be of the four footed beasts which are unclean, he must redeem it according to the valuation, and add thereto a fifth, and it shall be his. And if he will not redeem it, it shall be sold for its value.
[Leviticus] [27]:[28] But with respect to every Anathema which a man may devote to the Lord from all that he hath, whether from man or beast, or from field of his possession, it shall not be sold nor redeemed. Every anathema shall be most sacredly devoted to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [27]:[29] And every thing which hath been devoted as an anathema from among men, shall not be redeemable, but by death.
[Leviticus] [27]:[30] The whole tenth of the land, both of the produce of the land, and of the fruit of the trees, is for the Lord. It is dedicated to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [27]:[31] And if any man chuse to redeem his tenth by purchase, he shall add to the valuation a fifth, and it shall be his.
[Leviticus] [27]:[32] Now the whole tenth of herds and flocks—even every one which in numbering shall come under the rod, that tenth shall be dedicated to the Lord.
[Leviticus] [27]:[33] Thou shalt not change a good for a bad, nor a bad for a good; and if thou shalt actually make an exchange, that given in exchange shall also be consecrated, and shall not be redeemed.
[Leviticus] [27]:[34] These are the commands which the Lord gave in charge to Moses for the children of Israel at mount Sina.
[Ruth] [1]:[1] Now when the Judges ruled, there was a famine in the land, and a man went from Bethlehem Juda to sojourn in the country of Moab, he and his wife and his two sons.
[Ruth] [1]:[2] The man's name was Elimelech, and his wife's name was Noemin, and the names of his two sons, Maalon and Chelaion. They were Ephrathites of Bethlehem Juda.
[Ruth] [1]:[3] They went to the country of Moab, and while they were there, Elimelech, the husband of Noemin, died, and she was left with her two sons.
[Ruth] [1]:[4] And they took for themselves wives of the daughters of Moab, the name of the one was Orpha, and the name of the other Ruth.
[Ruth] [1]:[5] And when they had dwelt there about ten years, both Maalon and Chelaion died, so that the woman was bereaved of her husband, and her two sons.
[Ruth] [1]:[6] Whereupon she arose with her two daughters in law, and set out on her return from the country of Moab; for she had heard, in the country of Moab, that the Lord had visited his people in giving them bread.
[Ruth] [1]:[7] So she set out from the place where she was with her two daughters in law.
[Ruth] [1]:[8] And when they had travelled some part of the way on their return to the land of Juda, Noemin said to her two daughters in law, Go, I pray you, return each to her mother's house.
[Ruth] [1]:[9] May the Lord deal kindly with you, as you have done with the dead, and with me. The Lord grant, that you may find rest, each in the house of her husband.
[Ruth] [1]:[10] So she kissed them, and they wept aloud, and said to her, We will go back with thee to thy people.
[Ruth] [1]:[11] But Noemin said, Return, I pray you, my daughters! for why should you go with me? Have I yet any more sons in my womb to be husbands for you?
[Ruth] [1]:[12] Return, I pray you, my daughters! For I am too old to have a husband—for should I say, I am in condition to marry, and should I bear sons, would you wait for them till they grow up;
[Ruth] [1]:[13] or should you be restrained by them from having husbands? No, my daughters. Indeed it grieveth me much for your sakes, that the hand of the Lord hath gone forth against me.
[Ruth] [1]:[14] And when they had raised their voice, and wept again, Orpha kissed her mother in law, and returned to her people; but Ruth followed her.
[Ruth] [1]:[15] Then Noemin said to Ruth; Behold thy sister in law is gone back to her people, and to her gods, return thou also, I pray thee, after thy sister in law.
[Ruth] [1]:[16] Whereupon Ruth said, Force me not to leave thee, nor to return from following thee; for whither soever thou goest, I will go and wherever thou lodgest, I will lodge. Thy people shall be my people; and thy God, my God; and wherever thou diest, I will die, and there will I be buried.
[Ruth] [1]:[17] The Lord do so to me and more also, if any thing but death shall part me and thee.
[Ruth] [1]:[18] And when Noemin saw that she was steadfastly determined to go with her, she refrained from speaking to her any more.
[Ruth] [1]:[19] So they both proceeded on till they came to Bethlehem. And when they arrived at Bethlehem, all the city was moved about them, and they said, Is this Noemin?
[Ruth] [1]:[20] But she said to them, Call me not, I pray you, Noemin. Call me, Bitter: for the Almighty hath dealt very bitterly with me.
[Ruth] [1]:[21] I went out full but the Lord hath brought me back empty. Why therefore do you call me Noemin, seeing the Lord hath brought me low, and the Almighty hath afflicted me?
[Ruth] [1]:[22] Thus Noemin returned, with Ruth the Moabitess, her daughter in law; and having returned from the country of Moab, they came to Bethlehem, at the beginning of the barley harvest.
[Ruth] [2]:[1] Now Noemin had a kinsman of her husband's; a mighty man of wealth, of the family of Elimelech, and his name was Boaz.
[Ruth] [2]:[2] And Ruth the Moabitess said to Noemin, Let me go, I pray thee, into the field, and glean among the stubble after him in whose eyes I shall find favour. And she said to her, Go, my daughter.
[Ruth] [2]:[3] So she went, and coming into a field, gleaned after the reapers. Now she happened to light upon the part of the field which belonged to Boaz, who was of the kindred of Elimelech.
[Ruth] [2]:[4] And behold Boaz came from Bethlehem, and said to the reapers, The Lord be with you! And they said to him, The Lord bless thee.
[Ruth] [2]:[5] Then Boaz said to his servant, who was set over the reapers, Whose damsel is that?
[Ruth] [2]:[6] And the servant who was over the reapers said in reply, It is the Moabitess girl, who came with Noemin from the country of Moab.
[Ruth] [2]:[7] She said, Let me, I pray thee, glean, and gather among the sheaves after the reapers. So she came, and hath been on her feet from early in the morning, and is continuing on till evening. She hath not rested a moment in the field.
[Ruth] [2]:[8] Then Boaz said to Ruth, Hearest thou not, my daughter? Thou must not go to glean in any other field, nor must thou go hence, but abide here fast by my maids,
[Ruth] [2]:[9] let thy eyes be on the field where they reap and go after them. Behold I have given a charge to my servants not to touch thee, and when thou art thirsty, go to the vessels, and drink of what the servants have drawn.
[Ruth] [2]:[10] Upon which she fell on her face, and made an obeisance to the ground, and said to him, How have I found favour in thy sight, that thou shouldst take notice of me, seeing I am a stranger?
[Ruth] [2]:[11] And Boaz in reply said to her, I have been fully informed of all that thou hast done to thy mother in law after the death of thy husband; and how thou hast left thy father, and thy mother, and the land of thy nativity, to come to a people whom thou knewest not heretofore.
[Ruth] [2]:[12] The Lord recompense thy work, and may a full reward be given thee by the Lord God of Israel, to whom thou art come, to put thy trust under his wings.
[Ruth] [2]:[13] And she said, Let me find favour in thy sight, my Lord, for thou hast comforted me. Now because thou hast spoken affectionately to thy servant, behold let me therefore be like one of thy maid servants.
[Ruth] [2]:[14] Then Boaz said to her, It is now meal time, draw near, and eat of the bread, and dip thy morsel in the vinegar. So Ruth sat down by the side of the reapers. And Boaz helped her to a large piece of barley bread, so that she eat, and was satisfied, and had some left.
[Ruth] [2]:[15] And when she arose to glean, Boaz gave a charge to his servants, saying, Let her glean among the sheaves, and put her not to the blush.
[Ruth] [2]:[16] And when you carry out, carry out for her. Leave also for her some out of the bundles, and let her eat, and glean, and rebuke her not.
[Ruth] [2]:[17] So she gleaned in the field till evening, and beat out what she had gathered, and it was about an epha of barley. And she took it up, and went to the city.
[Ruth] [2]:[18] And when her mother in law saw what she had gathered, and Ruth had taken out, and given her some of the bread that was left after she had been satisfied,
[Ruth] [2]:[19] her mother in law said to her, Where hast thou gleaned to day? And where hast thou been at work? Blessed be he who hath taken notice of thee. And when Ruth told her mother in law where she had been at work, and said, The man's name is Boaz, with whom I worked to day.
[Ruth] [2]:[20] Noemin said to her daughter in law, Blessed is he of the Lord, because he hath not dropped his kindness to the living, nor to the dead. Then Noemin said to her, The man is near of kin to us. He is one of our nearest relations.
[Ruth] [2]:[21] And Ruth said to her mother in law, Indeed he also said to me, Thou must keep close by my maids till they finish my whole harvest.
[Ruth] [2]:[22] And Noemin said to her daughter in law Ruth, it is good my daughter that thou hast gone out with his maids, therefore let them not meet thee in another field.
[Ruth] [2]:[23] So Ruth kept fast by the maids of Boaz, to glean till they finished the barley and wheat harvest.
[Ruth] [3]:[1] Then she abode at home with her mother in law. And Noemin her mother in law said to her, My daughter, shall I not seek a rest for thee, that it may be well with thee?
[Ruth] [3]:[2] Now is not Boaz our relation, with whose girls thou hast been? Behold he is winnowing barley in the threshing floor.
[Ruth] [3]:[3] This very night therefore thou shalt wash, and anoint thyself, and put on thy raiment, and go up to the threshing floor. But make not thyself known to the man till he hath done eating and drinking.
[Ruth] [3]:[4] And when he goeth to rest, mark the place where he lieth down, and go and uncover his feet, and lie down, and he will tell thee what thou shalt do.
[Ruth] [3]:[5] And Ruth said to her, All that thou hast ordered me I will do.
[Ruth] [3]:[6] So she went up to the threshing floor, and did all that her mother in law commanded her.
[Ruth] [3]:[7] And when Boaz had eaten, and drunk, and his heart was cheered, he went and lay down at the side of a heap of corn. Then she went in softly and uncovered his feet.
[Ruth] [3]:[8] And in the middle of the night the man was astonished, and troubled, for lo! a woman was lying at his feet.
[Ruth] [3]:[9] And he said, Who art thou? And she said, I am Ruth thy handmaid; therefore spread thy skirt over thy handmaid, for thou art near a kinsman.
[Ruth] [3]:[10] And Boaz said, Blessed art thou of the Lord God, my daughter; for thou hast shewn more kindness in this last act than in the former, in not going after young men either poor or rich.
[Ruth] [3]:[11] Now therefore, my daughter, be not afraid, I will do for thee all thou hast said; for all the tribe of my people know that thou art a virtuous woman.
[Ruth] [3]:[12] Now indeed it is true that I am a near relation; but there is a relation nearer of kin than I.
[Ruth] [3]:[13] Tarry this night, and it shall be, in the morning, that if he perform to thee the part of a kinsman, well: let him do so. But if he decline to perform to thee the part of a kinsman, I myself will do the kinsman's part for thee, as the Lord liveth. Go to sleep till the morning.
[Ruth] [3]:[14] So she lay at his feet till the morning, and arose before one could know another. And Boaz said, Let it not be known that a woman came to the threshing floor.
[Ruth] [3]:[15] Then he said to her, Bring the sash which is girt about thee. So she held it, and he measured six measures of barley, and laid it on her, and went to the city.
[Ruth] [3]:[16] And Ruth went to her mother in law. And her mother in law said to her, My daughter! And when Ruth had told her all that the man had done to her;
[Ruth] [3]:[17] and said to her, He gave me also these six measures of barley; for said he to me, Thou must not go empty to thy mother in law;
[Ruth] [3]:[18] she said, Sit still my daughter till thou know the issue of this matter; for the man will not rest till the affair is now brought to a conclusion.
[Ruth] [4]:[1] And Boaz went up to the gate and sat down there. And behold when the kinsman of whom Boaz had spoken was passing by, Boaz said to him, Turn aside and sit down here thou hidden man.
[Ruth] [4]:[2] So he turned aside, and sat down. Then Boaz took ten men of the elders of the city, and said, Sit down here.
[Ruth] [4]:[3] And when they had taken their seats, Boaz said to the kinsman, With respect to that portion of the field which belonged to our brother Elimelech, and which is given to Noemin, who is returned from the country of Moab,
[Ruth] [4]:[4] I have promised to open thine ear, saying, Purchase it in the presence of these men who are sitting here, even in the presence of the elders of my people. If thou wilt do the kinsman's part, do it; but if thou wilt not perform the office of a kinsman, tell me, that I may know for there is none besides thee to perform the office of a kinsman, except myself, who am next after thee. And he said, I will do the kinsman's part.
[Ruth] [4]:[5] Then Boaz said, On the day thou purchasest the field of Noemin, thou must purchase it with Ruth, the Moabitess, the wife of the dead, to raise up the name of the dead on his inheritance.
[Ruth] [4]:[6] Thereupon the relation said, I cannot take upon me the office of a kinsman, lest I mar my own inheritance. Take thou upon thyself that office of a kinsman which is my right; for I cannot perform it.
[Ruth] [4]:[7] Now it was the custom heretofore in Israel, in regard to the transfer of the right of consanguinity, in order to give it validity, that the man loosed his sandal, and gave it to his neighbour, who took upon him his right of consanguinity, and this was the evidence in Israel.
[Ruth] [4]:[8] So when the relation said to Boaz, Purchase thou for thyself, what by the law of consanguinity it was my right to purchase, he loosed his sandal, and gave it to him.
[Ruth] [4]:[9] Thereupon Boaz said to the elders, and to all the people, You are this day witnesses that I have purchased all that belonged to Elimelech, and all that belonged to Chelaion and Maalon, at the hand of Noemin;
[Ruth] [4]:[10] and that I have purchased Ruth, the Moabitess, the wife of Maalon, to be a wife to myself, to raise up the name of the deceased on his inheritance; that the name of the deceased may not be blotted out from among his brethren, and from the tribe of his people. You are this day witnesses.
[Ruth] [4]:[11] And all the people who were in the gate said, We are witnesses. Then the elders said, The Lord make this thy wife who is coming to thy house, like Rachel and like Leia, who both built up the house of Israel, and laid the foundation of power in Ephratha, that there may be a name in Bethlehem:
[Ruth] [4]:[12] And may thy house be like the house of Phares, whom Thamar bore to Juda, from the seed which the Lord will give thee from this young woman.
[Ruth] [4]:[13] So Boaz took Ruth, and she became his wife, and he went in unto her, and the Lord gave her conception, and she bore a son.
[Ruth] [4]:[14] Whereupon the women said to Noemin, Blessed be the Lord who hath not left thee this day without a kinsman to make thy name famous in Israel.
[Ruth] [4]:[15] He shall be to thee a restorer of life, and a nourisher of thine old age. For thy daughter in law, who loved thee, hath brought forth a son. She is better to thee than seven sons.
[Ruth] [4]:[16] And Noemin took the child, and laid it in her bosom, and became its nurse,
[Ruth] [4]:[17] and the neighbours gave it a name, saying, A son is born to Noemin, and called its name Obed. He was the father of Jessai, the father of David.
[Ruth] [4]:[18] Now this is the genealogy of Phares. Phares begot Esrom,
[Ruth] [4]:[19] and Esrom begot Aram; and Aram begot Aminadab;
[Ruth] [4]:[20] and Aminadab, Naasson; and Naasson, Salmon;
[Ruth] [4]:[21] and Salmon, Boaz; and Boaz, Obed;
[Ruth] [4]:[22] and Obed, Jessai; and Jessai, David.
[Esther] [1]:[1] After these things, in the days of Artaxerxes the same who reigned from India, over a hundred and twenty seven provinces,
[Esther] [1]:[2] when Artaxerxes was settled on his throne in the city Susoi,
[Esther] [1]:[3] in the third year of his reign, he made an entertainment for his friends, both for other nations, and for the nobles of the Persians and Medes, and for the governors.
[Esther] [1]:[4] And having after this displayed to them the riches of his kingdom, and the transporting glory of his riches, for a hundred and eighty days,
[Esther] [1]:[5] when the days of his marriage were fulfilled, the king made an entertainment for all the nations who were in the city, for six days, in the court of the royal palace,
[Esther] [1]:[6] which was adorned with painted hangings of cotton, extended with cords of cotton, and purple yarn, to capitals of gold and silver, on pillars of parian marble and alabaster. The couches were of gold and silver, on a pavement of smaragdine, pinine, and parian stone and their covers were of gause, painted with a variety of flowers; and roses were scattered all around.
[Esther] [1]:[7] The goblets of gold and silver, with a small carbuncled cup to each, amounted in value to thirty thousand talents. There was wine in abundance and of a sweet quality, such as the king himself drank.
[Esther] [1]:[8] Now this entertainment was not according to an established custom; but the king would have it so, and he commanded the stewards to comply with his pleasure, and that of the guests.
[Esther] [1]:[9] Astin the queen also made an entertainment for the women in the royal palace, where king Artaxerxes was.
[Esther] [1]:[10] And on the seventh day, the king, in high good humour, ordered Anian and Basan and Tharra and Barazi and Zatholtha and Abataza and Tharaba, the seven chamberlains, who waited on king Artaxerxes,
[Esther] [1]:[11] to bring the queen to him to proclaim her queen, and crown her with a diadem, and shew her to the chiefs, and her beauty to the nations; for she was beautiful.
[Esther] [1]:[12] But queen Astin hearkened not to him to come with the chamberlain, at which the king was greatly offended and inflamed with anger
[Esther] [1]:[13] and he said to his friends, Astin hath spoken so and so; do you therefore execute law and judgment, touching this matter.
[Esther] [1]:[14] Thereupon there came before him, Arkesaius and Sarsathaius and Malisear the chiefs of the Persians and Medes, who were near the king, and had the first seats next him;
[Esther] [1]:[15] and told him according to the laws what ought to be done to queen Astin, because she had not done what the king had commanded by the chamberlains.
[Esther] [1]:[16] And Muchaius said to the king and to the chiefs, Queen Astin hath wronged, not the king only, but also all the king's chiefs and leaders;
[Esther] [1]:[17] for he hath told them the queen's conduct and how she hath contradicted the king. Therefore as she hath contradicted the king Artaxerxes,
[Esther] [1]:[18] so will the rest of the ladies now, the wives of the Persian and Medean princes, upon hearing what she hath said to the king, presume in like manner to dishonour their husbands.
[Esther] [1]:[19] If therefore it seemeth good to the king, let him pass a royal decree and let it be written according to the laws of the Medes and Persians, and let him not alter it nor suffer the queen any more to approach him; but let the king give her royalty to another woman, better than her.
[Esther] [1]:[20] And let this edict of the king be proclaimed, which if he cause to be done throughout his kingdom, then will all the women both rich and poor, pay respect to their husbands.
[Esther] [1]:[21] And the speech pleased the king and the princes; and the king did as Muchaius had spoken,
[Esther] [1]:[22] and sent the decree throughout the whole kingdom, into every province in the language and writing thereof, that men might be feared by their own families.
[Esther] [2]:[1] After this when the king's wrath was appeased and he no more remembered Astin, nor made any mention of what she had spoken, and how he had condemned her;
[Esther] [2]:[2] the king's servants said, Let there be sought for the king a virgin of unblemished chastity, and beautiful;
[Esther] [2]:[3] and let the king appoint officers in all the provinces of his kingdom, and let them select and send to the city Susoi to the house of the women, virgins remarkable for beauty; and let them be delivered to the king's chamberlain, the keeper of the women; and let the things necessary for purification be given them.
[Esther] [2]:[4] And let the woman who shall please the king be made queen in the room of Astin. And the proposal pleased the king, and he did so.
[Esther] [2]:[5] Now there was at the city of Susoi, a man, a Jew, whose name was Mordecai. He was the son of Jairus, the son of Semeias, the son of Kisaius, of the tribe of Benjamin.
[Esther] [2]:[6] He had been carried away captive from Jerusalem among them whom Nabuchodonosar king of Babylon had captivated.
[Esther] [2]:[7] And this man had a foster child, a daughter of Aminadab, his father's brother; and her name was Esther. On the decease of her parents he educated her to be a wife for himself.
[Esther] [2]:[8] But as the girl was beautiful, when the decree of the king was published, and many young women were brought to the city Susoi and put under the care of Gai, Esther also was brought to Gai the keeper of the women.
[Esther] [2]:[9] And the young woman pleased him and found favour in his sight, so that he hastened to give her the things for purification and the allotted portion; and seven waiting maids were assigned to her out of the king's house: and he treated her and her maids courteously in the house of the women.
[Esther] [2]:[10] Now Esther did not disclose her family, nor her country; for Mordecai had charged her not to tell.
[Esther] [2]:[11] But Mordecai walked every day before the court of the womens' house to see what would happen to Esther.
[Esther] [2]:[12] Now the time for every young woman to go to the king was when she had compleated twelve months; for thus were the days of their purification fulfilled. Six months they were anointed with oil of myrrh, and six months with aromatics and lotions used by women.
[Esther] [2]:[13] Then she goeth in to the king. The keeper is to deliver her to whomsoever the king ordereth to go with her from the womens' apartment to the king's house.
[Esther] [2]:[14] In the evening she goeth in, and in the morning goeth with all speed to the second house of the women, which is under the care of Gai the king's chamberlain, the keeper of the women, and no more cometh to the king unless called for by name.
[Esther] [2]:[15] So when the time was fulfilled for Esther the daughter of Aminadab, Mordecai's uncle, to go to the king, she neglected nothing which the chamberlain the keeper of the women ordered her; for Esther found favour with all who saw her.
[Esther] [2]:[16] And Esther went in to Artaxerxes the king, in the twelfth month which is the month Adar in the seventh year of his reign.
[Esther] [2]:[17] And the king was enamoured of Esther, and she found favour above all the virgins, so that he set the queen's crown on her head
[Esther] [2]:[18] and made an entertainment for all his friends and potentates for seven days, and to celebrate the marriage of Esther he made a release to those under his government.
[Esther] [2]:[19] Now Mordecai performed service in the court,
[Esther] [2]:[20] and Esther had not yet declared her family, for Mordecai having charged her in this manner to fear God and execute his commands as when she was with him, Esther did not alter her conduct.
[Esther] [2]:[21] And two of the king's chamberlains, who were captains of his life guard, being displeased because Mordecai was promoted, sought to kill king Artaxerxes.
[Esther] [2]:[22] But the matter being made known to Mordecai, he informed Esther and she disclosed the conspiracy to the king;
[Esther] [2]:[23] whereupon the king having examined the chamberlains, caused them to be hanged, and ordered a record to be made in the royal library with an encomium on Mordecai's fidelity.
[Esther] [3]:[1] After these things king Artaxerxes honoured Haman of Amadathus the Bougaian, and promoted him, and seated him first of all his friends,
[Esther] [3]:[2] so that all the court bowed down to him for so the king had commanded to be done. But Mordecai did not bow down to him;
[Esther] [3]:[3] whereupon they who were in the king's court said to him, Mordecai, Why trangressest thou the king's commands.
[Esther] [3]:[4] And when they spoke to him daily and he hearkened not to them, they told Haman, that Mordecai disobeyed the king's commands. Now Mordecai had told them that he was a Jew.
