[Daniel] [12]:[5] Then I Daniel looked, and lo! two others stood, one on this bank of the river and the other on the other bank of the river.
[Daniel] [12]:[6] And one said to the man clothed with the flowing robes who was over the water of the river, How long to the end of these wonderful things which thou hast spoken?
[Daniel] [12]:[7] And I heard the man clothed with the fine robes who was over the water of the river. He lifted up his right and his left hand to heaven and sware by him who liveth forever and ever that it will be until a time of times and the half of a time. When an end is put to the dispersion, they will know all these things.
[Daniel] [12]:[8] As for me though I heard I did not understand. So I said, Lord, What will be the issue of these things?
[Daniel] [12]:[9] And he said, Come Daniel. Because these words are shut up and sealed until the end of a time.
[Daniel] [12]:[10] Though many be chosen and made white and purified and sanctified, and transgressors transgress; yet no transgressor will understand but the prudent will understand.
[Daniel] [12]:[11] Now from the time of the change of the daily sacrifice, when the abomination of desolation shall be set up there will be a thousand two hundred and ninety days.
[Daniel] [12]:[12] Happy he who will wait and come to a thousand three hundred and thirty five days.
[Daniel] [12]:[13] But as for thee, come and go to rest, for still there will be days and hours to the final accomplishment. And thou shalt stand up for thy portion at the end of days.
[Nahum] [1]:[1] The sentence on Ninive. The book of the vision of Nahum, the Elkosite.
[Nahum] [1]:[2] God is zealous. The Lord is about to take vengeance: the Lord is about to take vengeance with wrath: the Lord is about to take vengeance on his adversaries, and to destroy utterly his enemies, himself.
[Nahum] [1]:[3] The Lord is slow to anger, though his power is great; will not the Lord then acquit the innocent? Is his way in destruction and whirlwind, when clouds are the dust of his feet?
[Nahum] [1]:[4] When he rebuketh the sea he maketh it dry, and drieth up all the rivers. Bashan and Carmel are diminished and the bloom of Libanus languished.
[Nahum] [1]:[5] At him the mountains quake and the hills tremble; at his presence the earth is startled—the world and all its inhabitants.
[Nahum] [1]:[6] At the presence of his wrath who can stand, or who can resist his fierce indignation? His wrath dissolveth governments, and by him the rocks are rent to pieces.
[Nahum] [1]:[7] The Lord is kind to them who wait for him in a day of distress: and he knoweth them who reverence him.
[Nahum] [1]:[8] When with a deluging march he maketh destruction; them who raise themselves up and are his enemies, darkness shall pursue.
[Nahum] [1]:[9] What are you devising against the Lord? He will make complete destruction, and not punish twice by distress for the same thing.
[Nahum] [1]:[10] Because one shall be utterly destroyed, and consumed like bindweed and dry stubble;
[Nahum] [1]:[11] shall a reasoning against the Lord proceed from thee; O wicked city, devising things in opposition?
[Nahum] [1]:[12] Thus saith the Lord, With respect to the ruler of many waters! they shall be so dispersed, that what thou hearest shall be heard no more.
[Nahum] [1]:[13] I will now break his sceptre from thee, and burst the bands asunder.
[Nahum] [1]:[14] Concerning thee the Lord will command, let none of thy name be sown any more. From the house of thy God I will root out the graven and molten images; "I will make it a sepulchre for thee."
[Nahum] [1]:[15] Because yonder on the mountain are the swift feet of one bringing good news—even of one announcing peace; O Juda, solemnize thy festivals; pay thy vows; for they shall no more proceed to pass through thee for conflict. It is completely laid waste, entirely removed.
[Nahum] [2]:[1] Into thy presence came up panting one who is delivered from affliction. Watch the way; strengthen thy loins; act manfully with all thy might,
[Nahum] [2]:[2] since the Lord hath averted contumely from Jacob, as he did reproach from Israel, since emptiers have emptied them out and destroyed their branches—
[Nahum] [2]:[3] the instruments of their tyranny from among men—their mighty men insulting with fire. In the day of his preparation the reins of their chariots, and the horsemen will be disordered in their ranks;
[Nahum] [2]:[4] and the chariots will justle together and be entangled with one another in the streets. The appearance of them is like flaming torches and like streamy lightning.
[Nahum] [2]:[5] Their grandees will recollect and flee by day; but they shall be weak in their march. While they shall be hasting to her walls, and preparing their out guards;
[Nahum] [2]:[6] the gates of the cities are opened, and the palaces are fallen.
[Nahum] [2]:[7] And the empress herself is exposed to view; even she herself is gone up, and her maids are led along cooing plaintively like doves, in their hearts.
[Nahum] [2]:[8] As for Ninive, her waters are like a standing pool. They who fled away made no halt, nor was there one who looked back.
[Nahum] [2]:[9] They plundered the silver; they plundered the gold; but of her furniture there was no end; every one is loaded with her precious utensils.
[Nahum] [2]:[10] What quaking and driving! what groans and heart breaking! what feebleness of knees and pangs in every loin! what blackness, like that of a pot, on the countenance of all!
[Nahum] [2]:[11] where is the habitation of the lions, and the feeding places of the young lions? Where had the old lions gone, that a young lion entered and there was none to scare him?
[Nahum] [2]:[12] The old lion had seized enough for his whelps, and had strangled for his lionesses, and had filled his hole with prey, and his den with ravin:
[Nahum] [2]:[13] behold I am against thee saith the Lord Almighty, and I will burn out thy multitude with smoke; and a sword shall devour thy lions: and I will cut off thy prey from the earth; and of thy deeds nothing more shall be heard.
[Nahum] [3]:[1] O bloody city, wholly perfidious and full of lies! shall not thy prey be handled?
[Nahum] [3]:[2] A sound of whips! and the sound of the rumbling of wheels, and of the pursuing horse! and bounding chariot!
[Nahum] [3]:[3] and of the cavalry advancing! and of the glittering sword and gleamy arms! and of the multitude of wounded! and of the hideous crash! Though there was no bound to her nations, they shall be weak of body
[Nahum] [3]:[4] by reason of great fornication. O fair harlot and bewitching mistress of sorceries, who sellest nations by thy whoredom, and peoples by thy sorceries!
[Nahum] [3]:[5] behold I am against thee, saith the Lord God Almighty, and I will expose thy skirts to view; and shew nations thy shame; and kingdoms, thy dishonour:
[Nahum] [3]:[6] and cast abomination on thee according to thine impurities; and make thee a publick example,
[Nahum] [3]:[7] so that every one who seeth thee shall go down from thee, and say, Wretched Ninive! who can bemoan her? Whence can I find comfort for her?
[Nahum] [3]:[8] Prepare a funeral cake! attune the lyre! prepare the funeral cake of Ammon! she dwelt among rivers; water was around her. The sea was her dominions, and water, her walls,
[Nahum] [3]:[9] and Ethiopia and Egypt, her strength; and the end of her flight was not stopped and the Lybians were her auxiliaries.
[Nahum] [3]:[10] Yet she is to go captive into banishment; and her infants shall be dashed to the ground at the head of all the streets; and for all her splendid treasures lots shall be cast; and all her grandees shall be bound with fetters.
[Nahum] [3]:[11] And as for thee thou shalt be made drunk and be despised, and shalt seek for thyself a resting place from enemies.
[Nahum] [3]:[12] All thy fortresses are like figs which have watchers; when shaken they will fall into the mouth of the eater.
[Nahum] [3]:[13] Behold thy people in thee are like women; the gates of thy land shall be opened wide to thine enemies; a fire shall utterly devour thy bars.
[Nahum] [3]:[14] Draw thee water for a siege, and fortify thy bulwarks. Go down into the clay, and let him be trampled with straw. Make it harder than any brick.
[Nahum] [3]:[15] There a fire shall devour thee; a sword shall cut thee off. Like a locust it will devour thee; and like a brouchus locust thou shalt be afflicted.
[Nahum] [3]:[16] Thou hast multiplied thy merchandise, above the stars of heaven; the brouchus came rushing on, and is flown.
[Nahum] [3]:[17] Thy merchandise is swept away like the attelebos locust—like the small locust mounted on a hedge, in a cold day: the sun broke out and they are swept away, and their place is not known. Alas for them!
[Nahum] [3]:[18] thy shepherds slumbered; an Assyrian king lulled to sleep thy mighty men: thy people fled to the mountains and there was none to rally them.
[Nahum] [3]:[19] There is no cure for thy bruise. Thy wound is festered. All that hear the news of thee will clap their hands at thee. For upon whom hath not thy wickedness come continually?
[Habakkuk] [1]:[1] The vision which Ambakum the prophet saw.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[2] How long, O Lord shall I cry, and thou not hearken? How long shall I being injured cry to thee, and thou not save?
[Habakkuk] [1]:[3] Why hast thou pointed out to me to behold labours and sorrows, misery and impiety? A suit is instituted against me and the judge receiveth bribes
[Habakkuk] [1]:[4] therefore law is disregarded and the cause is not brought to an issue. Because a wicked man oppresseth the just, therefore wrong judgment will be given.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[5] See ye despisers! and view intently and be amazed at wonderful things and vanish. For in your days I am doing a work which you will not believe though one tell you.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[6] For lo! I am raising up the Chaldeans that bitter and hasty nation, which marcheth over the breadth of the earth, to possess dwellings which belong not to them.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[7] He is terrible and illustrious; his judgment will be from himself, and from himself his prophesy will proceed.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[8] His horses can out leap leopards and are swifter than the lynxes of Arabia. When his horsemen have mounted, they will rush impetuously from afar, and will fly like an eagle, eager for prey.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[9] Destruction will come on the wicked who set themselves against him; and he will gather captives like the sand.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[10] He indeed will riot in kings; and petty princes will be his scorn. Of every fortress he will make a scoff and will raise a mount and take it.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[11] Then he will change his mind and depart and be pacified.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[12] This is the majesty which belongeth to my God. Art not thou from everlasting? Lord, my God, my Holy One! let us not die. Thou, Lord, hast appointed this for judgment: and formed it that its correction may work conviction in me.
[Habakkuk] [1]:[13] Thine eye is too pure to behold wicked deeds, or to look on the labours of sorrow. Why lookest thou upon despisers? Wilt thou be silent when the wicked swallow up the righteous?
[Habakkuk] [1]:[14] Wilt thou make these men like the fishes of the sea, and like those reptiles which have no leader?
[Habakkuk] [1]:[15] He hath drawn up destruction with a hook, and hath dragged out one with a dredge, and enclosed another with his sweep net. For this cause he will rejoice and his heart will be glad;
[Habakkuk] [1]:[16] therefore he will sacrifice to his net and burn incense to his dredge. Because by these he made his mess rich and his food delicious,
[Habakkuk] [1]:[17] shall he on the account of this cast his dredge, and not spare to slay nations continually?
[Habakkuk] [2]:[1] I will stand on my watch and go up upon a rock and look around, that I may see what he will say to me and what answer I shall receive to my expostulation.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[2] And the Lord answered and said, Write a vision: write it distinctly in a book that the reader may trace these things;
[Habakkuk] [2]:[3] for the vision is for a time yet to come. But it will spring up at last and will not be vain. Though he may tarry, wait for him; for he will assuredly come and will not fail.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[4] If any one draw back my soul hath no pleasure in him. But the just shall live by faith in me.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[5] Now this arrogant and contemptuous man—this man of boasts shall not exceed a certain bound. He enlarged his soul like Hades and like death was not satisfied. When he shall have gathered to him all the nations and received for himself all the peoples,
[Habakkuk] [2]:[6] will they not all take up a parable against him and a fable to tell of him, saying, Woe to him who is heaping up for himself things which do not belong to him? For how long? And making his yoke grievously heavy.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[7] For biters of him shall start up suddenly. And the plotters against thee shall be sober and thou shalt be plunder for them.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[8] Because thou hast plundered many nations, all the remaining peoples shall plunder thee for the blood of mankind, and for the impieties of a land and a city and of all them who inhabit it.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[9] Woe to him who coveteth wicked gain for his house, that he
[Habakkuk] [2]:[10] may raise his nest high to be out of the reach of evils. Thou hast contrived shame for thy house; thou hast provoked many
[Habakkuk] [2]:[11] peoples and thy soul hath sinned; for a stone out of a wall will cry out, and a Worm from a beam will proclaim these things.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[12] Woe to him who buildeth a city with blood and furbisheth up a city with iniquities!
[Habakkuk] [2]:[13] Are not these things from the Lord Almighty? When many nations have fainted by fire, and many peoples have been dispirited,
[Habakkuk] [2]:[14] that the earth may be filled with a knowledge of the glory of God. Like water it will cover them.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[15] Woe to him who maketh his neighbour drink a foamy intoxicating draught, making him drunk that he may inspect his caves.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[16] Because of thy glory drink thou also a full draught of dishonour, and be confounded at heart and shaken. The cup of the right hand of the Lord is come round to thee and dishonour is heaped upon thy glory.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[17] For the impiety at Libanus shall cover thee and the sufferings of wild beasts shall terrify thee, on the account of the blood of mankind, and for the wickedness of a land and a city and of all them who inhabit it.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[18] Of what avail is a graven thing, that they have engraved it? Did one cause a molten mass, a false fantasy, to be cast, because the caster had a confidence that by casting he would make dumb idols?
[Habakkuk] [2]:[19] Woe to him who saith to Wood, Awake; arise. And to stone, be thou exalted. The one is indeed a fantasy; and the other, a plating of gold and silver: there is no breath in it.
[Habakkuk] [2]:[20] But the Lord is in his holy temple. Let all the earth be awed at his presence.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[1] The Prayer of Ambacum the prophet; with an Ode.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[2] Lord, I have heard the report of thee and am terrified; I have considered thy works and am struck with amaze. In the midst of two living beings thou wilt be known; when the years draw nigh thou wilt be acknowledged; when the time is come thou wilt be pointed out: when my soul is troubled, in wrath remember mercy.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[3] God will come from Thaiman, even the Holy One from the thick shady mountains of Pharan.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[4] His glory obscured the heavens; and the earth was full of his praise. His splendor will be like light—beaming rays in his hands, which impressed a lasting love of his majesty.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[5] Before him Logos the word will march, and advance into the plain.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[6] He stood and the earth was shaken: he looked, and nations melted away: the mountains were violently convulsed, the everlasting hills melted.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[7] Instead of labours I beheld his marches of old. The dwellings of the Ethiopians shall be terrified! And the tents of the land of Madian!
[Habakkuk] [3]:[8] Wast thou, Lord, angry with the rivers? Or was thy wrath against the rivers? Or thine indignation against a sea, that thou shouldst mount thy horses? Thy riding is indeed salvation.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[9] Thou hast bent thy bow effectually against sceptres. The Lord saith, "The land of rivers shall be broken;
[Habakkuk] [3]:[10] peoples shall see and be in pangs." Thou art scattering the rolling waters. The deep roared. It swelled high.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[11] The sun was withdrawn: and the moon stood on its orbit. Thy bolts will glance for light, for a glare of the lightening of thine arms.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[12] With a threat thou canst diminish a land, and with wrath crush nations.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[13] Thou hast marched forth for the salvation of thy people, to save thine anointed One. Thou wilt pour death on the head of transgressors, thou hast raised chains up to the neck.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[14] Thou hast struck with consternation the heads of mighties, at this they will be appalled! They will slacken their reins and be like a poor man eating in secret,
[Habakkuk] [3]:[15] whilst thou art pressing into the sea thy horses ruffling a mighty water.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[16] I watched myself; and at the sound of the prayer of my lips my bowels trembled and a tremor seized my bones; and under me my whole frame quivered. I shall be at rest in the day of affliction, at the time of his coining to the people of my neighbourhood.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[17] Since the fig tree will not flourish, nor will there be fruit on the vines; the product of the olive will fail and the fields will supply no food—For want of food the flocks have failed, and there are no kine in the stalls;
[Habakkuk] [3]:[18] As for me, I will rejoice in the Lord; I shall have joy for God my saviour.
[Habakkuk] [3]:[19] The Lord God is my strength, and will guide my feet to the end. He maketh me walk in high places; that I may triumph with his song.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[1] I was little among my brethren, and the youngest of my father's family. I fed my father's flocks.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[2] My hands had made an organ; and my fingers had tuned a psaltery.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[3] But who will tell my Lord. My Lord himself heareth.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[4] He sent his messenger and took me from my father's flocks and anointed me with anointing oil.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[5] My brothers were comely and great; but the Lord did not delight in them.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[6] I went out to meet the Philistine, and he cursed me by his idols.
[Psalm 151] [1]:[7] But I drew his own sword and cut off his head, and took away reproach from the children of Israel.
